## Visual tests are allowed again (2026-09-12, #924)

The standing "NO VISUAL TESTS" clause that the #904-#923 tasks carried (do not
run `run_examples.sh`, `make test-examples`, a screenshot gate or an example in
a window; hand the visual check to the user) is LIFTED. From #924 on, a queue
task may run the visual example gates and examples itself to verify its own
claims — with explicit timeouts, one run at a time, and still never alongside a
live `watch-tests.sh` run. Every still-open task that carried the clause has it
replaced by a "VISUAL TESTS OK" note; the clause stays verbatim on the closed
tasks as the record of how they were verified.

## How SUMU reads this file

SUMU owns the task list between the `SUMU_QUEUE_START` and `SUMU_QUEUE_END`
markers below. **That block is rebuilt from scratch on every save** — notes,
docs and anything else worth keeping go ABOVE the start marker, like this
section.

### Who writes what

SUMU writes the RUNNING marker when it dispatches a task. The AGENT writes the
outcome. SUMU never writes an outcome on an agent's behalf.

| Marker | Meaning | Written by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[ ]` | Not started | you, or an agent filing a new task |
| `[queued]` | Picked to run next | SUMU |
| `[>claude]` `[>codex]` | Running now, naming the agent | SUMU |
| `[x]` | Done | the agent |
| `[failed]` | Tried, did not work. STOPS the queue | the agent |
| `[question]` | Needs an answer. STOPS the queue | the agent |
| `[postponed]` | Not done, not now. The queue moves on | the agent, or you |

A `[question]` puts the question in the line body after `QUESTION:`, never
inside the brackets — a `]` in there corrupts the marker.

Putting a task that has ALREADY been started back to `[ ]` is forbidden.
`[ ]` means "never started", so it erases the fact that a turn happened, its
findings with it. Use `[postponed]`, `[failed]` or `[question]`.

Write the outcome marker as the LAST thing in a turn, in the same commit as
the work.

### Task metadata

Directly after the `#id`, before the sentence, a task may carry one brace
group of `key:value` fields:

| Example | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `{difficulty:3}` | Difficulty 3 of 5 |
| `{difficulty:5 model:gpt-6-astra}` | Difficulty 5, pinned to one model |

It sits at the HEAD of the line on purpose: agents append their findings to
the END, so anything kept there gets buried between notes and eventually
dropped. Fields SUMU does not know are left alone, so this is where other
per-task data can go later.

`{d3}` and a bare `{3}` are also read, and normalise to `{difficulty:3}` the
next time SUMU rewrites the line. SUMU hides the group in its task editor —
it is metadata, not part of the sentence — and puts it back when you save.

### Difficulty selects the model

Level 5 means "this agent's strongest model", so one task picks the right
model whichever agent runs it. SUMU applies this on autoplay and on Start
task alike.

| Level | Codex | Claude |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 5 · Hardest | 6 Astra | Fable 5.1 |
| 4 · Hard | 5.6 Sol | Opus 5.0 |
| 3 · Moderate | 5.6 Terra | Opus 4.8 |
| 2 · Easy | 5.6 Luna | Sonnet |
| 1 · Trivial | 5.4 Mini | Haiku |

A `model:` field overrides the ladder for that one task. A task with no
difficulty runs on whatever model the agent is already set to.

- [x] #870 {difficulty:5} [gpu/lifetime] Add recording/submission lifetime tracking over #869, including PipelineId/BindGroupId and SubmissionId. Retain direct and indirect bind-group/view dependencies from recording through completion, release abandoned recordings, and prevent retired resources from acquiring new uses while existing work finishes safely. Expose nonblocking polling; distinguish native reference release from resource destruction and ordinary independent value copy. Integrate explicit shutdown/device-loss behavior with the manager destructor and existing field cleanup order without freeing callback-reachable state. Verify early retirement, old bind groups, abandoned recordings, repeated submissions and independent managers using deterministic and hardware checks. Normal polling never waits. Use watch-tests.sh and hardware runs with explicit timeouts plus C-surface gate. Deps: #869. DONE: implemented lib/gpu/GpuLifetime.rae (new, ~455 LOC) over an extended lib/gpu/GpuResources.rae (~595 LOC): PipelineId/BindGroupId/SubmissionId (same copyable layout), a `Recording` owner, and nonblocking completion polling — pure Rae over the generated bindings, tracking completion with wgpu's own submission index (wgpuQueueSubmitForIndex + wgpuDevicePoll(wait: 0)), so there are no callbacks and no callback-reachable state in this layer; normal polling never waits. Slot tables gained useCount/retired pins, a flat dependency arena and a view's texture as recorded INDIRECT dependency. Retention is two-level: natively an encoder/bind group holds what it records/binds and wgpu keeps a submission's resources alive; at the manager level a use is validated at record time (a retired resource never enters a recording), re-validated at submit (retired in between -> the whole submission returns none) and pinned from submit until the first poll observing completion; a bind group pins its direct deps (buffers/views/samplers/pipeline) and each view's texture for its lifetime. retire always bumps the generation (no new use) and releases the native ref only when the last pin drops (pendingReleaseCount shows the deferred ones); a bind group whose dep was retired is an "old bind group": unusable for new recordings while in-flight work finishes safely. Release vs destroy vs copy: only wgpu*Release, never Destroy; ID copy has no native effect. Abandoned recordings: dropping an unsubmitted Recording releases its encoder (no manager access needed, so nothing leaks); submit takes mod Recording and SPENDS it (own params are read-only in Rae). shutdown polls once without waiting, treats the rest as finished so deferred releases proceed, then the destructor releases dependents-first; also the device-loss path. Found that wgpu-native ABORTS the process on a WebGPU validation error at Finish, so Rae-side validation (usages, ranges, alignment, no self-copy, usable bind group) is a safety property and is enforced before wgpu sees anything. Verified: deterministic model 795_gpu_lifetime_model (early retirement, old bind group, abandoned recording, refused submit, repeated submissions with fresh generations, submission exhaustion, independent managers) and hardware check examples/zz_gpu_lifetime_check (real compute dispatch + copy with byte-exact readback of i*2, nonblocking polling, early retirement with deferred release, old bind group refused, abandoned/refused recordings, repeated submissions, independent managers, shutdown). Full watch-tests.sh green: 455 unit + 72 examples, 0 fail; C-surface gate unchanged (16 symbols, no new C). Filed #902 for a pre-existing codegen bug (explicit .drop() of a local emits an undeclared live flag for a sibling-scope local of the same type); #901's List(Ptr).copyAt workaround still applies. ArgWriter accumulates a List(UInt8); pushU32/pushI32/pushF32, pushVec2F/3F/4F, pushVec2U/4U, pushMat4 each pad to the member's WGSL alignment (scalar 4, vec2 8, vec3/vec4/mat4x4 16) so offsets match the shader; finalizeArgs rounds a uniform buffer to 16; uploadArgs writes the packed bytes to a manager buffer (a queue write that snapshots them before submit). f32 bytes are exact IEEE-754 via a raw byte reinterpret (rae_ext_rae_buf_copy is a memmove with explicit element size — Rae has no bitcast), u32/i32 peeled to little-endian bytes arithmetically (no bitwise ops). Safety is structural: the API takes ONLY numbers, so a Rae owner/List/String or a CPU validation identity (BufferId/TextureId manager tag) can never be uploaded as shader data; IDs are resolved to native handles on the CPU when a bind group is built (#870) and native holds preserved by the manager pins. ComputeCommand snapshots its workgroup counts + copyable pipeline/bind-group IDs at build time so a later CPU write cannot change recorded work; recordComputeCommand records it over #870's checked recordCompute. Compute-only manager, so this is the dispatch input; a draw command awaits a render-pipeline surface. Verified: deterministic 797_gpu_args_layout (scalar/vec2/vec3/vec4/mat4/u32 offsets — vec2@8, vec3@32, vec4@48, mat4@64, u32@128, uniform total 144 — and exact bytes for u32/i32/f32) and hardware examples/zz_gpu_args_check (pack a known struct, upload, read every field back THROUGH the shader's own std140 layout, bit patterns match). Purely additive — no existing module changed, no new C (C-surface gate unchanged at 16 symbols). Full watch-tests.sh: 457 unit + 74 examples pass; the lone example failure 119_ocean_fft is the flaky hardware FFT-ocean screenshot gate (passes in isolation, PASS: 119_ocean_fft) — unrelated (water does not import GpuArgs; this change is additive). The water consumer adopts this packer when it moves onto the manager (water still uses raw waterSet/waterGet slots, not #870); GpuArgs is the centralized layer it targets.

The #869/#870/#871/#872 manager is COMPUTE-ONLY — no render pipeline, render bind group, render pass or draw (recordCompute + createBindGroup use wgpuComputePipelineGetBindGroupLayout only; #870 explicitly deferred the draw command to "a render-pipeline surface on the manager"). But water's SURFACE is a render pass: wgpuDeviceCreateRenderPipeline (g_water_ptrs slot 0), wgpuRenderPipelineGetBindGroupLayout (slot 2 render bind group), BeginRenderPass + RenderPassEncoderSetPipeline/SetBindGroup/SetVertexBuffer/SetIndexBuffer/DrawIndexed, and that render bind group is built from the LEGACY gbuffer's per-frame resources (gbFrameUbuf/gbViewDepth/gbLitCopyView + gbMeshVbuf/Ibuf/Icount) which are not manager-owned. So water's render surface (slots 0-2) cannot move onto the manager as it exists, and because those are the render slots, the "two simultaneously active instances" and "remove g_water_ptrs/accessors" goals are blocked with it. The FFT COMPUTE half (slots ~32-159: compute pipelines, buffers, textures, views, compute bind groups, readback) IS representable today, but a compute-only partial leaves water a hybrid that still needs g_water_ptrs for the render surface and still cannot support two instances — and it is ~157 waterSet/waterGet sites across ~2000 lines gated by the flaky hardware 118/119 screenshot gates (119_ocean_fft already flakes under load). I did NOT start a partial migration because guessing wrong wastes a large, gate-risky effort. Please choose: (A) FIRST add render support to the manager as a new prerequisite task — RenderPipelineId, render bind groups from a render-pipeline layout, render-pass recording (color/depth attachments + indexed draw + vertex/index buffers), and a way to reference the gbuffer's frame resources — then migrate water onto it; (B) re-scope #873 now to migrate ONLY the FFT compute resources to manager instance groups (render surface stays on a small dedicated store; two-instance support and full g_water_ptrs removal deferred to after render support lands); or (C) another approach. The #868/#891 boundary is UNCONDITIONAL: the source-file adoption bypass is removed (parser: every extern must carry the post-parameter `unsafe`, wrong modifier order always rejected; sema: unsafe checks enabled for every decl). To make that enforceable without breaking the renderer, the 3D/2D modules were made #868-clean in this task rather than deferring to #904-#910: 342 plain externs flipped to `unsafe extern`; public APIs (Math incl. math_log->naturalLog, Sdl3, prelude nowMs/nowNs/nextTick/sleep, Time, Filesystem, Image, Gpu, Gpu2d, Gpu3d, Gbuffer/Passes/Shadow/Terrain/Inspector, TransparentForward, Shadow3d, RenderScale, SdfText) became SAFE Rae wrappers over renamed `native*` externs with explicit `rae_ext_<Module>_<name>` symbols (call sites unchanged; ~200 wrappers); externs with Ptr/Any signatures stay unsafe and their ~400 Ptr-handling functions (render modules, UI, examples, stdlib templates List/StringMap/IntMap/Query/ComponentTable, ecs) wrap at the source site; positive fixtures updated (30) and negatives retained. Compiler fixes found by the enforcement: (1) generic specializations were analysed inside the CALLER's context — inheriting its unsafe depth (obligations erased through generics), diagnostic origin and loop depth — now reset per specialization; (2) generic templates with positional `T: type` params are never analysed at all (bound by name in the backend) — added a by-name unsafe-obligation scan of template bodies (same rule as #897), which is what caught the stdlib containers; (3) escape analysis did not see through `unsafe { }` (a `ret` of a buf_get alias inside the block was treated as owned -> double free in Json/scene/gltf); (4) collection-mutation invalidation walker ignored unsafe blocks; (5) backend prototypes for explicit `rae_ext_*` symbols (platform-guarded headers). New fixtures: 798 declaration-only file w/o unsafe token, 799 cross-file call from a token-free file, 800 generic obligation not erased by the caller's unsafe block, 801 unsafe-block ret-alias no double free; 441 expectation re-anchored. docs/unsafe-boundary.md documents guarantees, the wrapper convention, identity limitations and remaining platform constraints (last slot table g_gt_handles -> #906; examples/legacy unmaintained and not compiling under the boundary). DONE. Rebuilt the Compiler tab as ONE screen (verified with a 1440x900 headless-Chrome screenshot of the live dashboard: no page scroll). Layout: a toolbar with `Build & test` as the primary action, then Build/Clean/Rebuild and Run tests/Stop, with the run timer and the test + build status chips pushed right (green `is-success` outline kept); a compact options row (disable-tests, graphical-examples, parallel toggles); then a segmented step strip `Build log | Test log | Results` over a SINGLE log stage that fills the remaining viewport — only one log is shown at a time, the suite summary (pass/fail pills) rides on the strip so it is always visible, and each step carries its own copy buttons (`Copy full log`, `Copy errors and warnings`, `Copy errors only`). The view auto-follows a run by observing the two status chips (MutationObserver, no edits to the existing run logic): build running -> Build log, tests running -> Test log, tests finished -> Results, which shows the green `All tests passing!` box or the failures with context and tints the Results step green/red. Removed from the tab: the per-test list + `Test details & actions` inspector (Result/History/Source, and the dead `Run live` button — the Live target was deleted in #957), the `Next steps` panel, and the `Status stream` — the latter was RELOCATED (same `#status-feed` id, so app.js keeps prepending) into the Test log tab as a collapsed `Server events` drawer. Every DOM binding app.js still uses was kept with its id; the removed ones were already null-guarded in app.js (audited), so no JS logic changed beyond the appended 49-line step/observer block. Sizing uses a body/main flex column (no magic header-height number); other tabs keep their natural height and page scroll. rebuild finished (exit 0) in 3.5s DONE — fixed properly (deleted), not hidden: every one of the 9 warnings was genuinely dead code. The 7 in main.c were VM-era leftovers the #957 Live-target deletion missed — write_u32/u8/i64/f64 + write_bytes (the bytecode chunk-file serialisers), derive_entry_stem/derive_manifest_path/write_function_manifest/type_ref_to_cstr (the VM output/manifest helpers) and watch_compile_sigsegv + its g_watch_compile_jmp/g_watch_compile_armed sigjmp globals + the now-dead <setjmp.h> include (the VM-watch crash guard); removing the first layer exposed a second layer of helpers whose only callers were the first, so both layers went (12 functions + 2 globals + 2 forward decls + 1 include). sema.c: sema_import_package was unreferenced. c_expr.c: lhs_tr was computed and never read (the string-equality path keys off eff_base_name). Result: `make clean build` (DEV_FAST, the mode in the report) is 0 warnings, and `make MODE=CI_STRICT clean build` (-Werror) now passes with 0 warnings/errors — the first time the strict mode builds clean. Verified: full watch-tests.sh suite 489/0 (RAE_RUN_END exit=0), so nothing live was removed.

- [postponed] #827 {difficulty:4} [renderer/TAA] Improve motion-vector precision when a named desktop scene needs TAA. Replace the current 8-bit G-buffer motion encoding with an RG16Float target or an agreed 16-bit packing; retain neighborhood clamping and evaluate negative texture LOD bias under jitter. FXAA remains the default and TAA opt-in; water work does not reopen this task automatically. Verify: 114 top-down terrain and moving crowds compared with FXAA for reprojection error, smear and shimmer. Enables #828.
- [postponed] #828 {difficulty:4} [renderer/TAA] Improve TAA resolve after #827: YCoCg variance clipping from the 3x3 mean/stddev, velocity-weighted history blending, unjittered history sampling and optional sharpening. Revisit only for a scene that needs TAA; FXAA remains the accepted default. Verify: static detail, fast crowd motion, ghosting and disocclusion against the current resolve and FXAA. Deps: #827.
- [postponed] #811 {difficulty:5} [compiler/design] Revisit module-qualified type references only when a real collision cannot reasonably be solved with distinct prefixed type names. UiWorld/World3d/Scene naming remains the accepted answer. If that trigger occurs, obtain design sign-off on type qualifier spelling, generic arguments, ambiguity diagnostics and module/type resolution before implementation. Refs: #770, docs/naming-conventions.md, docs/module-namespacing.md.
- [x] #888 {difficulty:2} [lifecycle/adoption] Delete createList/createStringMap/createIntMap/createComponentTable and give every type one constructor verb `create`. Superseded by #890, which does this with the decided syntax `List.create(EntityId, cap: N)`. (First attempt renamed the four to `create` and swept to bare/qualified `create` that infer the element type; reverted because inference hit #889. The decided syntax passes the type explicitly and avoids that.) CLOSED 2026-09-12 (queue review): delivered by #890 (`List.create(T, cap:)` and the one `create` verb are the tree-wide spelling now).
- [x] #863 {difficulty:3} [compiler/interop] Fix existing Ptr lowering needed by the ReadRequest owner pilot: a Ptr field must compile without an invalid generated string conversion; List(Ptr) and mod Ptr must not emit nonexistent rae_Ptr types. Reproduce each case and repair the maintained C backend without changing pointer ownership or adding syntax. Native pointer storage remains internal interop, not a public ownership model. Add focused compile/run regressions, including a destructor-bearing Ptr wrapper with no copy; verify through compiler/tools/watch-tests.sh with an explicit timeout. Reactivated because #887 is now a concrete consumer.
- [x] #864 {difficulty:3} [compiler] Fix view UInt64 emission in C struct field expressions: createBuffer(usage: view UInt64) must not emit a scalar uint64_t parameter and then dereference it as (*usage) when initializing WGPUBufferDescriptor.usage. Reproduce with a minimal C-boundary field and cover unsigned view reads while preserving parameter semantics. copy UInt64 is the current workaround. Compiled only; verify through compiler/tools/watch-tests.sh with an explicit timeout. Independent of GPU ownership and proposed unsafe syntax.
- [x] #887 {difficulty:4} [gpu/readback pilot] Wrap the existing webgpu/Readback native request in an ordinary Rae owner using shipped create/drop and no copy, after #863. Preserve the current C callback-owned state, retained buffer, bounded mapping and cancellation protocol. Prove optional construction failure, ownership transfer, automatic and explicit consuming drop, fields and relevant supported container cleanup; do not add ownership keywords or re-ban containers. Use direct if let owner bindings where appropriate: regression 775 already proves App ownership, mod frame calls and branch cleanup without a second var/own binding. Verify two independent requests, repeated map/unmap, late cancellation callbacks, destination List growth and exact-once release through existing deterministic/native tests and narrow hardware checks. Label this an internal lifecycle pilot until the pointer boundary is implemented; do not mechanically remove renderer shutdown protocols or migrate all GPU owners here. Use watch-tests.sh and hardware runs with explicit timeouts. Deps: #863; shipped lifecycle #880-#886.
- [x] #891 {difficulty:5} [design/interop] Review the minimal pointer-operation boundary in docs/ptr-and-gpu-resource-design.md against the concrete #887 owner. Produce complete user-authored module and normal App/RenderSystem examples using shipped create/drop/copy and direct if let app: App = App.create(); no redundant mutable owner rebinding. Specify proposed unsafe blocks/function obligations for raw Ptr reads/writes/default initialization, foreign calls and raw callbacks, while whole-owner borrowing/transfer remains ordinary Rae. Demonstrate rejection of accidental wrapper duplication and generic/reflection/serialization/callable escapes, and show safe bounds/state checks remain valid if public metadata is modified. Any module may author interop; no privileged registry, opaque/noncopyable/drop property, stored Rae borrows or special main parameter. Obtain approval of exact syntax and rules before #868; agreement with this queue is not syntax approval. Reuse the shipped lifecycle contract instead of reopening it. Deps: #887. QUESTION: Do you approve revision 6's nine exact rules, including `unsafe { ... }`, `unsafe func`, automatically unsafe extern calls, local unsafe blocks inside unsafe functions, operation-level Ptr restrictions, and unchanged whole-owner lifecycle behavior; if not, which numbered point and exact replacement do you want? ANSWERED: The maintainer approved the contract with Rae-shaped post-parameter declarations: `func name(...) unsafe ret T` and mandatory `func name(...) unsafe extern("symbol") ret T`; `extern` selects the foreign ABI while `unsafe` independently records the caller obligation, so every extern has both and unsafe Rae functions need not be extern. OUTCOME: Revision 7 records this syntax and the unchanged operation, lifecycle, generic and callback rules; dependent tasks #868, #876 and #877 now require it.
- [x] #868 {difficulty:4} [compiler/interop] Implement the approved revision 7 contract from #891: lexical `unsafe { ... }`; the post-parameter modifier `func name(...) unsafe ret T`; and explicit dual marking on every foreign declaration as `func name(...) unsafe extern("symbol") ret T`, with an unmarked extern rejected. `extern` selects the foreign ABI while `unsafe` independently carries the caller obligation; all unsafe-function and extern calls require an unsafe block, and unsafe-function bodies still mark their own raw operations locally. Require unsafe for Ptr creation/read/write/copy/defaults and resolved generic/reflection/serialization escape paths while whole-owner borrow/move/return/drop and supported owner containers remain ordinary Rae. Unsafe must not bypass shipped create/drop/copy or stored-borrow diagnostics. Do not add pointer arithmetic, first-class callables, module privileges, owner properties or a safe-extern escape hatch; preserve raw callback obligations in generic C state. Adapt the #887 owner to a safe collection-borrow copy surface while retaining internal raw protocols. Inventory existing bindings/Ptr consumers and provide a staged audit/compatibility path until #877 final enforcement; add bounded migration tasks for uncovered consumers. Verify the reviewed user-module example, modifier placement, rejected unmarked externs and negative escapes through watch-tests.sh with an explicit timeout. Deps: #891. OUTCOME: Added lexical unsafe blocks, post-parameter unsafe functions and explicit unsafe-extern syntax with staged source-file enforcement; raw Ptr operations and resolved unsafe calls now require local blocks without weakening create/drop/copy or borrow checks. ReadRequest now copies through a checked mod List(T) surface. Queued generated bindings (#893), non-renderer interop (#894), legacy platform APIs (#895) and remaining GPU consumers (#876) before final enforcement. Verified 448 compiler tests, 70 example gates, deterministic sanitizer coverage, hardware readback and the C-surface gate.
- [x] #866 {difficulty:3} [compiler/borrows] Enforce the existing prohibition on stored view/mod references in the maintained Compiled front end, a prerequisite from docs/ptr-and-gpu-resource-design.md. Reproduce the 334_field_restriction gap that reached invalid generated C; reject direct and nested/generic field escapes as applicable, while preserving legal parameter and local borrows. Inspect current enforcement before adding duplicate checks. No new reference semantics and no VM work. - [x] #769 {difficulty:4} [ecs] Flip hierarchy authority: `Children` (ordered) is AUTHORITATIVE and serialized; `Parent` is the DERIVED reverse index, rebuilt by HierarchySystem. Reverses #711. Why: sibling ORDER is real information (layout + paint order) that lives only in Children — component tables are swap-remove, so rebuildFromParent scrambles sibling order after any destroy; a Parent-serialized snapshot would reload with shuffled siblings. Children arrays are also how humans and .raescene author trees. API: setParent stays the single mutation entry (now edits the parent's Children list + rebuilds the child's Parent); add insertChild(parent, child, index) for explicit ordering; a `rebuildParentsFromChildren` self-heal; enforce the single-parent invariant (a child may appear in exactly one Children list — reparent removes it from the old list first). depthOrder dirty-tracks on the Children table generation instead of Parent's. Update registry flags (#730): Children `serialize`, Parent 0. Files: lib/ecs/hierarchy.rae, lib/ecs/HierarchySystem/HierarchySystem.rae, lib/ui/registry_flags.rae, callers (scene_loader/scene_instance/frames unchanged API). Deps: #726. Verify: REGRESSION TEST — build a 5-sibling tree, destroy sibling 2, rebuild Parent from Children, sibling order must survive; 687/688/692/704 pass; 106 screenshot byte-identical. DoD: Children is the source of truth for the hierarchy including order; Parent is a cache. - [x] #772 {difficulty:4} [compiler] Compile-time field reflection v1, per docs/compile-time-reflection.md: (1) LEXER: reserve lowercase `any` as a keyword (compile-time type wildcard, legal only inside a type pattern; the existing `Any` runtime box is UNTOUCHED — `ComponentTable(Any)` stays the concrete dynamic-box table type, which is exactly why the wildcard needs its own spelling); (2) SEMA: recognise `fields(value)` in the iterable position of the EXISTING `loop let x: <mode> T in EXPR` form (parser unchanged) as a field loop over a struct value; the binding's explicit type is the filter (`ComponentTable(any)` matches every ComponentTable field regardless of element type, a concrete type matches only that type); type-check the body ONCE PER MATCHING FIELD with the binding aliased to `value.<field>` at that field's concrete type; `let`+`view`/`mod` only (`var` = copy of a heap struct, rejected by the existing alias rule); a non-`fields(...)` struct iterable and `any` outside a type pattern are errors with clear diagnostics; (3) `fieldName(binding)` = compiler-recognised function folded to the slot-name String literal, also reachable via UFCS `binding.fieldName()`; (4) EXPANSION: unroll the loop into N copies of the body BEFORE codegen so BOTH backends (C + VM) get it for free — no per-backend special case; zero matching fields = empty. Scope OUT (v2, #774): `fields(Type)` / construction, bare `view any` + `match`, nested recursion. Tests: compiler/tests/cases: filter by concrete type, filter by `ComponentTable(any)`, `mod` write-through into the real field, `fieldName` literal, zero-match loop, `any` misuse diagnostic, `var` rejected. Verify: full suite via watch-tests.sh + examples green. DoD: a non-generic `func clearAll(world: mod UiWorld, entity: view EntityId) { loop let table: mod ComponentTable(any) in fields(world) { componentRemove(this: table, entity: entity) } }` compiles on both backends to the same calls the hand-written destroyEntity makes today. [LANG-CHECK: after finishing, note in docs/ecs-language-wishlist.md any missing Rae language feature that would have made this task easier or cleaner — add a bullet only if it is new there; skip if nothing new.] DONE: lexer reserves `any`; parser accepts it in type position; sema unrolls `loop let x: view/mod T in fields(value)` into `if true { let x => value.f; body }` per matching field BEFORE the switch, so both backends see plain AST; binding type is the filter (`ComponentTable(any)` vs concrete); `fieldName(x)` / `x.fieldName()` fold to the slot-name literal; clear diagnostics for `any` as a real type and for `var`. Tests 714_field_loop_clear/715_field_loop_fieldname/716_field_loop_any_misuse/717_field_loop_var_rejected. DoD verified: `clearAll(world: mod UiWorld, ...)` compiles + unrolls. Suite 377/377 + examples 66/66 green. v2 (`fields(Type)`, bare `view any`+match, recursion) is #774. LANG-CHECK: nothing new.

- [x] #776 {difficulty:1} i saw a pub function. grassDiagTick has ”pub” marked. That is legal, but redundant syntax. Remove the ”pub” from at least that module in all funcs. - Then i saw "var profiler: Profiler = profile.createProfiler()". Does that need to be "var"? Make it "let" if possible in a one line change. If not, then tell me why it needs to be var. DONE: (1) removed `pub` from all 6 funcs in the grass module (GrassCompute.rae after #777) — redundant syntax, 114 builds. (2) `var profiler` -> `let profiler` WORKS (examples/zz_gpu_timing_check/main.rae:13): profiler is only passed as a `mod Profiler` argument, never rebound or directly field-assigned, and Rae lets a `let`-bound heap value be mutated THROUGH a `mod` ref — `var` is only needed for rebinding or a direct `profiler.x = ...` write. zz builds+links. Incidental #777 sweep-miss fixed in the same zz file so it builds: the renamed module GpuTiming collided with `type GpuTiming` on qualified calls (`GpuTiming.f()` resolved the type, not the package) — switched to `open GpuTiming` + bare calls; also fixed one stale `gpu_timing.` qualifier the sweep skipped because it sat inside a string interpolation `{...}`.

(2) UiWorld->World2d measured at 579 occurrences across 105 files (not "100+"): pure churn that drops the UI-domain hint; the collision problem it targets is better solved by per-module qualified type refs (the wishlist item), per the task's own recommendation — declined, not gated as a [question] because the task already recommends against it. (3) lib/ui Scene->Scene2d is NOT low-churn: ~38 type refs across ~29 files, and a type-only rename splits Scene from its own family (SceneNode/parseScene/SceneRegistry/loadScene) — a bigger, worse sweep — declined. (4) the originally-blocked Scene3dAsset->Scene3d half is moot (both deleted by #771/#797). Net: the two ECS worlds stay UiWorld / World3d and the one authored-content type stays Scene (2D UI); future collisions should prefer module-qualified type names over renaming. - [x] #774 {difficulty:4} [compiler, deferred] Field reflection v2, per docs/compile-time-reflection.md open questions: (1) `fields(Type)` — iterate a TYPE's fields with no value, plus the small design for how the loop body assembles a struct literal, so `createUiWorld`/`createWorld3d`/`createGameWorld` become one generic `createWorld(W: type)`; (2) bare `loop let field: view any in fields(x)` + `match field { case ComponentTable(any) {...} case EntityAllocator {...} }` for multi-kind dispatch; (3) decide one-level vs recursive `fields()` (design says one level, recurse explicitly). Only start if #760/#768/#771 show a real need — construction is one line per table and the cheapest of the three whole-world ops. Deps: #773. Verify: suite green. DoD: the create half of the world boilerplate is gone, or the task is closed as not-needed with the reason noted in the doc. [LANG-CHECK: after finishing, note in docs/ecs-language-wishlist.md any missing Rae language feature that would have made this task easier or cleaner — add a bullet only if it is new there; skip if nothing new.] DONE — CLOSED AS NOT-NEEDED (the DoD's second branch), reasoning recorded in docs/compile-time-reflection.md (new "Construction via `fields(Type)` — decided NOT to build (#774)" section + Status line + open-questions bullet marked RESOLVED). The built-in gate ("only start if #760/#768/#771 show a real need") is NOT met, and the assessment against the current tree is decisive: (1) The failure mode reflection would prevent is ALREADY a compile error — Rae builds a struct from a literal with EVERY field present, so createUiWorld/createWorld3d/createGameWorld cannot silently drop a table the way a hand-listed clear/serialize loop can (that silent-drop risk is exactly why the CLEAR + SERIALIZE halves DID land via fields(value) in #760/#772/#773). Reflection buys no safety here, only brevity. (2) It is the cheapest boilerplate, written once per world, one line per field. (3) It is the MOST expensive to implement for the least gain: unlike clear/serialize which ITERATE an existing value, construction has no value to iterate and no incremental-assembly form in the language (a struct is built all-at-once from a literal) — a generic createWorld(W: type) would need type-level fields(Type), a NEW struct-assembly construct (the unsolved "how the loop assembles the literal" open question), AND per-field-kind match dispatch because the worlds are NON-uniform (alongside ComponentTable(T) they carry EntityAllocator, a StringMap `UiWorld.nodeIds`, a `GameWorld.hierarchyOrder`, and a scalar `GameWorld.splashTick: 0`); that match+assembly is plausibly as much code as the three explicit constructors plus large new compiler machinery. (4) No real need surfaced — #771/#793-797 added World3d and used the explicit createWorld3d (6 tables) without friction. LANG-CHECK: nothing NEW — the enabling gap (no incremental struct assembly) is already covered by the wishlist's "Synthesize a world from its component set" bullet (whose design doc IS compile-time-reflection.md); I did NOT add a duplicate bullet, only kept that existing bullet's status accurate (noting the construction half was declined). VERIFIED: docs-only change (git status: QUEUE.md + docs/compile-time-reflection.md + docs/ecs-language-wishlist.md — zero code files), so the suite is unaffected and green as of #797's run (cases 390/0, examples 66/0). No commit contains only a QUEUE tick — the doc decision is the substantive change.

- [x] #798 {difficulty:4} [compiler] Fix move-vs-copy for a named heap-owning local placed into a struct-literal FIELD. Found in #795: `var world: World3d = createWorld3d(); ...; ret TetrisRenderState { world: world, ... }` shallow-COPIES `world` instead of moving it, so the local and the struct field share the same ComponentTable buffers and BOTH drop them at scope exit -> double-free (SIGTRAP in libsystem_malloc free, via rae_drop_struct_rae_World3d -> ComponentTable drop -> rae_buf_free). `ret namedLocal` (whole return value) DOES move correctly (buildScene #794, buildTetrisWorld), and an INLINE rvalue in a struct field moves too (World3dAsset `{ world: buildWorld3dFromScene3d(...) }`) — only a NAMED local as the last use in a struct-literal field is mishandled. Workaround shipped in 97: build via a helper returning World3d and inline it in the literal. Fix: treat a named local's last use inside a struct-literal field as a MOVE (or, if a copy is intended, deep-copy the heap). Verify: a repro (own-heap local -> struct field, no use after) drops exactly once; full suite green. DoD: the pattern moves rather than double-frees, and 97's helper workaround can be simplified back if desired. DONE. ROOT CAUSE (confirmed via minimal repro): `ret Struct { field: namedLocal }` where the field type transitively owns a NON-deep-copyable type (a `ComponentTable` inside `World3d`) took the struct-field deep-copy path (c_expr.c) — but the SYNTHESISED `rae_deep_copy_<T>` shallow-copies such a field (`dst->table = src->table`) instead of erroring like a DIRECT ComponentTable field does; the local is then dropped at scope exit → the field and the local free the same buffers twice (SIGABRT in rae_ext_rae_buf_free). FIX (the task's preferred MOVE): a bare owning LOCAL used as a struct-literal field is now MOVED when that literal IS the function's return value (last use is guaranteed). New `CFuncContext.return_value_expr` (set to the ret value node around its emit in c_stmt.c) lets the struct-field emitter (c_expr.c) recognise the exact `ret Struct { f: local }` node; for a bare owning (non view/mod) LET/var local appearing exactly once in that literal it emits the bare value (ownership transfer) and marks the local moved (mark_local_moved_by_name) so the return epilogue's implicit-drop pass skips it — no deep-copy, no double-free. Tightly scoped: only the exact return-value literal (nested/wrapped literals and non-return `let x = Struct{ f: local }` still deep-copy, verified a local USED AFTER still deep-copies correctly); owned-String fields keep their existing path; parameters keep their own move logic. VERIFIED: new case 731_ret_struct_field_move (World-wrapping-ComponentTable moved through a struct field, drops exactly once — SIGABRT'd before the fix, clean after); emitted C for the repro + 97 shows `.field = local` (bare move) not a deep-copy. SIMPLIFIED 97 back: inlined buildTetrisWorld into createTetrisRenderState using the now-safe `ret TetrisRenderState { world: world, ... }` named-local pattern (helper removed); 97 renders non-blank (829 colours). Full suite green via watch-tests.sh (RAE_RUN_END exit=0): cases 391 pass/0 fail (incl. 731), examples 66 pass/0 fail; the ownership/leak regression tests (432_mobile_ui_leak_regression, 425-453 owning-field/move suite) all pass — no double-free or leak regressions from the codegen change.

- [x] #807 {difficulty:5} [compiler+stdlib, DESIGN-FIRST] ECS queries over N component types + ergonomic iteration — the "best minimal ECS language" gap flagged in #805's report. STATE: multi-type-param generic inference LANDED (#707), but joins are hand-written PER ARITY — only `query2`/`query3` exist (lib/ecs/Query.rae), no `query4`+, and consuming a result is still the verbose index+accessor form (`componentCount` + `componentEntityAt(i)` + `componentDataAt(i)` + `componentGet`). Listed as OPEN in docs/ecs-language-wishlist.md "Generics & queries"; this task turns it into a design + implementation. SCOPE — TRIMMED through the data-oriented/minimalism lens (decision recorded in the wishlist): (1) N-ARITY as a small FIXED LADDER — add `query4`/`query5` in the same hand-written style as query2/query3 (explicit, analyzable, and enough; nobody joins 8 tables in a hot loop). NOT variadic / type-list generics — that is metaprogramming and is DECLINED. (2) ITERATION SUGAR — `for (entity, mod a, mod b) in query(world, A, B)` desugaring to the join walk with mod/view binding modes preserved; this is the real boilerplate win on the single most-used ECS operation. (3) FILTERS as PLAIN FUNCTIONS, not a builder: `with T`/`without T` is just "is the tag/component present on this entity" (an O(1) sparse check), and change-detection ("only entities whose A changed since last run") is a plain comparison against the `generation`/`denseStamps` the tables ALREADY carry. NO composable query-builder object / mini-DSL — DECLINED as a framework creeping in. Keep every join O(smallest table): drive off the smallest ComponentTable, O(1) sparse membership on the rest — exactly how query2/query3 work. This is the data-oriented core, what a performance engineer hand-writes; the task only removes the boilerplate around it. DoD: a short design note in the wishlist with the maintainer's sign-off on the sugar spelling; then query4/query5 + the iteration sugar + with/without + change-detection as plain functions, with tests (4-/5-way join, tag filter, change-detection skip, mod write-through). Refs: #707, docs/ecs-api-reference.md. PROGRESS — the NON-SYNTAX half is DONE, verified and committed; the iteration SUGAR needs your sign-off on its spelling before it can be built (it is new syntax, and AGENTS.md forbids adding syntax without approval). LANDED in lib/ecs/Query.rae: (1) `query4`/`query5` + `Query4Match`/`Query5Match` + `query4ViewA..D`/`query5ViewA..E`, hand-written in exactly the query2/query3 smallest-table-probe style (iterate the smallest table, O(1) sparse probe on the rest, O(min table)) — a FIXED ladder, no variadic generics; (2) filters as plain functions, no builder: `with T` is the existing `tagged(data, tag)`; NEW `without(data, tag)` (entities lacking the tag — a negation walks the data table, one O(1) probe each); NEW `changedSince(table, sinceGeneration)` (per-ENTITY change-detection read straight off the `denseStamps` the table already keeps — a system records `componentTableGeneration` after a run and passes it next time; complements Schedule's table-level skip). TESTS: 732_ecs_query4_query5 (4-/5-way joins with the smallest table landing in different driver slots, view reads, mod write-through read-back) and 733_ecs_query_filters (with/without/changedSince incl. the idle skip case). Full suite GREEN via watch-tests.sh (RAE_RUN_END exit=0: cases 393/0, examples 66/0). DOCS: docs/ecs-api-reference.md query section extended (query4/5, the three filters) and three drift bugs fixed (`hit.denseA`->`indexA`, `queryTagged`->`tagged`, `query.rae`->`Query.rae`); the sugar design note is written into docs/ecs-language-wishlist.md "Ergonomic query iteration". FINDING that shapes the question: the verbose index+accessor form the wishlist complained about is ALREADY gone — a query returns match records with each entity's dense index, so today's idiom is one line per component (`let p: mod Pos => queryModAt(table: pos, denseIndex: hit.indexA)`). The only sugar left is binding components in the loop head; Rae has no `for` and no tuple destructuring, so the informal `for (entity, mod a, mod b) in query(...)` sketch is not Rae — the one spelling that fits the grammar is a MULTI-BINDING loop head with POSITIONAL binding->table mapping: `loop let entity: EntityId, p: mod Pos, v: view Vel in query2(tableA: pos, tableB: vel) { … }` (desugars to the match loop + accessor aliases; a `mod` binding bumps the stamp like queryModAt, a `view` binding does not). Honest lens: not a second vocabulary and it is on the hottest path, but the payoff is ONE saved line per component, it adds an implicit positional mapping, and it costs parser+sema+codegen — a performance engineer does not need it. QUESTION: for the iteration sugar, pick one — (A) ADOPT the multi-binding `loop let entity: EntityId, p: mod Pos, v: view Vel in query2(...)` as the single blessed spelling and I implement it (parser+sema+codegen, positional binding->table, mod/view modes preserved, tests), or (B) DECLINE the sugar and keep the explicit one-line-per-component accessor form as the idiom (consistent with the data-oriented lens; I would then record it in the wishlist as a decision and close this task). My lean is (B). Which? DONE (option A chosen by the maintainer). The ECS QUERY LOOP is now a first-class statement: `loop let entity: EntityId, p: mod Pos, v: view Vel in query2(tableA: pos, tableB: vel) { p.x = p.x + v.v }` — works over query2..query5 and forEach (bare or `Query.`-qualified); an optional leading `EntityId` binding receives the entity; the remaining bindings map POSITIONALLY to the joined tables and must be `mod` (writes through, bumps the change stamp exactly like queryModAt) or `view` (never dirties); nested query loops each own their own result. IMPLEMENTATION: pure sugar resolved in the PARSER (compiler/src/parser.c parse_query_loop) — the statement expands to a two-statement chain: a hoisted hidden owned `let raeQueryHits<N>: List(QueryNMatch) = query…(…)` plus the match loop `loop let raeQueryHit<N>: view QueryNMatch in raeQueryHits<N>` whose body is prefixed with `let entity = hit.entity` and one `let p: mod T => queryModAt(table: pos, denseIndex: hit.indexA)` / `queryViewAt` alias per component. Sema and codegen are UNTOUCHED and see ordinary Rae, so the compiled C is byte-for-byte the hand-written idiom. The hoist matters: codegen snapshots a loop iterable once but explicitly does not own/drop it, so iterating a call directly would have leaked the result list every loop — hoisting into an owned `let` makes ownership identical to the hand-written form. parse_block now walks a returned statement chain when appending (a no-op for every other statement) and parallelLoop marks the loop inside the chain. DIAGNOSTICS (parse-time, exact caret): component-count vs table-count mismatch; a component binding that is neither mod nor view; a non-query iterable; a computed table argument (must be a name/field path because the component is aliased back into that same table). TESTS: 734_ecs_query_loop (query2 entity+mod+view with read-back, view-only loop leaves the table generation unchanged, query3 with two mod bindings, forEach single-table, nested query loops) + rejects 735_reject_query_loop_arity and 736_reject_query_loop_mode (expected.txt captured from the real diagnostics). Full suite GREEN via watch-tests.sh (RAE_RUN_END exit=0: cases 396/0 incl. the 5 new #807 cases, examples 66/0) — no existing loop regressed. DOCS: docs/ecs-api-reference.md "The query loop — Rae's native join walk" (rules + the exact desugaring) and docs/ecs-language-wishlist.md marks the iteration-sugar item DECIDED (A) + LANDED with the rationale: a language that bets on ECS should make its most common operation its cleanest line, and it costs nothing at runtime. Together with the earlier half (query4/query5 fixed ladder, without, changedSince as plain functions) this closes #807. [>claude]->[x].
- [x] #809 {difficulty:3} [compiler] Compile-time `typeName(T)` / element-type-name reflection (wishlist "Element type-name reflection", surfaced by #760). `fieldName(table)` folds a ComponentTable's FIELD name (`positions`) to a String literal, but the serialize/registry vocabulary is keyed by the COMPONENT type name (`Position`); with only `fieldName` the registry must be re-keyed by field name (the workaround shipped for example 116), forcing TWO vocabularies (`Rect` vs `rects`) — one for code, one for the human-authored `.raescene` loader (#768). Add a compile-time primitive that folds the name of `T` in a `ComponentTable(T)` (or a bare `T` type param) to a String literal, SAME rules as `fieldName`: a plain compile-time function called with parens (UFCS ok), never a phantom member, never an `@`-sigil, not runtime type info. Lets `loop ... in fields(world) { addComponentTable(name: typeName(table)) }` key by the canonical component name and share ONE registry vocabulary with the scene loader. Small, concrete, mechanism already exists (fieldName is the template). DoD: `typeName`/element-type-name folds correctly for owned + generic component types, a test, and example 116's registry re-keyed back to the component name. Refs: #760/#772/#773 (fields/fieldName landed), docs/compile-time-reflection.md. DONE. `typeName(binding)` / `binding.typeName()` now folds to the COMPONENT type name as a String literal — the `Position` in `ComponentTable(Position)` — beside `fieldName`, which folds the SLOT name (`positions`); for a non-generic field it folds to the field's own type name (`Int` for `count: Int`). Same rules as fieldName: a compile-time plain function called with parens, UFCS ok, never a phantom member, never an `@`-sigil, not runtime type info; using it outside a `fields()` loop is just an unknown function. IMPLEMENTATION (compiler/src/sema.c, mechanism = the fieldName template): the fold walker was GENERALISED rather than duplicated — `reflect_is_field_name_call` became `reflect_metadata_call_kind` (0 none / 1 fieldName / 2 typeName) and the three fold functions carry a `type_name` literal alongside `field_name`, picking one by kind; the literal is computed by a new `reflect_element_type_name(field_type)` (base name of the first non-value generic arg, else the field type's own base name) at the ONE shared unroll site `reflect_fill_unrolled`, which serves both the non-generic (#772) and the generic-W instantiation (#773) paths — so both fold identically with no backend change. TEST 737_ecs_type_name: generic-arg branch (`rects=Rect sizes=Size`), non-generic branch via an `any` loop (`count:Int label:String`), UFCS `table.typeName()`, a concrete `ComponentTable(Size)` filter, and a generic-W function to exercise the #773 path; 715 (fieldName) unchanged. - [x] #810 {difficulty:2} [stdlib+docs] Stable dense-iteration order — DOCUMENT the guarantee (the default); do NOT add an ordered structure unless a named system provably needs it. Migrations rely on "dense order == insertion order" for render-submit order and pool slots, but `componentRemove` does a swap-remove that silently REORDERS the table — an undocumented invariant that can break a render pass or pool with no compile error. The swap-remove is the CORRECT data-oriented choice (O(1) remove, keeps the array dense); order instability is its honest cost, so the fix is to write the truth down, not to compromise the layout. DO: (a) document the exact guarantee in docs/ecs-api-reference.md ("dense order is insertion order UNTIL the first swap-remove, then unspecified") and AUDIT every order-dependent system (render submit, pool slots) so each either provably holds under that rule or is made order-independent (e.g. sort by a stable key at submit). ONLY IF a specific system is found that genuinely cannot be made order-independent, then (b) add the SMALLEST explicit opt-in — e.g. an order-preserving `componentRemove` variant at O(n) used by THAT table alone — never a general ordered index paying memory on every table. Cheap correctness win. DoD: guarantee written; audit recorded per system; a test pins the chosen behaviour across a remove. DONE — option (a) throughout; NO ordered structure was added. CONTRACT written in docs/ecs-api-reference.md as a dedicated "Iteration-order contract (#810)" section: dense order is insertion order UNTIL the first non-tail componentRemove; a non-tail remove is a swap-remove that moves the LAST row into the hole (its data and change stamp move with it, sparse map repointed, array stays dense — the deliberate O(1) data-oriented choice); after that, order is UNSPECIFIED; removing the TAIL leaves the rest untouched; dense indices / QueryNMatch are valid only for the current walk, never across a structural mutation or frames; never derive draw order, pool slots or "the first one" from dense order — carry the key as a component field or pick by a stable key. Also records WHY there is deliberately no order-preserving remove and no ordered index (memory/O(n) on every table for a convenience no system needs), and the smallest opt-in if a future system ever genuinely needs it. PINNED by new test 738_ecs_dense_order (insertion order; non-tail remove -> tail row lands in the hole with data + stamp intact and the moved entity still findable at its new dense index; tail remove -> rest untouched). AUDIT recorded per system in the same doc as a table — every dense-order consumer in the tree: UI paint (explicit LayerRoot.order + hierarchy traversal, never dense draw order), deferred G-buffer (depth-resolved), forward Gpu3d meshes (opaque + depth-tested), 114 grass (each blade carries `slot`), 114 terrain props/tiles (each carries `PropKind.slot` — the pool slot is a component field, never the dense index), ui/Layer (explicit order), 106 hero widgets (key lookup / rename-all), 106 debug anchor (first-of-one singleton), query joins (walk-scoped by contract), and a grep confirming ZERO cross-frame-cached dense indices exist. All hold. ONE latent dependence found and made order-independent: the SDF metaball cluster HEAD in lib/Gpu3dWorld.rae (renderSceneSdfWorld + renderSceneShadowSdfWorld) — the primitive supplying the fused surface's material/smoothing — was "first cluster member in dense order", so a swap-remove could silently change which ball leads a cluster; it now picks the member with the LOWEST EntityId.index (a stable key, same complexity, membership still the explicit `cluster` id). Visually a no-op today (cluster members share material), verified by the unchanged render gates. Also fixed two stale lines in the same doc section (Query2Match/Query3Match were still shown with denseA/B/C; the real fields are indexA/B/C) and marked the wishlist bullet landed. Full suite GREEN via watch-tests.sh (RAE_RUN_END exit=0: cases 398/0, examples 66/0 incl. the 111/112/96/97 SDF+deferred render gates). [>claude]->[x].
- [x] #824 {difficulty:3} make a test of a script, which runs the tests in parallel? How fast could we potentially run them, and would they still be reliable? DONE: prototype `compiler/tools/run_tests_parallel.sh` (opt-in; `make test`/watch-tests.sh untouched) — runs the 450 cases N at a time by invoking the OFFICIAL `tools/run_tests.sh <case>` per case (no re-implemented comparison), aggregates the per-case logs, then runs the example gate sequentially. MEASURED (10-core Apple Silicon, cases only): sequential 571 s; 4 jobs 449 s (1.3x); 10 jobs 215 s (2.7x). RELIABILITY: 3 consecutive 10-job runs + the 4-job run gave byte-identical verdict sets; after the fixes below, 3 more 10-job runs matched the sequential set EXACTLY (411/0, zero diff) — no flaky case found. Why not 10x: each case pays a fixed ~4 s (bash + one `rae run` that gcc-compiles the runtime), tail dominated by the long cases; the next real win is caching the compiled runtime object across cases (compiler/build change), not more jobs. SAFETY CHECKED: temp C/bin are pid-unique; per-case build cache is per-dir; the ONE shared write was stats/test_history.json (+ a `git log` per passed test) at the end of EVERY run_tests.sh call -> new `RAE_TEST_NO_HISTORY=1` opt-out, history updated once at the end. TWO RUNNER BUGS FOUND+FIXED: (1) a name-filtered run_tests.sh bypasses the compiled-target skip list (fine for a human force-run, wrong for a driver) so the parallel runner "ran" 33 deprecated Live-only cases (18 extra passes + 15 fails) -> new `RAE_TEST_APPLY_SKIPS=1` re-applies it under a filter; (2) macOS/BSD `xargs -I` caps a substituted argument at 255 bytes ("command line cannot be assembled, too long") -> after the first few short-named cases every case read "runner died"; the name is passed as `$1` via `xargs -n 1`. THE REAL HAZARD is not the tests: the documented kill-before-run `pkill -9 -f run_tests.sh` matches a parallel run's per-case children, so any concurrent suite start kills it mid-way — "one run at a time" still holds. NOTE: another agent was editing the same script + tools/run_tests.sh concurrently during this task (it converged on the same -n 1 fix and added live per-case progress lines) and left an UNCOMMITTED `RAE_TEST_PARALLEL=1` switch in compiler/Makefile; that hunk is theirs and is deliberately NOT in my commit. Two late timing runs were taken under an external load average of 51-73 (459-503 s) and are documented as such. Write-up: docs/parallel-tests.md.

- [x] #812 {difficulty:3} [docs] Document the disjoint-field-borrow rules for worlds/structs (wishlist "Element mutation & aliasing"). Holding `mod` on two component tables of one world at once (`world.controllers` + `world.animStates`) and keeping a `mod` element ref across several calls (`updateWalkerMovement` then `groundWalker` on the same borrowed controller) WORKS today, but the rules are folklore — every system relies on them without a written spec. Write the permissive, precise story for borrowing DISJOINT fields of a struct/world (what the checker allows, what it rejects and why, how it maps to `mod`/`view` params and to `componentMod` element refs) into docs (e.g. a "Disjoint borrows" section of docs/ownership-model or the ECS reference), with worked ECS examples. Docs-only; removes uncertainty at every system. No code change expected — if writing it exposes an actual checker gap, file that separately. DONE. Wrote docs/ecs-api-reference.md §"Borrowing rules — disjoint fields and element references (#812)" (cross-linked from docs/ownership-model.md's normative parameter-mode rules and a scope note in docs/viewat-modat-aliasing.md; wishlist bullet marked documented). It is grounded in the CHECKER, not folklore: 14 compile probes, each in its OWN directory — first attempt was invalid because Rae auto-scans a project folder into one compile unit, so sibling probe files contaminated each other and a stale file's error was reported for every probe (recorded in the doc/queue so nobody repeats it). WHAT THE CHECKER ENFORCES (complete list, with the exact diagnostics): bind-once reference bindings (`let`, never `var`); no view->mod promotion / no assignment through a view; a reference cannot alias a `copyAt` copy; a reference cannot be bound to an ownership-returning call; and the ONE aliasing rule (#658): while an `if let … => list.viewAt/modAt` binding is live the SAME List may not be structurally mutated (add/set/insert/remove/swapRemove/clear/drop/grow/pop), same for a collection-loop body; a DIFFERENT List is fine. There is NO struct-field aliasing rule at all. WHAT IS PERMITTED (by design, verified accepted): two `mod` borrows of DISJOINT fields of one world at once (`world.controllers` + `world.animStates`); an element ref (`componentMod`/`queryModAt`) threaded through several calls (the 114 `updateWalkerMovement` -> `groundWalker` shape); a ref into one table plus a ref into another; and — honestly stated — the SAME field passed twice as `mod` (allowed only because nothing checks it; programmer's responsibility). Explained why disjoint field refs are sound (a field borrow never moves the struct) and how `mod T` params are borrows of the argument place. ELEMENT-REF VALIDITY RULE written precisely: a componentView/componentMod/queryViewAt/queryModAt pointer is valid until the table is STRUCTURALLY mutated — componentSet of a NEW entity (append -> realloc -> dangling) or componentRemove (swap-remove moves data under the ref); overwriting a present entity is fine. GAP FOUND + CONFIRMED AT RUNTIME: the checker does NOT cover the ECS accessors (only List viewAt/modAt) — holding a componentMod ref across 599 componentSet appends then writing 999 through it leaves the value at 1 (silent lost write / use-after-free, no diagnostic). Filed as #814 with the fix shape (extend #658 to componentMod/queryModAt vs the table mutators, plain `let =>` included) rather than fixed here, per the task. Worked 114-shaped examples (allowed + the NOT-SAFE shape with its fix). NEW positive test 739_ecs_disjoint_borrows pins the four PERMITTED shapes at runtime so a future checker tightening cannot silently break the walker (the hazardous shape is deliberately NOT pinned — it is #814's reject test). Full suite GREEN via watch-tests.sh (RAE_RUN_END exit=0: cases 399/0, examples 66/0 incl. 114). Docs + one test; no compiler code changed. [>claude]->[x].
- [x] #814 {difficulty:4} [compiler] Extend the #658 aliasing borrow check to the ECS element accessors — a CONFIRMED silent use-after-free. `componentMod`/`componentView` (by entity) and `queryModAt`/`queryViewAt` (by dense index) return a pointer into a ComponentTable's dense storage, exactly like `List.modAt`, but the #658 check only recognises `viewAt`/`modAt` on a raw List and only the List METHOD mutators (add/set/insert/remove/swapRemove/clear/drop/grow/pop), so a table-level structural mutation while such a ref is live compiles with NO diagnostic. Reproduced at runtime while writing #812 (each probe isolated in its own dir — Rae auto-scans a project dir into one unit, so do NOT put several probe files in one folder): `let p: mod Pos => componentMod(this: pos, entity: e)`, then 599 `componentSet` appends of new entities (forces realloc), then `p.x = 999` -> `componentGet(pos, e).x` is still 1: the write landed in the freed old buffer. `componentRemove` of ANOTHER entity while the ref is live is the sibling hazard (swap-remove moves data under the ref). Overwriting an already-present entity does not move storage and is fine. FIX (same shape as #658, conservative + lexical, zero runtime cost): when a `let x: view/mod T => componentMod|componentView|queryModAt|queryViewAt(this|table: <place>, …)` binding is established, treat `<place>` as the aliased table for the rest of the enclosing block (and the `if let` branch form), and reject the STRUCTURAL table mutators on that same place while the ref is live — `componentSet` (append of a new entity; if you want to allow the in-place overwrite case you cannot tell statically, so reject all componentSet on that place), `componentRemove`, `clearEntityComponents`/table clears — with a diagnostic worded for tables ("cannot mutate a ComponentTable while a componentMod/queryModAt reference aliases one of its rows"). A DIFFERENT table stays legal; passing the table as a `view` arg stays legal; passing it as a `mod` arg to an arbitrary user function is the judgment call (the #658 rule does NOT reject that for Lists either — keep parity: allow). Note plain `let … =>` bindings (not just `if let`) must be covered — that is the ECS idiom. TESTS: reject cases for componentMod+componentSet-new-entity and componentMod+componentRemove on the same table; an accept case for the same ops on a DIFFERENT table and for a ref threaded through calls (the 114 pattern, pinned by 739_ecs_disjoint_borrows); update docs/ecs-api-reference.md §"Borrowing rules" (remove the "not diagnosed today" hole) and docs/viewat-modat-aliasing.md scope note. Found while doing #812. DONE — the ECS element-reference borrow rule is enforced (compiler/src/sema.c), same shape as #658: conservative, lexical, zero runtime cost. RULE: a `let x: view/mod T => accessor(...)` binding through componentMod/componentView (by entity), queryModAt/queryViewAt (by dense index), or the queryNViewX/forEachView accessors makes the accessor's table argument the ALIASED TABLE for the rest of the enclosing block (nested blocks included; `if let` branch form for parity), and a STRUCTURAL mutation of that same table while the ref is live is rejected: componentSet (any — append vs in-place overwrite cannot be told statically), componentRemove, and clearEntityComponents through the OWNING WORLD (a place-prefix match, so `world.positions` is reached by a mutation through `world`). A different table stays legal; passing the aliased table on as an argument (view or mod) stays legal (parity with the List rule). Diagnostic: "cannot mutate a ComponentTable while a componentMod/queryModAt reference ('p') aliases one of its rows — finish with the reference first, or mutate before taking it", reported at the mutating statement. Because the #807 query loop binds its rows through queryModAt/queryViewAt, mutating a JOINED table inside a query-loop body is caught too. PRECISION FIX found on the way: the table argument is the first NAMED argument (this:/table:/tableA:), skipping a leading positional TYPE argument (`componentMod(Rect, this: tab, …)`) — the naive first-arg version matched on the type ident, giving false positives across two same-typed tables and false negatives when only one side spelled the type. TESTS: rejects 740 (componentMod + componentSet of a new entity, same table), 741 (componentView + componentRemove via a world field), 743 (query loop mutating its joined table) — expected.txt captured from the real diagnostics; accept 742 (different table, passing the table on, a query loop mutating a NON-joined table, and mutate-first-then-borrow — placed FIRST in its block, since a ref is lexically live to block end); 739/673/734 unchanged. FALLOUT: three test cases (487/534/540) bound a mod ref then mutated the same table later in one block — the lexical-liveness shape — restructured by scoping the borrow in a tiny helper function (the documented fix); output byte-identical. ONE real site in examples: 106 UiPlaybackSystem.rae refreshPlaybackIconEntity held a `view` into world.dataDependencies across the componentSet that writes it back (a safe in-place overwrite in practice, but the exact copy-out/set-back shape) — switched to a value copy via componentGet (DataDependency is POD, so free); zero lib fallout. Side finding filed as #815: in a multi-file project sema reports errors against the LAST-loaded file (merged module file_path), so this rule's first hit printed HotReloadGlue.rae:188 (a 155-line file) for a site actually in UiPlaybackSystem.rae:188 — line/col right, path wrong; pre-existing and shared by every merged-mode diagnostic. - [x] #815 {difficulty:3} [compiler, diagnostics] Sema errors in a MULTI-FILE project name the wrong file. After merge_module_graph the merged AstModule's `file_path` is only the LAST-loaded file (main.c:2113 `merged.file_path = graph->tail->module->file_path`; the comment at main.c:2175 already admits it "only remembers the LAST file"), yet every sema `diag_error(module->file_path, line, col, …)` uses it — so an error in any earlier file is reported with the right line/column but the wrong path. Concrete case from #814: the new ComponentTable borrow error at examples/106_mobile_ui/UiPlaybackSystem.rae:188 was printed as `HotReloadGlue.rae:188` (a 155-line file), which sent the fix hunt to the wrong file; the #658 List rule and every other merged-mode sema diagnostic have the same defect (single-file test cases hide it, which is why the reject tests looked right). FIX: report the statement's OWN file — decls already carry `origin_file` (stamped at merge); thread the enclosing decl's origin_file (or a per-statement file) into sema_analyze_stmt/sema_analyze_expr and use it in diag_error instead of module->file_path, falling back to module->file_path when unknown. TEST: a two-file project case where the error is in the NON-last file; expected.txt must name that file. Found while doing #814. DONE. Sema diagnostics now name the file the offending code actually lives in. FINDING: the infrastructure already existed — sema keeps a static `s_current_decl_origin` (the decl being analysed) that import/alias resolution already keys on, and a handful of diagnostics already preferred it — but 28 `diag_error(module->file_path, …)` sites (incl. the #658 List rule and the #814 table rule), 4 `const char* file = module->file_path` lookups and a `diag_report` bypassed it, and the origin was only set around FUNCTION BODIES, so a global-let initializer or type-level error had no origin at all. FIX (compiler/src/sema.c): one helper `sema_diag_file(module)` = `s_current_decl_origin ? … : module->file_path`; every diagnostic site routed through it mechanically (28 diag_error + 9 explicit ternaries normalised + 4 file lookups + 1 diag_report — `diag_error(module->file_path` no longer occurs); and `sema_analyze_decl` is wrapped so the origin is saved/set/restored around EVERY decl kind, not just function bodies. Scope semantics untouched (resolution already used the origin); fallback to the merged path only when no decl is current (specialization/backend entry points). REPRO + TEST: the "last-loaded" file follows directory scan order (unsorted, machine-dependent) — my first test placed the error in a sibling that happened to load last here and passed pre-fix by luck; the deterministic construction is that the project ENTRY is loaded first and siblings after, so the entry is never the merged tail once any sibling exists. New case 744_reject_error_in_sibling_file: the sema error is in main.rae (entry) with an innocent sibling Helper.rae; PRE-FIX it printed `Helper.rae:6:7` WITH Helper's line 6 (`type Pos { x: Int }`) as the caret snippet — a fully misleading picture; POST-FIX `main.rae:6:7` with `p.x = 1` under the caret (expected.txt captured from the real output). All plain single-file reject cases are byte-identical (there the origin equals the merged path). - [x] #816 {difficulty:3} [compiler] Module-qualified CONST access on a PROJECT-SIBLING module is broken in two ways (found while rewriting case 730 for the all-PascalCase rename; lib-module consts like `Keys.keyUp` (case 660) work, so this is specific to project modules). Repro: sibling `Pal.rae` = `const shade: Int = 7`. (1) INTERPOLATION: `log("{Pal.shade}")` emits `Pal.shade` VERBATIM into the C (`rae_ext_rae_str((Pal.shade))`) -> C error "use of undeclared identifier 'Pal'" — the interpolation path does not perform module-qualified const lookup. (2) BARE C GLOBAL / SHADOWING: `let shade: Int = Pal.shade` compiles and prints 4831870992 (garbage). Emitted C: the module const is a bare unprefixed global `static int64_t shade = ((int64_t)7LL);` and the access lowers to the bare name, so the local `shade` SELF-INITIALISES (`int64_t shade = shade;`) — any local (or any other module's const) with the same name silently collides in the C namespace. FIX: (2) emit project-module globals with their module prefix (as functions already are, `rae_<Module>_<name>`) and lower `Module.name` to that prefixed symbol; (1) route interpolation parts through the same qualified-const resolution as plain expressions (sema.c ~3105 handles `modname.name` for GLOBAL_LET; the interp walker must reach it). TESTS: a sibling-module const read via plain init AND via interpolation, with a same-named local in scope, expecting 7 both times; extend 660 with the interpolation form for a lib module. Case 730 was deliberately rewritten to use the (working) function-qualified form `Pal.shadeOf()` to avoid this hole. DONE: (1) was NOT an interpolation bug — interp parts already go through sema_analyze_expr; the real cause was that `Pal.shade` failed to RESOLVE whenever the launch dir differed from the project root (harness runs from compiler/, so the sibling's module_name is the root-relative PATH `tests/cases/…/Pal` and both sema_is_module_name + sema_find_module_global required an EXACT match; from /tmp it worked). Fix: a PROJECT decl (no lib package) also matches its module_name's file component (new sema_module_file_component_is, mirrors the shadow diagnostic's rule; lib qualifiers untouched). (2) c_backend: module globals are now emitted as `rae_g_<module>_<name>` (global_c_name; module path with / -> _) at the definition and the deferred-init site, and c_expr IDENT emission goes through ident_c_name (decl_link GLOBAL_LET, else a non-local name matching a global, own-module first) at all 5 print sites — a same-named local can no longer self-initialise. FALLOUT: the #801 `#ifndef RAE_HAS_WEBGPU` guard on lib/webgpu enum mirrors was hiding the definitions, so the 16 webgpu cases lost their symbols; replaced by a header-first form (`#ifdef RAE_HAS_WEBGPU rae_g_… = (T)(WGPU_X); #else <rae literal>`), verified all 624 lib/webgpu consts exist in the native header. That in turn exposed a real pre-existing bug (UInt64 literals > INT64_MAX saturate to INT64_MAX via strtoll const-folding, made 7 3D examples abort) -> filed #817; not needed now since native builds take the header value. TESTS: new 745_sibling_module_const (sibling const via plain init + interpolation + arithmetic with a same-named local: plain=7 interp=7 sum=14), 660 extended with `log("interp={Keys.keyUp}")` -> 82. Docs: module-namespacing.md const section. Full suite GREEN: cases 405/0, examples 66/0.
- [x] #817 {difficulty:3} [compiler] A `UInt64` integer literal above INT64_MAX is emitted SATURATED: `const WGPU_WHOLE_SIZE: UInt64 = 18446744073709551615` (lib/webgpu/WebgpuEnums.rae, also WGPU_WHOLE_MAP_SIZE, WGPU_STRLEN, WGPU_LIMIT_U64_UNDEFINED) comes out in C as `((int64_t)9223372036854775807LL)` — the const-fold path parses every integer literal with `strtoll` (sema.c ~1337 `sema_eval_const`, `.i` is an int64) and then the C emitter prints the folded value, so 2^64-1 becomes 2^63-1 and a wgpu buffer copy with that size aborts (`memory allocation of 9223372036854775807 bytes failed`). It was MASKED until #816 because the webgpu mirrors were hidden behind `#ifndef RAE_HAS_WEBGPU` (the header value won); #816 now initialises the mirrors FROM the header on native builds so the bad literal is only in the non-native fallback. FIX: carry unsigned literals through const-folding (parse with strtoull when the value does not fit int64, keep an `is_unsigned`/u64 field) and emit `%lluULL` for UInt64-typed contexts; TEST: `const big: UInt64 = 18446744073709551615` logged via a UInt64->String path equals `18446744073709551615`, plus `let x: UInt64 = 4294967295 * 4294967295` style arithmetic. Then the `#ifdef RAE_HAS_WEBGPU` header-first branch in c_backend.c (global emission) can stay as the "defer to the cheader" rule but is no longer load-bearing for correctness. DONE: three layers were wrong, all fixed. (a) sema const folding: ConstResult/Symbol carry `is_unsigned`; literals parse with strtoull (ERANGE beyond uint64 -> not foldable); integer folding runs in uint64 (no signed-overflow UB) and the result is unsigned when an operand was or when non-negative operands exceed INT64_MAX (so `const prod: UInt64 = 4294967295 * 4294967295` folds to 18446744065119617025); the rewritten literal prints %llu. (b) C emitter: an integer literal above INT64_MAX, or ANY integer literal in a UInt64-typed context, is emitted `((uint64_t)…ULL)`; the binary-op expected-type picker no longer demotes a UInt64 expectation to the literal's plain `Int` (so a local `let y: UInt64 = 4294967295 * 4294967295` multiplies in uint64). (c) runtime: new rae_ext_rae_str_u64; the `rae_ext_rae_str` _Generic, rae_str_any and the runtime_filesystem any->str path route UInt64 through it (2^64-1 printed as -1 before). The #816 `#ifdef RAE_HAS_WEBGPU` header-first branch stays as the "header is truth" rule; its Rae fallback now reads `((uint64_t)18446744073709551615ULL)`. TEST: new 746_uint64_literal (const + local, max literal, hex max, overflowing product, const division, interpolation + log(value:)). Docs: primitive-types.md "Literals are not conversions". VM path (vm_emit_expr strtoll) untouched — Live target is frozen. Full suite GREEN: cases 406/0, examples 66/0.

- [x] #819 {difficulty:1} [stdlib] DECISION (maintainer question answered): `List(T)` STAYS in core — it is the language's array type, the compiler knows it by name (sema 6 refs incl. "collection loops require a List(T)", c_backend 21, ownership 1), ComponentTable/queries/everything are built on it, 331 files use it, and "always available, bare" is exactly the test for a core feature. `StringMap(V)` / `IntMap(V)` are NOT core: 9 user files total (lib/ui/SceneLoader, SceneInstance, Ecs + 6 test cases), zero sema knowledge (backend has 9 by-name refs each for drop/any glue only), and they are the seed of a collections library that will grow (Set, Queue, RingBuffer, SparseSet, …). So: create the package `lib/collections/` — `collections/Collections.rae` (package main module; may hold only the package doc comment + shared helpers) + `collections/StringMap.rae` + `collections/IntMap.rae` (moved verbatim from core/ after #818, incl. StringMapEntry/IntMapEntry) — and do NOT put it in the prelude: a user writes `import collections/StringMap` explicitly, which is the whole point of the split (core = what the language itself needs; collections = a library you ask for). Update the 9 users with the import; check the backend by-name refs still match (they key on the TYPE name, which is unchanged — verify 370/372/416/440/501/611 pass); mangler: their `rae_ext_*`/generated symbols must not change (StringMap has none — confirm). DOCS: docs/module-namespacing.md stdlib section, a short `docs/collections.md` (what is in core vs collections and WHY: "core = types the compiler itself understands; collections = pure Rae libraries over List/Buffer"), AGENTS.md rule of thumb one-liner. Depends on #818. DONE: created `lib/collections/` — Collections.rae (package main module, doc), StringMap.rae + IntMap.rae moved verbatim from core/ (incl. StringMapEntry/IntMapEntry). List stays in core. collections is NOT in the prelude. The 9 users each got `open collections/StringMap` and/or `open collections/IntMap` (3 lib/ui: Ecs/SceneInstance/SceneLoader; 6 cases: 370/372/416/440/501/611) — `open` not bare `import` because every call site is bare (createStringMap(..), .set/.get) and `open` implies import; `import` alone only gives the qualified form `StringMap.createStringMap`. FOUND + FIXED a real overload-resolution bug the move exposed (sema.c ~865): common generic method names (`drop`, `set`, `remove`, `free`, `clear`) now exist in BOTH core/List (open) AND the collections maps (not open); a bare `drop(list)` in a unit where ANY file opens a collections map was hijacked by the not-open collections `drop` and errored "is in package collections/StringMap, which is not open here", even though core/List.drop is open and is the real target (this resolver returns NULL for it and a downstream fallback binds it — same as when collections is absent). Fix: track `open_arity_match` (an OPEN same-name+arity candidate exists) and only emit the not-open diagnostic when there is none; the genuine not-open case (import-without-open) still errors. Verified: maps have NO rae_ext_* externs of their own (only core buf/string externs, unchanged); their generated symbols now carry the collections/ module path (self-consistent, no hand-written C binds them). DOCS: new docs/collections.md (core = types the compiler understands; collections = pure-Rae over List/Buffer), module-namespacing.md prelude section, AGENTS.md core-vs-collections rule of thumb. Full suite GREEN: 407 cases, 66 examples.
