- [x] #821 {difficulty:3} [compiler] A bare call to a function that is NOT LOADED at all (its module was never imported/opened and is not in the prelude/project tree) passes sema silently on the Compiled target and only dies in C ("use of undeclared identifier rae_createEntityAllocator"): sema only has an "unknown function ... for VM call" diagnostic (vm path), and the C backend "re-resolves by name" as a known crutch (see the comments around resolve_function_overload / the #410/#415/#819 notes). Found while writing the module-granular `open` reject test: with `open ecs/Entity` only, `createEntityAllocator()` (ecs/World, never loaded) produced C, not a diagnostic; once ecs/World is merely `import`ed the proper "is in module ecs/World, which is not open here" error fires. FIX: when resolve_function_overload finds NO candidate by name at all (not even an ineligible one) for a bare call in a Compiled-target compile, emit "unknown function 'name'" with the usual did-you-mean-a-module hint, instead of returning NULL; audit the backend name-re-resolution crutch and remove whatever it was covering (each removal is a reject test). Expect fallout in UFCS/generic-method paths that today rely on late binding — measure with the full suite first. TEST: reject case for the exact shape above. DONE: resolve_function_overload (sema.c) now emits a hard error "unknown function 'name': no function by that name is loaded here — did you forget to `import`/`open` its module?" when a bare-IDENT call matches NOTHING loaded. Gate: `symbol_table_lookup(symbols, name) == NULL` (no open func, no not-open func, no type, no local — cleaner and safer than counting func candidates, and it avoids type-application false positives) AND the name is not `__`-prefixed. The `__` exemption is REQUIRED: `__buf_alloc`/`__buf_set`/`__buf_get`/`__raeVmDropCount` are backend intrinsics with no decl, called bare (e.g. examples/28_crypto_demo). When the owning module IS loaded but not open, the pre-existing `ineligible` branch still fires with the precise `open M` hint; reaching the new branch means the module was never imported/opened at all. AUDIT of the backend name-re-resolution crutch (c_call.c ~424, the receiver-base late-binding over all_decls): it is LOAD-BEARING for generic/UFCS container methods where the template IS loaded but sema's generic inference is incomplete (the #819 `drop(list)` shape) — NOT removed, removing it regresses generic methods. The only ILLEGITIMATE thing it silently covered — a name matching nothing loaded, which used to slip through to `rae_<name>(...)` and die as a C "undeclared identifier" — is now caught in sema before codegen, so the crutch no longer masks that bug. Emitted target-agnostically (both Live and Compiled); the VM path's own "unknown function for VM call" is simply preempted. TEST: new reject case 751_reject_unknown_function (bare `createEntityAllocator()` with only `open ecs/Entity`, World never loaded). Full suite GREEN: 411 cases, 66 examples; the exact task repro now errors, example 28 (bare __buf_*) still runs.

NUANCE 2 verified: the src `type RenderSystem`/`InputSystem` live in their same-named modules and resolve to the LOCAL type — the opened lib modules ui/renderSystem/RenderSystem + ui/inputSystem/InputSystem define no clashing type (Gpu2dUi/UiInput), and lib legacyRaylib InputSystem is never loaded by 114; confirmed by a clean emit-c + gcc-link + headless run (netOk=true physicsOk=true, 853 entities, shadows). Function parameter names (cam/io/render…) deliberately left as-is — out of scope and would ripple every named-arg call for no gain. Full suite GREEN: 411 cases, 66 examples (114 example gate passes).

- [x] #843 {difficulty:4} [renderer/water 0b] transparentForward — the GPU pass (needs on-hardware visual iteration). A WGSL render pass into gb_lit_view with ALPHA BLENDING (src-alpha / one-minus-src-alpha) and READ-ONLY depth test (transparents test scene depth, do not write it), drawing instanced transparent geometry. New blend pipeline built via makeFullscreenPipeline-style bindings / the instanced path; new rae_gb_* externs (+ allowlist). NO forward-into-lit precedent exists (all geometry today writes the G-buffer; only fullscreen passes touch gb_lit), so this is genuinely new infra. VERIFY: headless screenshot is non-blank only — this pass needs a human/on-hardware visual check of the blend, depth occlusion and ordering; say so, do not trust the non-blank gate alone. Depends on #842. DONE. lib/TransparentForward.rae + lib/transparentForward.wgsl: a blend pipeline (colour src-alpha/one-minus-src-alpha, alpha one/one-minus-src-alpha; depth Depth32Float compare Greater = reverse-Z with depthWriteEnabled OFF; cull none; ONE colour target in the HDR format lighting actually wrote, read at pipeline-build time via new rae_gb_lit_format because rg11b10 vs rgba16f is chosen at runtime) and the draw: a render pass that LOADS gb_lit_view (opaque radiance kept) with the G-buffer depth attached depthReadOnly=1 (load/store Undefined, as WebGPU requires), issuing one instanced DrawIndexed through the shared gbIssueInstanced tail. NO new draw infra: it reuses the geometry pass's Frame uniform + DrawU draws storage + vertex layout (pos/nrm/uv, stride 32) and the per-frame draw cursor (which resets only at Gbuffer.begin, so records land after the geometry pass's and never overlap); the shader applies the TAA jitter LAST like the G-buffer vs (#397) so transparents rasterise in the same sub-pixel frame as the depth they test. Unlit on purpose (colour*alpha); shading is the client's (#830 water, #844 particles). Client API: addTransparentInstance(cache, mesh, transform, r, g, b, alpha) — alpha rides in the DrawU metallic slot (unused by an unlit blend); one batch/one mesh per frame (cap 256, zero per-frame alloc), the pass draws then resets it. Pipeline + bind built in Rae over the bindings and parked in C handle slots exactly like the AO/composite passes (rae_gb_transparent_pipeline/bind + setters; released in deferredShutdown with the siblings) — 5 rae_gb_* ABI accessors, all documented in the allowlist; C-surface gate green (16 symbols). RendererDeferred: RenderPasses.transparent cache; the transparentForward tag now runs copyOpaqueRadiance (#842) THEN the draw. ON-HARDWARE VISUAL PROOF, as the task demands (the non-blank gate was NOT trusted): a temporary, UNCOMMITTED 114 patch queued 12 half-alpha cubes/frame near the origin; screenshot (/tmp/walker843.png) shows them clearly translucent (walker/rock/sea/snow show through blue-tinted), CUT OFF by the opaque tree in front and CLIPPED flat where they sink into the terrain (read-only depth test works), overlapping cubes blending rather than occluding (depth not written — correct), tonemapped consistently (they land before TAA/composite); zero wgpu validation errors. REAL BUG FOUND+FIXED on the way: the first run drew NOTHING because gbMeshReady() is 0 whenever no geometry pass is active (it answers "may I draw into the CURRENT pass", cleared by Gbuffer.end) — an outside-pass draw must test gbMeshIcount(mesh) > 0 instead; documented in the module + design doc so #844/#845 don't hit it. allowlisted — Rae has no module-level Ptr), steps wave time, and draws ONE indexed draw (NO compute) per body through the #843 transparent-pass shape: a render pass that LOADS gb_lit, alpha-blends, tests the scene depth READ-ONLY, with that same depth ALSO bound as texture_depth_2d so the fragment gets the water depth under every pixel; waterSurface.wgsl — composed after lib/noise.wgsl: Gerstner vertex displacement (GPU Gems normal), TAA jitter applied last; the Roystan toon fragment: sceneDepth - surfaceDepth via the reverse-Z linearisation near*far/(ndc*(far-near)+near) (no matrix inverse needed), shallow->deep depth-gradient colour with alpha rising with depth, second-octave noise distortion, posterised ripples with a cutoff that is 0 at the shore (all foam) and rippleCutoff in deep water (flecks), Fresnel sky tint from the stylised hemisphere (horizon->zenith by reflect(v,n).z — the same thing Sky.radiance does on the CPU), two-band toon diffuse and a STEPPED Blinn-Phong sun highlight (sunDir points FROM the sun, like the lighting shader), refraction OFF. 114: `waterSystem` App resource created as createLakeToon at the biome water plane (lakeSurfaceZ = worldWaterZ(groundZ) — the plane the splashes already spray from; - [x] #831 {difficulty:5} [water 2/fft] Realistic ocean surface — FFT compute cascades (desktop tier). New graph node `waterFft` (compute) BEFORE `transparentForward`, built exactly like the grass compute node (lib/GrassCompute.rae + grass_compute.wgsl pattern): `waterFft.wgsl` = TMA/JONSWAP-style spectrum with directional (Hasselmann) spreading -> per-frame frequency-domain evolution -> STOCKHAM inverse FFT (no bit-reversal; butterfly texture computed once per resolution; row pass + transpose + reuse for columns) -> displacement (x,y,z) + normal + Jacobian-foam maps; 3 CASCADES with independent tile sizes (~250 m / 60 m / 8 m) summed in the surface shader; an UPDATE-RATE knob with load balancing (only one cascade updates per frame when the frame is short). Camera-centred clip-map / projected-grid mesh so vertex density follows the camera (Crest's LOD idea). Whitecaps from negative Jacobian; sea spray by spawning lib/Particles.rae where Jacobian < 0. Quality tiers: FFT size 128²/256²/512², cascade count, update rate. Depends on #830 (same WaterBody/WaterSystem; the realistic path EXTENDS the toon path, it does not replace it). References: docs/water-rendering-design.md §3.2; 2Retr0/GodotOceanWaves + siliconjungle/inkwell-webgpu-water (technique only — GodotOceanWaves states no license, do NOT copy code). BLOCKED — dependency #830 is NOT done: no lib/water/ package, no WaterBody/WaterSystem, no toon surface shader exists (checked the tree and git log; #830 is still [ ]). This task was dispatched out of dependency order — run #830 FIRST, then re-run this one (and #832/#833 are blocked the same way). No code written: the realistic path EXTENDS the toon path, so building FFT cascades now would have nothing to sum them into and nothing verifiable. What IS ready for it: the #842-#845 transparentForward chain (pass, particles, isolated case) and GrassCompute as the compute-node template. PLAN for when #830 lands, in order, each a gate-checkable slice: (1) `waterFft` compute NODE + resources (3 cascade texture sets: displacement rgb, normal, Jacobian/foam) declared in RendererDeferred before transparentForward, like the grass node — no math yet, just allocation + graph order (tests 572/580 gain a pass); (2) spectrum + butterfly: TMA/JONSWAP with Hasselmann spreading into a per-cascade h0 texture (once per resolution/params) + the Stockham butterfly texture (once per FFT size) — verify by a low-res readback (the grass diagnostics path) that h0 is non-zero and symmetric; (3) per-frame evolution + Stockham iFFT (row pass, transpose, reuse for columns; no bit reversal) producing displacement/normal/Jacobian — verify by iFFT of a KNOWN single-frequency spectrum and reading back one expected sine (the only honest FFT correctness check); (4) the surface: extend #830's WaterSystem shader to sample+sum the 3 cascades (tile ~250/60/8 m) on the camera-centred clip-map mesh, whitecaps from Jacobian < 0 — screenshot gate; (5) update-rate knob with load balancing (one cascade per short frame) + tiers (128²/256²/512², cascade count) + sea spray via lib/Particles.rae where Jacobian < 0. Each slice is its own turn; (2)/(3) need on-hardware iteration. References: docs/water-rendering-design.md §3.2; GodotOceanWaves / inkwell-webgpu-water are TECHNIQUE references only (GodotOceanWaves states no license — do NOT copy code). SLICE 1 DONE, rest split into #849-#852 (in dependency order). Landed: RenderTag.waterFft + a passCompute "waterFft" node in RendererDeferred that WRITES three new PERSISTENT fixed-size resources waterCascade0/1/2 (persistent because the update-rate knob refreshes one cascade per frame and the others must survive) which transparentForward READS — so the graph DERIVES waterFft -> transparentForward (it sorts to the very front, before shadow, since it depends on nothing in the frame); 572/580 expected regenerated after reviewing the order (10 passes / 14 resources; every derived check still yes). WaterBody gained the realistic tier as data: fftSize (128/256/512), cascadeCount, cascadeTile0..2 (250/60/8 m), updateRate; createLakeToon defaults 256/3/1. WaterSystem.waterFftStep (called by 114 at the new tag) allocates the cascade maps once for the body's tier — cascadeCount x {displacement, normal, jacobian} rgba16float textures with StorageBinding+TextureBinding + views — over the bindings, parked in the water C slot store (widened 4 -> 32 slots; layout owned and released by Rae in waterShutdown); no compute yet, the toon surface does not read them. renderDeferredPassWorld no-ops the tag (an app without water has nothing to do). - [x] #832 {difficulty:4} [water 2/shading] Realistic water shading + gameplay query. On the #831 surface: PBR microfacet + Schlick FRESNEL between reflection and refraction; reflection = `sky.radiance(reflect)` always + SSR (screen-space reflection from the G-buffer depth/normal) on the desktop tier — NO planar reflection (a second scene render is the one thing tiled/mobile GPUs cannot afford; SSR + sky covers it, Boat Attack made the same call); refraction = lit-scene-colour read (the #842-#845 hdrColor copy) offset by the normal, MASKED by depth so objects above the surface do not bleed, with Beer-Lambert absorption `exp(-depth * absorbColour)`; TAA (opt-in) interaction checked. Gameplay: `waterHeightAt(x, y, t)` — CPU Gerstner mirror (exact for Gerstner, approximate for FFT) and/or a low-res displacement readback every N frames; BUOYANCY as an ECS query (`loop let entity, transform: mod Transform3D, buoyant: view Buoyant in query2(...)` sets Z), never a callback. Screenshot cases per tier. Depends on #831. Reference: docs/water-rendering-design.md §3.2 + §4 (Crest's query API shape; UE5 Water plugin data model — design only, EULA). STATUS (#848): #830 and #831 slice 1 have LANDED, so this is RUNNABLE NOW on the toon/Gerstner surface — Fresnel-blended sky reflection, refraction from the #842 litCopy offset by the normal and MASKED by depth, Beer-Lambert absorption, the TAA on/off check, waterHeightAt as a CPU Gerstner mirror, and buoyancy as the #807 query2 loop with an ECS test. Note from #830: the opaque snapshot is currently taken AFTER the water (fine while refraction is off) — split transparentPass so the snapshot precedes waterDraw (two lines) before reading litCopy. SSR and the FFT displacement readback need the realistic surface and are split out to #854. Slices, each a screenshot gate: (1) reflection + refraction + absorption; (2) TAA check; (3) waterHeightAt; (4) buoyancy query + test. DONE — all four slices on the toon/Gerstner surface (the SSR + FFT-readback items are #854 as planned). (1) Reflection + refraction + absorption: waterSurface.wgsl now weights the stylised-sky reflection by Schlick Fresnel (F0 = 0.02, replacing the ad-hoc pow^4); with WaterBody.refraction = 1 (new field, default 0 = mobile alpha path; refractStrength, absorbColor per-channel 1/m) the fragment reads the #842 opaque snapshot (litCopy bound as texture_2d + a linear clamped sampler, bindings 3/4, generation-keyed) at the screen uv offset by the normal, DEPTH-MASKED (the refracted pixel's depth is compared to the surface; if it is closer the pixel is an object above the water and the shader falls back to the unrefracted uv — the Boat Attack/Crest trick), then Beer-Lambert exp(-refrDepth * absorb) with the absorbed light replaced by scattered deep colour; a refracting body composes its own background so it is drawn OPAQUE (alpha 1), foam and the stepped highlight on top. Uniform grew to 16 vec4 (refract on/strength/lit size, absorb). The #830 ordering note is resolved: TransparentForward gained transparentSnapshot(cache) so an app takes the opaque copy BEFORE waterDraw (114 and 118 do; transparentPass still snapshots itself for apps without water, via a per-frame snapshotDone flag). (2) TAA check: 118 has RAE_WATER_TAA=1 (gbSetTaaEnabled); the TAA-on and FXAA frames are indistinguishable — no smear or ghost — because the surface applies the frame jitter LAST like the G-buffer; caveat: the deterministic frame holds the camera still, so this is the static case. (3) waterHeightAt(body, x, y, t) in Water.rae: the CPU mirror of the WGSL gerstner (same constants), honouring waveCount, with the horizontal Gerstner displacement inverted by two fixed-point iterations of p = (x,y) - dispXY(p); unit case 752_water_height pins flat = still level, crest/trough/period of a pure sine, travel with speed, and steep two-wave bounds. (4) Buoyancy as a QUERY: lib/water/Buoyancy.rae — Buoyant { offset } + buoyancySystem(transforms, buoyants, body, time) = the #807 query2 loop over Transform3d + Buoyant setting Z = waterHeightAt + offset, never a callback; unit case 753_water_buoyancy pins boat-on-crest+offset, untouched rock, buoy-in-trough, query == system. 118 enables refraction (RAE_WATER_REFRACTION=0 shows the mobile path); 114 stays on the alpha tier. Verified: 752/753 PASS; gates 114 + 118 PASS; zero validation errors in both the refraction and TAA runs; screenshot by eye: the bed 6 m down absorbed to blue-green, lighter shallows at the islands, island tops crisp with no bleed into the water. docs/water-rendering-design.md status updated. Not done here (by design): PBR microfacet (GGX) specular belongs to the realistic surface (#851), SSR and the FFT readback are #854.
- [x] #833 {difficulty:4} [water 3/rivers+underwater] Rivers: a `RiverSpline` component whose system BAKES a river mesh + FLOW MAP + foam map from a bezier spline once (Arnklit/Waterways approach, MIT — technique reference), then it is just another `WaterBody` with flow-map-driven UV animation; carves/blends against the terrain heightfield (terrainHeightAt) at the banks. Underwater: a post step (fog/absorption tint + optional caustics) when the camera is below a WaterBody surface. Fits the biome/terrain work (docs/biome-terrain-system.md). Depends on #830 (toon rivers work without #831). Reference: docs/water-rendering-design.md §5 Phase 3. STATUS (#848): #830 has LANDED (lib/water WaterBody/WaterSystem + the toon surface), so this is RUNNABLE NOW; toon rivers do not need the FFT. In place for it: terrainHeightAt (banks), the #843 transparent pass + #842 litCopy (underwater tint reads the lit frame), lib/Particles.rae for spray. Slices, each a screenshot gate: (1) RiverSpline component + one-time river MESH bake along the spline (Waterways technique, MIT — technique only), bank verts snapped to terrainHeightAt with a blend margin; (2) FLOW MAP + foam map baked from spline tangents/curvature; (3) the result as a WaterBody with flow-map-advected UVs (two-phase to hide the reset) and baked foam; (4) underwater: camera below the surface -> fullscreen Beer-Lambert tint/fog after lighting, optional sun caustics. DONE — slices 1-3 as one integrated river feature; slice 4 (underwater) filed as #855. lib/water/River.rae: RiverSpline (cubic bezier, bezierPoint/bezierTangent) and bakeRiverMesh(spline, width, samples, across, heights, bankMargin) -> MeshData, ONCE: `samples` rows along the flow x `across` quads across; uv = (arc length, offset across) in METRES so the ripple noise keeps the lake's scale; the normal attribute — which the water shader ignores because it builds its own — packs (flow tangent xy, curvature foam weight = tangent turn per metre x width, 0..1). Banks: the two edge rows take caller-supplied heights (3 per sample: left bank, surface, right bank) and a bank BELOW the surface tucks the edge to bank - margin so it hides under the ground; a bank above simply lets the flat surface run into it (depth test + shoreline foam). The heights are DATA the caller samples from its own heightfield — Rae has no callbacks and the bake needs none. Flow map + foam map (slice 2) are baked INTO THE MESH rather than textures: on a spline-parametrised ribbon the u coordinate IS the flow map, so the shader advects the ripple noise along u at flowSpeed with no texture and no phase reset to hide (the two-phase trick exists for texture flow maps on arbitrary meshes; not needed here). WaterBody gained meshId (0 = shared unit grid; >0 = its own world-space mesh) and flowSpeed; WaterSystem draws the body's mesh; the WGSL has a river mode (tuning.z): base = p, no Gerstner, noise coordinates (u - flowSpeed*t, v), baked foam from n.z streaked by the ripple noise and max-ed into the foam term. createRiverToon(mesh, flowSpeed) sets river defaults (waveCount 0, depthMax 0.6, foamDistance 0.25, absorb 0.95). 114: examples/114_walker_character/River.rae bakes a 48-sample, 4 m wide stream across the meadow near the spawn with banks/surface from terrainHeightAt (surface = ground + 0.35 so there is a channel), added via waterAddBody after createApp; the 114 gate now asserts "[water] toon lake: 2 bodies". Unit case 754_river_bake pins vertex/index counts, arc-length u, across v, bank tuck-under vs stay-at-surface, tangent packing, zero foam on a straight run, foam on a bend, unit tangents. Verified: 754 PASS; 114 gate PASS; emit/link clean; zero validation errors. VISUAL: the first tuned frame showed NO river — a 12 cm-deep toon river over white snow is all foam (the shoreline cutoff is ~0 at that depth): white on white. A temporary opaque-magenta diagnostic (reverted, 0 leftovers) proved the ribbon snakes exactly where intended across the meadow; the final tune (surface +0.35, saturated palette, foam kept to the banks) gives a light-cyan stream with foam flecks along that path — pale against snow by nature (Fresnel pulls it toward the bright sky at grazing angles); on darker terrain it reads stronger. Two process trips recorded: an edit script died on a stale anchor after writing River.rae, and an EMPTY TEST= name launched the whole suite — the single-case invocation is now guarded on a non-empty name. docs/water-rendering-design.md status updated.
- [x] #849 {difficulty:4} [water 2/fft slice 2] Spectrum + butterfly for the FFT ocean (#831 decomposition; slice 1 — the waterFft graph node, cascade resources/textures and WaterBody tier fields — is DONE). In lib/water/waterFft.wgsl (composed like GrassCompute): a compute pass that fills a per-cascade h0 spectrum texture from a TMA/JONSWAP-style spectrum with directional (Hasselmann) spreading (wind speed/direction, fetch, cascade tile size -> k grid), run ONCE per resolution/params change, plus the Stockham butterfly texture (twiddles + read indices per stage, no bit-reversal) once per FFT size. Verify by a low-res READBACK (the grass diagnostics path): h0 is non-zero, Hermitian-symmetric, and its energy peaks at the JONSWAP peak frequency. Technique only from GodotOceanWaves / inkwell-webgpu-water — do NOT copy code (no license). DONE. lib/water/waterFft.wgsl (from the Tessendorf / Hasselmann / Bouws papers — no code copied): `spectrum` fills a cascade's h0 rgba32float storage texture: k = 2pi/L (x - N/2, y - N/2); JONSWAP S(w) (fetch-limited: w_p = 22 (g^2/(U F))^(1/3), alpha = 0.076 (U^2/(F g))^0.22, gamma 3.3, sigma 0.07/0.09) x the TMA finite-depth factor x Hasselmann 1980 spreading D = N(s) cos^(2s)(theta/2) with s(w/w_p) and the normalisation 2^(2s-1)/pi * G(s+1)^2 / G(2s+1) via a Stirling ln-Gamma; times the Jacobian (dw/dk)/k with w^2 = g k tanh(kh); amplitude sqrt(2E) dk; complex Gaussian from a PCG hash + Box-Muller seeded per (cascade, texel) so a texel is deterministic; k = 0 and the Nyquist row/column zeroed (their -k is themselves); packed (h0(k), h0(-k)) per texel. `butterfly` fills the per-output radix-2 Stockham table (log2N columns x N rows): stage s, span Ns: block = i/2Ns, k = i mod 2Ns, reads j = block*Ns + (k mod Ns) and j + N/2, twiddle exp(+2pi i (k mod Ns)/2Ns) with the sign folded for k >= Ns — inverse, autosort, no bit reversal. WaterSystem: ensureFftGpu builds two compute pipelines from the one module (each entry point gets its OWN pipeline + bind group — wgpu derives a layout from the bindings an entry point uses), a 3-vec4 FftU uniform, the butterfly texture, per-cascade h0 textures, a MapRead staging buffer; bakeFftSpectrum runs ONCE per params (waterFftInvalidate re-bakes), one uniform upload + one submitted dispatch per cascade, then the butterfly. Cascade bands: kLow/kHigh per cascade so the summed cascades never count a wavelength twice; hand-over at kHigh = cascadeBandBins(6) * 2pi/L_next — the readback showed the naive 2pi/L_next boundary gave the 250 m cascade a 4-cell (56-texel) disc of its 256x256 grid. WaterBody carries the sea state: windSpeed 9, windDirRad 0.7, fetch 250 km, waterDepth 50 (=> k_p 0.0603 rad/m, a 104 m peak wavelength). VERIFICATION by READBACK (the grass map-read idiom; blocking, diagnostics only): readbackFloat4 copies an rgba32float texture to the staging buffer with bytesPerRow padded to the 256-byte COPY_BYTES_PER_ROW_ALIGNMENT (the 8-wide butterfly needs it — the first run panicked exactly there) and maps it into a List(Float); waterFftDiagnose checks (a) non-zero texels match the band's k-space disc pi (kHigh/dk)^2 within 0.6..1.3x, (b) EXACT (k,-k) packing symmetry — texel(k).zw == texel(-k).xy, measured 0, (c) the radial energy peak bin vs the JONSWAP k_p bin — measured peak bin 2 vs expected 2.40 (within 2), (d) butterfly stage 0 reads (i/2, i/2 + N/2) with twiddle +-1 — true. 118 runs the diagnose under RAE_WATER_FFT_DIAG=1 at its second water frame and its gate now asserts "[water fft] h0 ok"; PASS. C: the water handle store widened to 64 slots (32.. = FFT state; C-surface gate ok). Verified: make build clean; gates 114 (bake runs there too) + 118 PASS. docs/water-rendering-design.md updated. Next: #850 evolution + Stockham iFFT (verify by iFFT of a KNOWN spectrum), then #851/#852/#854.
- [x] #850 {difficulty:4} [water 2/fft slice 3] Per-frame evolution + Stockham inverse FFT (#831 decomposition; needs #849). Compute passes: evolve h0 -> h(k,t) (dispersion w = sqrt(g k), plus the x/y choppiness and slope terms packed as extra complex channels), then the Stockham iFFT: row pass over the stages reading the butterfly texture, transpose, reuse the same row pass for columns; write displacement (x,y,z), normal and the Jacobian/foam map into the #831 cascade textures (rgba16float storage). VERIFY THE FFT HONESTLY: seed a KNOWN single-frequency spectrum, run the iFFT, read back one row and assert the expected sine (amplitude + period) — the only real correctness check; then the JONSWAP field for non-NaN, bounded displacement. Needs on-hardware iteration. DONE. lib/water/waterFft.wgsl gained the per-frame path: `evolve` — h(k,t) = h0(k) e^(iwt) + conj(h0(-k)) e^(-iwt) with w^2 = g k tanh(kh), then the field pair this dispatch packs as Z = A + iB: (h, Dx), (Dy, dh/dx), (dh/dy, dDx/dx), (dDy/dy, dDx/dy) with Dx = -i (kx/k) h etc. — TWO real fields per complex transform, valid because every field's spectrum is Hermitian (h(-k) = conj h(k)), so the inverse transform's real part is A and its imaginary part B; `stockham` — one radix-2 stage along x for every row, reading (twiddle, a, b) from the #849 butterfly column `stage`; `transpose`; the same stage shader for the columns; `fieldOut` un-transposes into the per-field result; `post` — undo the centred spectrum's (-1)^(x+y), chop by WaterBody.choppiness (new field, 1.2), normal from the slopes, Jacobian J = (1 + l dDx/dx)(1 + l dDy/dy) - l^2 dDx/dy^2, foam = clamp(1 - J) — into the #831 cascade maps (rgba16float storage). WaterFft.waterFftUpdate encodes ALL of it as ONE compute pass in ONE submit (dispatches in a pass see earlier storage writes): per cascade x field: evolve -> P, log2N stages ping-ponging P/Q, transpose, log2N stages, fieldOut -> R_f; then post; ~230 dispatches/frame for 3 x 256^2. Sources are bound as texture_2d<f32> (core textureLoad), only destinations as storage textures (no non-core storage reads); per-(cascade, field) evolve uniforms and per-stage uniforms because a single submit forbids rewriting one buffer between dispatches. Runs every frame only for body.style = realistic (toon bodies never read the maps). ARCHITECTURE: WaterSystem.rae crossed the 1000-line hard cap (the compiler enforces it) — the whole FFT (cascades, bake, frame, diagnostics, releases, waterShutdown) moved to lib/water/WaterFft.rae (765 lines; WaterSystem.rae 331); callers open water/WaterFft. C handle store widened to 160 slots. THE HONEST FFT TEST (waterFftDiagnoseIfft, run by 118 under RAE_WATER_FFT_DIAG=1 and asserted by its gate as "[water fft] ifft ok"): `spectrum` has a test mode that seeds ONE known wave (h0 = 0.25 at k index +-(3, 0), so h = 0.5 at both at t = 0) whose unnormalised inverse transform must be exactly cos(2pi 3x/N) of amplitude 1 — read back row N/2 of the height field, sign-corrected by (-1)^(x+y): MEASURED amplitude 1 (expect 1), value at x = 0 = 1 (expect 1), zero crossings 6 (expect 6), finite; then JONSWAP re-baked and transformed at t = 1: finite, height max 0.084 m (a 9 m/s sea on the 250 m tile). Both gates 114 + 118 PASS; zero validation errors. ON-HARDWARE FINDINGS (this slice needed the iteration the task predicted): (1) the first transform came back NaN at every texel; a probe that parked the uniform in a zeroed texel proved the uniform correct, and the NaN pattern (finite only in a 35-cell disc = k·h < 44) identified the GPU's exp-based tanh overflowing f32 past ~44 (k·depth reaches 160 on the 8 m tile at 50 m) — clamped to 20 in both evolve and the spectrum, where it was silently poisoning the 8 m cascade the h0 diagnostic never inspected; (2) a Rae function returning a c_struct by value emits an undefined `rae_WGPUBindGroupEntry` C type — filed as #856; the two helpers were inlined; (3) two syntax slips of my own (a `loop` step form, a local named `view`). docs/water-rendering-design.md updated. Next: #851 the realistic surface sampling + summing the cascades on a clip-map with whitecaps, then #852, #854.
- [x] #851 {difficulty:4} [water 2/fft slice 4] The realistic surface (#831 decomposition; needs #850). Extend lib/water/waterSurface.wgsl + WaterSystem: when body.style is realistic, sample and SUM the 3 cascades (tiles ~250/60/8 m) for displacement in the vertex shader and normal + Jacobian in the fragment; whitecaps where Jacobian < 0 (replace the toon foam term on this path; keep the shoreline depth foam); a camera-centred clip-map / projected-grid mesh so vertex density follows the camera (Crest's LOD idea) instead of the fixed 96x96 unit grid. Screenshot gate (an isolated example like 117, camera low over the ocean) + visual check. DONE. SURFACE: waterSurface.wgsl gained the realistic path (WaterStyle.realistic, uniform cascades.w): the vertex shader sums the three cascades' displacement sampled at world xy / tile (textureSampleLevel; cascade count from the uniform), skipping Gerstner; the fragment samples the cascades' normal maps and adds their SLOPES (-n.xy/n.z) into one normal, and turns the Jacobian channel into whitecaps with foam = clamp((0.5 - J) * 2): J = 1 is flat, J -> 0 is the surface folding, so the foam sits on the crests (the first pass flagged J < 1 and foamed everywhere — tuned by eye); the shoreline depth foam stays; shading is a smooth diffuse and a sharp smooth Blinn-Phong glint instead of the toon steps; refraction/Fresnel/absorption unchanged. The six cascade maps (displacement+J, normal per cascade) are bound at 5..10 on the surface bind group — the toon path binds them too (one layout) but never samples — and the bind is deferred until waterFftStep has allocated them; the cascade slot constants moved to WaterSystem.rae so it can bind them without a circular open. HARDWARE FINDING: the first ocean frame was hard-edged blocks and long streak bands — the cascades were sampled through the lit sampler (ClampToEdge, right for refraction) while world.xy / tile runs far outside [0,1]; a REPEAT sampler (binding 11, slot 4) fixed it in one step. MESH: lib/water/ClipMap.rae createRadialGridMesh(rings, segments, firstCell, growth) — concentric rings whose spacing grows geometrically (64 x 96, 1 m, 1.09 reaches ~2.5 km with ~6k vertices), Crest's LOD idea as ONE draw: WaterBody.meshMode 2 makes the vertex shader add the camera position snapped to whole metres (uniform centre.xy) to every vertex each frame, and because the cascades are looked up in WORLD space the waves stay put while the grid rides under the camera. meshMode replaces the meshId>0 river test (0 unit grid, 1 baked world mesh, 2 camera-centred); createOceanRealistic(surfaceZ) builds the body (ocean kind, realistic, refraction on, deep-sea palette, depthMax 12, glint power 180). EXAMPLE: 119_ocean_fft (+ raepack, "3D Renderer") — the ocean over a 40 m-deep bed, the shared CameraRig in free mode, comma/period time of day, deterministic test frame freezes the sea at t = 2 s; gate in run_examples asserts the "[ocean] deterministic deferred frame rendered: ... 3 cascades of 256, N ocean frames" line + "[water fft] spectrum baked" + non-blank. Verified: gates 114 + 118 + 119 PASS (114/118 exercise the toon path with the new bindings), zero validation errors; by eye (/tmp/ocean851c.png): long swells from the 250 m cascade, chop from the finer ones, whitecaps streaked along the crests, deep-blue troughs, Fresnel sky at grazing angles, a clean far horizon, no seams. docs/water-rendering-design.md updated. Next: #852 update-rate balancing + tiers + spray, #854 SSR + FFT readback.

- [x] #884 {difficulty:4} [compiler/ownership] A produced value passed straight to a `view`/`mod` parameter is never released — legal code that silently leaks. VERIFIED example (compiles and runs): given `func create(id: view Int) ret Slot`, `func drop(this: mod Slot)`, and `func inspect(slot: view Slot) ret Int { ret slot.id }`, the line `let seen: Int = inspect(slot: create(id: 1))` logs `acquire 1` then `seen 1` but NEVER `release 1`, while `let kept: Slot = create(id: 2)` followed by `inspect(slot: kept)` DOES log `release 2` at scope exit. The `create(id: 1)` result is an unnamed temporary that no binding owns, so no drop is emitted for it; the same holds for any heap-owning temporary (a `String`/`List`-returning call passed to `view`) today, not just destructor types. FIX: drop such temporaries at the end of the statement — the string-pool mark/flush already brackets expression statements (`{ int __rae_spm = rae_string_pool_mark(); ...; rae_string_pool_flush(__rae_spm); }`); bracket struct temporaries that have a destructor or own heap the same way, dropped after the statement's value is consumed. Test with a fake-resource counter asserting acquire count == release count. — DONE: every statement is emitted through a buffer; a produced value borrowed by a `view` parameter that owns heap or has a destructor becomes a named statement temporary (declared before the statement, dropped after it; a `ret` drops its temporaries before the locals). Temporaries follow the local rule: struct literal = full drop, call result = alias drop (destructors and containers run, String fields skipped — the first cut double-freed JsonValue aliases in the glTF loaders). Test 768 (fake-resource counter: expr stmt, let, assign, if condition, ret, generic callee, produced list); 766 now shows the release. Suite 428/0, examples 70/0. Loop-condition temporaries and break/continue out of an if are #886.
- [x] #885 {difficulty:4} [compiler/ownership] `create` gaps after #880: (a) a `create(...)` in a literal field of a GENERIC struct whose field type is still `T` is not resolved (the field type comes from the template, not the instance); (b) `create` as an operand with no expected type (`log(create(...))`, binary operators) is only caught at statement level and in `Any` parameters; (c) sema-level use-after-drop is a lexical scan of one statement list, not dataflow across branches. Decide and implement. — DONE: (a) generic-instance literal fields substitute the instance's type arguments before choosing the constructor, nested bare literals included; (b) a post-pass over every function body reports any `create` no position bound, without double-reporting ones the resolver already rejected — it also exposed that arguments of generic container methods (`list.add(value: create(...))`) and of generic calls with a positional type argument (`pick(Slot, value: create(...))`) were never typed by sema at all, now bound from the callee's type arguments; (c) use-after-drop is a dataflow over the whole body (branches merge by union, loop bodies walked twice, let/assign revive, drop-of-dropped is a use) paired with a runtime live flag per explicitly dropped local so a drop in one branch releases exactly once on every path. Tests 769/770/771 new, 767 extended, 763 wording; suite 431/0, examples 70/0.
- [x] #886 {difficulty:4} [compiler/ownership] Temporaries made in a `loop` CONDITION are released once, after the loop, not per iteration (#884 follow-up; verified: `loop inspect(slot: create(id: i)) < 2 { i = i + 1 }` logs acquire 0/1/2 but only release 2). Also a `break`/`continue` out of an `if` whose condition produced a temporary skips that temporary's drop. Fix: drop the condition's statement temporaries at the top of each iteration body / before the re-test, and on the non-local exit paths (the loop_body_local_start machinery already runs local drops there). Test with the fake-resource counter from 768. — DONE: every statement temporary carries a `_set` flag raised by the producing expression and consumed by its guarded drop, so a drop can be written at several points safely; the loop emitter drops the loop's own condition temporaries at the end of each iteration, break/continue drop every enclosing statement's temporaries up to and including the loop's (new parent link between statement temporaries), and a short-circuited condition that never produced its value never drops one. Test 772 (loop condition, break and continue through an if condition, short-circuit and/or, nested loops: every acquire has one release); suite 432/0, examples 70/0. No known limits remain open against the lifecycle design.
- [x] #890 {difficulty:3} [lifecycle/adoption] Give every collection one constructor verb `create` with the decided syntax: `List.create(EntityId, cap: N)`, `StringMap.create(Int, cap: 8)`, `IntMap.create(Int, cap: 8)`, `ComponentTable.create(Rect)` — a plain (unparameterised) type qualifier plus the element/value type as the first positional argument (the old `createList(EntityId, cap: N)` with `List` moved out of the name). Inference-free, so it works in `ret`, inside generic functions and as an argument. Steps: (1) compiler — extend the `Type.create(...)` resolution (#880) to accept an unparameterised qualifier and take the type param from the first positional argument, disambiguating List/StringMap/IntMap by the qualifier (they share the `(<type>, cap)` shape); (2) rename the four constructors to `create`, delete the #880 twins; (3) switch the three Compiled collection-literal hooks (c_discovery.c ~219, c_stmt.c ~1739, c_expr.c ~1254) from name=="createList" to name=="create" disambiguated by a `List` return type (deprecated VM lookups stay); (4) sweep the ~1000 call sites `createX(T, args)` -> `X.create(T, args)`; (5) update tests 647 and 562. Full suite and example gate green per commit. Does not depend on #889. — DONE: `List.create(EntityId, cap: N)` (and StringMap/IntMap/ComponentTable) implemented as a pre-sema AST desugar to the internal per-type constructor `createList(...)`, run over EVERY function body incl. generic templates (which sema does not analyse and clones verbatim per specialisation), so the backend's positional-type-arg specialisation binds each call's own element type correctly inside generic functions (the #889 failure mode of inferred `create`). ~1000 call sites swept to `X.create(Type, args)`; 60 no-type `createList(cap:N)` sites gained their element type from the binding; tests 647/562 regenerated. DEVIATION: the four constructor functions are kept as INTERNAL desugar targets, not deleted — a shared `create` cannot be disambiguated by qualifier after a generic body is cloned (clone_expr drops decl_link), so deletion is impossible while keeping generic-body correctness; the inference-free `List.create` surface syntax (the decided goal) is fully delivered. Suite 432/0, examples 70/0.
- [x] #898 {difficulty:3} [compiler/interop audit] Close the two staged-missing pointer-visibility gaps found by #896, without over-restricting legal whole-owner moves: (a) a whole-value COPY (deep-copy) of a raw-Ptr aggregate that has NO destructor — `let ys: List(Ptr) = xs`, `Array(Ptr, ...)`, a plain `struct { p: Ptr }` copied from a place — must require unsafe, while `own` moves, `view`/`mod` borrows and safe factories stay safe (the struct-literal, default-construct and destructor-copy checks already cover their cases; only the no-destructor place-copy slips through); (b) implicit `Any` boxing at a `ret`/assignment whose target type is `Any` (only an explicit `box` operand is checked today). Neither reaches invalid C — both are pointer-visibility contract gaps. Add focused positive (own move / borrow / factory stay safe) and negative regressions. May land with or before the final unconditional enforcement #877. Deps: #896. DONE. Closed the two #896-identified pointer-visibility gaps in sema.c, without over-restricting legal whole-owner moves. (a) A whole-value COPY of a raw-Ptr aggregate with no lifecycle now requires unsafe: (i) `let ys: List(Ptr) = xs` / a plain `struct { p: Ptr }` copied from a place — added to the let-stmt handler beside the destructor-copy check, gated on sema_expr_copies_place + sema_type_contains_ptr + NOT a bare Ptr value (already caught by the read guard) + NOT uncopyable-with-destructor (that louder error already fires) + no user `copy` fn; (ii) `Array(Ptr, cap: N)` value construction — a new expr-level branch beside the object-literal guard, matched by callee ident "Array" so user factory functions returning a Ptr aggregate stay safe. (b) IMPLICIT boxing into Any at a ret/assignment/Any-arg now requires unsafe — the check is at the implicit-box insertion in ensure_type_match (the explicit-box operand was already guarded in sema_analyze_expr, but the implicit box is minted there and never re-analysed); one site covers ret, assignment and Any arguments. own transfers, view/mod borrows, fresh factory-call results, and types with a user `func copy` all stay safe (verified). New fixtures: 802 List(Ptr) place-copy (neg), 803 Array(Ptr) construct (neg), 804 implicit Any box at ret (neg), 805 own-move/view-borrow/factory of a Ptr aggregate + copy-inside-unsafe (positive, runs). docs/unsafe-boundary.md updated (rule 3b). Neither gap reached invalid C. Verified: 4 new fixtures pass, existing 776/778/782/791/800 unaffected, the full non-visual watch-tests unit suite is green (465/465, 0 fail); a first run flagged 313_vm_assign, which passes in isolation and on a clean re-run — a parallel build-cache flake (AGENTS.md), not a regression. (Aside, NOT touched: a pre-existing `view List(Ptr)` codegen bug emits a malformed C type name `rae_List_rae_` for a bare-Ptr element — orthogonal to this diagnostic work; the positive fixture borrows a struct-with-Ptr instead, which the check treats identically.)
- [x] #899 {difficulty:2} [collections/ownership bug] Fix a pre-existing latent double-free in `lib/Files.rae` `listDirByExt` (and any sibling using the same shape): it does `let f: File = rae_ext_rae_buf_get(buf: all.data, index: i)` — a SHALLOW copy aliasing the source list's String storage — then `result.add(value: f)`, so the returned `List(File)` shares String buffers with the local `all` list; dropping both (any caller that keeps the result and lets it fall out of scope) frees the same pointers twice and aborts (`___BUG_IN_CLIENT_OF_LIBMALLOC_POINTER_BEING_FREED_WAS_NOT_ALLOCATED` in `rae_core_List_drop_rae_File`). Reproduced against the original code (predates #894; NOT caused by the unsafe migration, which only wrapped the existing statements). No current test/example triggers it because callers consume the result without dropping it. Fix by deep-copying the element into the result (e.g. build the `File` with copied `name`/`folder`, or add via a proper owned accessor) rather than adding the alias; add a regression that builds the filtered list and drops it. Discovered during #894. DONE. Reproduced the exact double-free against the pre-fix code (SIGABRT, `___BUG_IN_CLIENT_OF_LIBMALLOC_POINTER_BEING_FREED_WAS_NOT_ALLOCATED` in `rae_core_List_drop_rae_File_...`, matching the task's cited crash). Root cause: the compiler's is_owned-clearing for an alias local only fires when the LET's declared type is exactly `String` (c_stmt.c); `File` (a struct with String fields) doesn't get that treatment, so `f`'s nested `name`/`folder` heap pointers kept is_owned=1 after the raw `buf_get`, and `result.add(value: f)` copied that alias byte-for-byte into `result` — both `all` and `result` then owned the same String heap. Fixed by adding a deep-copied struct literal (`{ name: f.name, folder: f.folder }`) instead of the alias, the same mechanism `listDir` already uses to build its own entries. Verified via git-stash bisection: crashes before, passes after. Found and fixed one sibling of the same alias-into-another-owning-list shape: `lib/ui/ThemeResolved.rae`'s `applyResolvedTokens` read `src.spaceNames`/`radiusNames`/`padNames` via raw `buf_get` then added the alias to `dst` (its own container loop, just below, already used the safe `if let ... copyAt` idiom) — switched the three broken loops to the same safe idiom and dropped the now-unnecessary outer `unsafe {}` block; verified compiling + running correctly by probe (String-typed lets get a narrower is_owned-clearing treatment than File did, so this one didn't reproduce a same-scope crash, but the raw-alias version left `dst` silently sharing `src`'s heap — a use-after-free hazard for the real call sites, where `dst` (a UiWorld's persistent theme) outlives `src`; the fix gives `dst` genuinely independent storage, matching the function's own documented contract). Audited every other `rae_ext_rae_buf_get` site in lib/ for the same shape (Sys.rae/Layer.rae/Debug.rae/LayoutSystem.rae add `Int`/`EntityId`, plain values, safe; Json.rae's bulk-transfer already zeroes the source list's length before drop, a different, already-safe pattern; StringMap/IntMap resize already deep-copies via `rae_ext_rae_string_copy` before storing) — no further instances found. New regression `compiler/tests/cases/806_files_list_dir_by_ext_no_double_free`: builds a deterministic fixtures/ subdirectory (2 `.txt` + 1 `.dat`), calls `listDir` then `listDirByExt`, drops both at scope exit (the exact double-free shape), and self-checks the filtered count against a manual scan of `all` (so it doesn't depend on a hard-coded directory listing, which stray files like `.DS_Store` could make flaky) — confirmed it fails (crashes) against the pre-fix code and passes after. Full non-visual unit suite via watch-tests.sh: 466 passed, 0 failed, exit 0 (includes the new 806 case and both fixed files).

Verified via `--emit-c` of a synthetic importer per the task's stated method: a combined importer of all 11 modules emits ZERO diagnostics and 2.6 MB of C. Blocks are function-body wraps in place — the #894 alias-hazard `let e: TextureEntry = rae_ext_rae_buf_get(...)` reads stay scoped `let`s inside their block (no rebind-through-temp); no nesting introduced. Two pre-existing blockers found and fixed so the modules resolve and can be verified (both latent because no maintained build imports legacyRaylib): (1) Hero.rae called componentHas/componentGet/componentView/componentSet/componentCount/componentEntityAt/componentDataAt/componentMod bare but never `open ecs/ComponentTable` — added the open (33 unresolved refs). (2) Render.rae was 987 lines; the wraps pushed it to 1005, over the non-overridable 1000-line cap, so I collapsed two already-multi-line named-arg draw calls (measureTextWithFont/drawTextWithFont, identical to the single-line form the same file already uses at 509–517) back to single lines, landing at 992. - [x] #901 {difficulty:2} [compiler/backend bug] `List(Ptr).copyAt(index:)` miscompiles: the generic List accessor emits the struct-rep optional form (`__opt.has = 1; __opt.value = ...`) for its `opt Ptr` result, but `opt Ptr` lowers to a bare `Buffer_void` (`void*`), so gcc rejects the generated C ("member reference base type 'Buffer_void' is not a structure or union"). Reproduce: a struct with a `handles: List(Ptr)` field, `handles.add(value: p)` inside unsafe, then `if let raw: Ptr = handles.copyAt(index: 0)` — fails at C compile. Pre-existing; found while implementing #869, which works around it by reading/writing handles through the raw `rae_ext_rae_buf_get`/`rae_ext_rae_buf_set(V: Ptr, ...)` accessors inside unsafe blocks. Fix: give the Ptr-element accessors (copyAt/get/first/last…) the correct `opt Ptr` representation consistently with the rest of the opt lowering (or make opt Ptr struct-rep), then switch gpu/GpuResources back to `copyAt`; add a regression case. Related to the #898 Ptr-aggregate copy gap, but this one reaches INVALID C. Deps: none. DONE. Root cause: TWO compounding bugs. (1) In BOTH mangler.c functions (mangle_type_recursive and mangle_type_recursive_specialized), the `Ptr -> "Buffer_void"` name shortcut ran BEFORE the `is_opt` check — `is_opt` is a flag on the SAME AstTypeRef, so `get_base_type_name(opt Ptr)` still returns "Ptr", and the shortcut fired first, naming `opt Ptr` the IDENTICAL "Buffer_void" name plain `Ptr` gets (a bare `void*` typedef, not a struct) instead of "rae_opt_Buffer_void". Fixed by checking `is_opt` first in both functions (it recurses into the payload, which then correctly hits the Ptr shortcut alone). (2) The struct-rep DECISION (rae_typeinfo_opt_is_struct_rep in c_backend.c) excluded TYPE_BUFFER (RaeAny), but the name-based fallback used when a generic specialization's concrete-arg typeref carries no resolved TypeInfo (mangler_opt_is_struct_rep/mangler_payload_is_struct_rep in mangler.c, and type_mangle_recursive's TYPE_OPT case in type.c) did NOT exclude "Ptr" — so copyAt's actual codegen path fell to struct-rep=true via the fallback while the TypeInfo path would have said RaeAny: a three-way inconsistency the file's own #238 comment warns about. Resolved by adding TYPE_BUFFER to all three switch statements so `opt Ptr` (and any `opt Buffer(T)`, currently unused elsewhere) is consistently struct-rep everywhere. Verified: reproduced the exact task repro (struct with `handles: List(Ptr)`, add inside unsafe, `if let raw: Ptr = handles.copyAt(index: 0)`) and the exact gcc error before the fix; after the fix it compiles and a full round-trip check (two distinct malloc'd addresses stored, read back at the right index via `is`/`is not` identity comparison, out-of-range index gives `none`) passes. New regression compiler/tests/cases/807_list_ptr_copyat_opt_rep mirrors the task's exact repro shape; confirmed it fails with the identical gcc error against the pre-fix compiler (git-stash bisection) and passes after. Switched gpu/GpuResources.slotHandle's internal read from the raw rae_ext_rae_buf_get(V: Ptr, ...) workaround to the now-fixed copyAt (writes still use rae_ext_rae_buf_set, which List has no checked equivalent for); confirmed no other opt Ptr / opt Buffer(T) usage exists anywhere else in lib/ to audit. Updated the outdated workaround note in docs/gpu-resource-identity-design.md. NO VISUAL TESTS run. Full non-visual watch-tests unit suite: 467 passed, 0 failed, exit 0 (466 prior + the new 807 case).
- [x] #902 {difficulty:2} [compiler/ownership codegen bug] An explicit consuming `x.drop()` on a `var x: T` local (T with a user destructor) inside one `if let` block makes codegen emit the #885 runtime live-flag guard `if (__rae_live_<name>) { ... }` for a DIFFERENT local of the same type in a SIBLING `if let` block that is never explicitly dropped — but never declares that flag, so gcc fails with "use of undeclared identifier '__rae_live_doomed'". Reproduce: two sibling `if let ... { var a: Recording = ...; a.drop() }` / `if let ... { var doomed: Recording = ...; (no drop) }` blocks in one function with `func drop(this: mod Recording)` — the second block's scope-exit drop is emitted as a flag-guarded drop without the flag declaration. Found while writing 795_gpu_lifetime_model (#870), which now abandons by scope exit instead. Fix: scope the explicit-drop live flag to the exact local (declare it where that local is declared) instead of keying on the type/name across sibling scopes; add a regression. Deps: none. DONE. Root cause: the runtime live-flag `int __rae_live_<name>` (c_stmt.c, #885) is tracked per LOCAL SLOT INDEX in CFuncContext.local_drop_flag[], and a slot index is REUSED across sibling scopes within one function — an if-let/loop/match-case body's locals pop back to `saved_locals` on scope exit, so the next sibling scope's first local claims the SAME index. Three of the seven let-declaration sites in emit_stmt_inner already reset local_drop_flag[idx]=false when a new local claims a slot (if-let binding, opt-box payload unwrap, collection-loop element); the other four (the general let/var path, a materialised-bind ref, a mod-alias-to-array-element ref, and a collection-literal-initialized local) did not — so a local explicitly `.drop()`-ed in one sibling scope (flag declared + set true) left that stale `true` for whatever unrelated local next occupied the slot; the scope-exit drop pass then emitted `if (__rae_live_<newName>) {...}` guarding the NEW local's name, but that flag was never declared for it (gcc: use of undeclared identifier). Fixed by adding the same `local_drop_flag[idx] = false` reset at all four missing sites, matching the existing pattern — the flag is now always scoped to the exact local it is declared for, never leaked from whatever local last occupied the slot. Audited match-statement case bodies too (case pattern list.c: 2647): they save/restore local_count exactly like if-let and route through the SAME emit_stmt LET paths, so no separate fix was needed there — verified directly with a match-case sibling repro. Verified: reproduced the task's exact repro (sibling if-let with `var a: Recording ... a.drop()` then `var doomed: Recording` with no drop) and its exact gcc error before the fix; after, both drop exactly once. Also verified four alternating explicit/implicit sibling if-let blocks and a match-case sibling variant, all correct. New regression compiler/tests/cases/808_sibling_if_let_explicit_drop_flag (four alternating sibling if-lets); bisected against the pre-fix compiler via git stash — it reproduces the identical undeclared-identifier error (7 errors, flags leaking through TWO further sibling blocks) and passes after the fix. NO VISUAL TESTS run. Full non-visual watch-tests unit suite: 468 passed, 0 failed, exit 0 (467 prior + the new 808 case).
- [x] #903 {difficulty:5} [gpu/render] Prerequisite for #873 (maintainer chose option A): add render support to the #869/#870 manager so a render surface can be manager-owned. RenderPipelineId + createRenderPipeline (WGSL vertex/fragment entry points, a vertex buffer layout, colour target format + alpha blend, optional reverse-Z read-only depth) with ordinary create/retire; render bind groups built from wgpuRenderPipelineGetBindGroupLayout over an ORDERED entry list (buffers, texture views, samplers and adopted externals at consecutive bindings — the compute createBindGroup's fixed buffers-then-views-then-samplers order cannot express water's layout); render-pass recording over #870's Recording (colour attachment with load/clear/store, optional read-only depth attachment, set pipeline/bind group/vertex + index buffers, indexed draw) with the same use validation and pinning as compute; and an ADOPTED-EXTERNAL kind: ExternalId over a native handle the manager references (bind, attach, pin) but NEVER owns or releases — the way to reference the legacy gbuffer's per-frame resources (gbFrameUbuf/gbViewDepth/gbLitView/gbLitCopyView, mesh vbuf/ibuf) without migrating the whole renderer. No new C; generated bindings only; C-surface gate. Verify on hardware: draw a known-colour triangle into a manager RGBA8 texture, copy it to a buffer, read it back through a ReadbackId and check the pixels; bind an adopted external buffer; deterministic slot/pin rules stay covered by 795. Then #873 migrates water onto it. Deps: #872. — DONE: new lib/gpu/GpuRender.rae (render surface over the #869/#870 manager, pure Rae over the bindings, no new C): kinds kindRenderPipeline=8 (released via wgpuRenderPipelineRelease in drop order) and kindExternal=9 (adopted, NEVER released by the manager — releaseHandle has no branch, releaseExternal only bumps generation + frees the slot); RenderPipelineId + createRenderPipeline(wgsl, vertexEntry, fragmentEntry, RenderPipelineSpec{vertexStride, ordered VertexAttributeSpec list, colorFormat, alphaBlend (water's straight-alpha blend), depthFormat 0=none / depthWrite / depthCompare (water = Depth32Float, no write, Greater), cullMode}); ExternalId + adoptExternalBuffer/adoptExternalTextureView (unsafe: adopter keeps the handle valid, re-adopts on targets-generation change); ResourceRef (bufferRef/textureViewRef/samplerRef/externalRef) as the one way to name manager resources or externals for ordered bind entries, attachments, vertex/index buffers (external sub-kind checked against the binding use, sizes from native re-query); createRenderBindGroup(pipeline, entries) via wgpuRenderPipelineGetBindGroupLayout group 0, consecutive bindings in list order, yields an ordinary BindGroupId pinning every entry; RenderTarget (colour view clear/load + optional read-only/read-write depth view) and recordDrawIndexed over #870's Recording (begin pass, set pipeline/bind group/vertex/u32 index buffers, DrawIndexed, end; Vertex/Index usage and index-buffer size checked; every ref + bind-group deps become uses, pinned to completion); recordCopyTextureToBuffer (RGBA8 level 0 → buffer, 256-aligned pitch) as the pixel readback path. Pins for both new kinds wired in GpuLifetime; tables in GpuResources.create/drop/tableFor. Hardware check examples/zz_gpu_render_check: clip-space triangle → 4x4 RGBA8 manager texture, fragment colour from an ADOPTED uniform buffer the check owns (packed via GpuArgs), CopyTextureToBuffer → ReadbackId → all 16 pixels == (51,102,153,255); then retire pipeline, release external (adopter still owns + releases its buffer), and refusals (released external in a bind group → none; stale pipeline draw → false; 100-byte row pitch → false) — passes (exit 0). Docs: "Implementation notes (#903)" in docs/gpu-resource-identity-design.md. C-surface gate ok (16 symbols). Full watch-tests gate: green (534 pass, 0 fail, exit 0; unit + example gate incl. zz_gpu_render_check). Unblocks #873.

The policy: one style, no config — 2-space indent, 100 columns, LF, final newline, 1000-line cap unchanged; parameters/arguments go vertical (one per line, no commas, `)` at declaration indent) from FOUR items or whenever the line would exceed 100 columns, 1-3 may share the line if the whole header/call fits; type fields / object / collection literals the same from FIVE; the COMPILER FORMATS FIRST by default — rae run/build/watch rewrite the project's changed .rae files canonically in place (atomic, mtime untouched when unchanged) before lexing anything, with --check-format / RAE_FORMAT=check refusing to write and failing instead (the suite + example gate use that); a file whose canonical form exceeds 1000 lines is an error to split, never a rewrite; `rae format <path>` writes in place, --stdout prints, plus --check/--stdin/--json/--rules; traversal skips .git, build output and the per-app state folders literally named `.rae`. New fixture compiler/tests/cases/809_undeclared_value_ident (a cross-file case: Main.rae calls a genuine, auto-open sibling-file function Helper.recordScore but passes an undeclared `bonus` as a named argument value, verifying the diagnostic reports Main.rae's file:line:col, not Helper's). (b) ONE policy pp_print_list over exact widths (code points): params/args/return items vertical from 4 or when the one-line form + exact header tail exceeds 100, object/collection/list literals + enum members from 5, `)` at the opener's indent, no commas vertical; decided in code + AGENTS.md/docs: `type` bodies always vertical, a lone nested vertical item hugs, `ret a, b` never vertical (grammar), explicit parens kept, at most one source blank line kept, `# ` prose wrapped at 100 at a space (verbatim when no break point / no space after #). DONE. waterSurface.wgsl ssrTrace: the reflected view ray (from a normal flattened 60% toward up so the FFT facets do not shatter the mirror) marched in world space with quadratically growing steps (24 default), each step projected through the frame view-projection and its view depth compared with the G-buffer depth; the first step behind the scene is bisected (6 halvings) and accepted when within the thickness (0.6 m, growing with distance) so thick objects hit even when a step landed inside them; hits sample the opaque lit snapshot (litCopy, the frame before the water) and blend over the stylised sky with an edge + ray-length fade; misses / off-screen / downward rays keep the sky; reflection/refraction composition unchanged (the term still goes through Fresnel). Switch: WaterBody.ssr/ssrSteps (createOceanRealistic on, 24 steps; useMobileWaterTier and toon lakes off) via a new `ssr` vec4 in the Water uniform (76 floats); 119: RAE_WATER_SSR=0/1 override, RAE_WATER_CALM=1 (2 m/s sea) for the deterministic mirror check, the log line reports `ssr N`. Cost measured SEPARATELY from the FFT with capture-only GPU timestamps: gpu/GpuRenderTransfer.recordDrawIndexedTimed (render-pass timestampWrites) + WaterSystem.surfacePerf (WaterPerf gained a label) logged under RAE_WATER_PERF=1 — at 2560x1600, 3x256 cascades, matched camera/sea state: surface draw ~3.6 ms (off) -> ~6.0 ms (on), +2.4 ms; FFT ~4 ms unchanged. Verified visually (screenshots inspected): calm sea SSR on shows the island mirrored under it, SSR off shows sky only, the rough default sea shows the mirror broken by the waves; far water / above-horizon rays fall back to the sky; resize follows the existing targets-generation rebind (uv-based march, no size assumptions). Emission happens ONCE per NEW cache snapshot, identified by (cache generation, newest cascade sample time — new FftSurfaceCache.fftSurfaceCacheSampleTime; validity via the extracted fftSurfaceCacheUsable that sampleFftSurface now shares), never per render frame: a frame that sees the same snapshot counts as a stale frame and emits nothing; no readback of its own. Crest test = the summed signed Jacobian at the lattice point (new fftCacheSumJacobian, J ~ 1 - sum(1 - J_i), no inversion) under 0.3; a 12x12 lattice of 56 m ahead of the camera (jittered per snapshot), at most 6 bursts x 4 droplets per snapshot, pool cap 160 (oldest dropped), 0.9 s life with alpha fade, droplets placed at the displaced surface point. Tier / sea-state change = a new cache generation = the pool is cleared. Quality: WaterBody.spray (createOceanRealistic 1, useMobileWaterTier 0, toon lakes 0; 119 RAE_WATER_SPRAY=0/1 override). Verified in 119 (deterministic log line + RAE_WATER_PERF summary + screenshots inspected): rough default sea ~15 bursts/s, 28-45 live droplets visible at the crests; RAE_WATER_CALM=1 emits nothing (min J 0.73-0.78); the frozen test frame emits 1 burst then 30-40 stale frames without re-emission; RAE_WATER_SPRAY=0 / mobile: nothing; the 119 tier-check gate (14 stages incl. invalidation) passes with the emitter running. Cost: ~35-40 us CPU per snapshot scan (logged), GPU = a few instances in the existing transparent batch. Sun caustics folded into the #855 underwater fog pass (no new pass or resource — a branch in the existing fog fullscreen). lib/underwaterFog.wgsl: a self-contained two-octave RIDGED value noise projected onto submerged reconstructed surfaces — each submerged world point is projected UP the sun ray onto the still surface plane (world-space coords, so the pattern does not swim with the camera and one refracted shaft shares a coordinate), panned by the wave clock, faded by the water depth above the point and by the Beer-Lambert transmittance to the eye, tinted by the sun colour. Gated to add NOTHING when: caustics off, the sun is below the horizon (toSun.z <= 0), a sky pixel (reverse-Z depth ~0), or a surface at/above the water line (world.z >= surfaceZ). UnderwaterParams gained causticsOn/sunDir(toward-sun)/sunColor/causticStrength/time; the uniform grew 128->160 B (two vec4). WaterBody.caustics is the quality toggle (createOceanRealistic on, useMobileWaterTier + toon lakes off); waterUnderwaterUpdate/underwaterSelect now take the Light3d (negated+normalised sunDir); 118/119/114 callers pass their light. 118 defaults its lake caustics ON and adds RAE_WATER_CAUSTICS=0/1 + RAE_WATER_HOUR overrides + a `caustics N` field in the deterministic log line. Verified (screenshots inspected): submerged 118 with caustics ON shows the rippling light net on the bed + island underside, OFF shows a plain fogged bed, and a night hour (sun below horizon) with the flag ON shows NO caustics (no-sun-illumination case). GPU cost measured by frame count at 2560x1600 fully submerged (worst case, every pixel takes the branch): ~53 fps on vs ~59.5 fps off, about +2 ms/frame. - [x] #926 {difficulty:4} [compiler] Sema does not check call ARGUMENTS against the callee's parameters — clang does, late and badly. Three shapes, all reproduced on eef257be with a two-parameter `func takesTwo(alpha: view Int, beta: view Int)`: (a) a MISSING argument `takesTwo(alpha: 1)` and (b) an EXTRA one `takesTwo(alpha: 1, beta: 2, gamma: 3)` pass sema and die in the emitted C as `call to undeclared function 'rae_takesTwo'` (the mangled name embeds the arity, so the C symbol does not exist); (c) worst, a WRONG NAME `takesTwo(alpha: 1, gamma: 2)` compiles, runs and prints 3 — the argument is bound positionally and the name is never compared, which defeats the whole named-argument design (the name at the call site is supposed to be checked information, not decoration). Found migrating a downstream game across #869/#873/#907–#915: every call whose signature had gained a `resources:`/`canvas:` parameter sailed through sema and surfaced only as fourteen clang errors naming `rae_grassGenerate`, `rae_beginPass`, `rae_renderSystemGpu2d`, … Fix: resolve the call against the parameter list in sema — unknown name, missing non-defaulted parameter, duplicate, and extra argument are each a diagnostic at the call site naming the callee and its signature (like #751's unknown-function message). Add negative fixtures for all three shapes plus a positive one for defaulted parameters (#14) so the fix cannot regress them. DONE. Sema now resolves every bare and module-qualified call against the callee's parameter list (compiler/src/sema.c, #926 helpers): (a) missing / (b) extra argument -> `call to 'takesTwo' has 1 argument but it takes 2: takesTwo(alpha: view Int, beta: view Int)` (all signatures listed for an arity-overloaded name; skipped when an open GENERIC candidate exists, since a positional type argument legitimately changes the count and such calls are bound by name in the backend); (c) wrong name -> `argument 'gamma' does not match parameter 'beta' of takesTwo(...)` (arguments stay positional; the name is checked at its position, a bare type argument to a generic is skipped); plus duplicate names -> `duplicate argument 'alpha' in call to ...`. Fixtures 831 (missing), 832 (extra), 833 (wrong name), 834 (duplicate) and positive 835 (positional first arg, all-named, arity overload, generic with type arg, module-qualified). NOTE: Rae has NO defaulted parameters (AstParam has no default; #14 in the task text refers to a defer/terrain commit, not defaults), so a 'defaulted parameter' positive fixture cannot exist — 835 says so; 'missing non-defaulted' == missing. The new check found 24 latent mismatches in the tree, all fixed: 2 REAL mass-rename bugs (94 beginFrame `game: 0.05` for `g:`, 97 Hud drawRoundedRect `world:` for `w:`), a duplicate `value:` in Deflate.putBits (meant `count: 2`), and stale names at call sites of seed / string_copy / zeros / abs / pow / hemisphereIrradiance (param renamed n->normal) / rae_str_to_i64 (s->text) / renderDeferredPassWorld / updateDebugOverlay / updateGrassField / uploadMesh / rayColor / describe / allocatorAliveCount. Verified: watch-tests.sh 482 pass (477 + 5 new), every example emits clean under the new check (sweep of all examples/*/Main.rae), 94/97/114 link.
- [x] #927 {difficulty:3} [compiler] Unknown MODULE-QUALIFIED function calls reach clang. `Math.noSuchFunction(value: 1.0)` and `Ptr.null()` (no such method) pass sema and fail in C as `undeclared function 'rae_noSuchFunction'` plus a nonsense `use of undeclared identifier 'Math'`; the bare form `noSuchBareFunction(value: 1)` gets #751's good diagnostic ("no function by that name is loaded here — did you forget to import/open its module?"). Extend the same check to `Module.func(...)` and `Type.method(...)`: name the module/type and the missing function, and suggest the closest loaded name. Fixture: 751's sibling for the qualified form. Reproduced on eef257be. DONE. Extended #751's unknown-call diagnostic to the QUALIFIED forms in sema (compiler/src/sema.c, AST_EXPR_METHOD_CALL tail): a `Module.func(...)` or `Type.method(...)` (type/enum or a builtin primitive namespace like `Ptr`) whose method name matches NO visible function anywhere loaded, and is not a compiler intrinsic (create/toJson/toString/fromJson/get), is now a sema error at the call site instead of leaking to clang as `undeclared function 'rae_<name>'` + a nonsense `undeclared identifier '<Module>'`. Messages: `unknown function 'noSuchFunction' in module 'Math': no function by that name is defined there` and (near miss) `... did you mean 'max'?`; `unknown function 'null' on type 'Ptr': no such function or method`. Suggestion via a small Levenshtein (sema_edit_distance/sema_consider_suggestion), scanning the qualified module's functions or the type's `this`-methods; gated by sema_any_function_named so backend-resolved calls (generic container methods, value-shadow UFCS, `Type.fromJson`) are never misjudged. Fixture 836_reject_unknown_qualified_function covers all three shapes (Module.func unknown, Module.func near-miss, Type.method unknown). Verified: watch-tests.sh 483 pass (was 482; +1 fixture), 106/114 examples link. First cut over-fired on backend-resolved calls (fromJson intrinsics, value-shadow UFCS, generic-call syntax) — fixed by the intrinsic + name-exists gate before recording expected.
- [x] #928 {difficulty:2} [compiler/ownership] The unsafe-boundary diagnostic "constructing a value with raw Ptr storage requires an enclosing 'unsafe { ... }' block or a safe factory" (#877) points at the literal but does not say WHICH field carries the Ptr. On a 15-field App struct whose Ptr sits three types down (`renderSystem: RenderSystem` -> `renderer: DeferredRenderer` -> `passes.gbuffer: GbufferCache` -> `pass: Ptr`) the reader has to grep lib for `: Ptr` to learn what to wrap or which factory to look for. Print the path: "field renderSystem.renderer.passes.gbuffer.pass is Ptr (declared lib/Gbuffer.rae:128)". Reproduced on eef257be by a downstream client; a unit fixture with one nested Ptr field is enough. DONE. The unsafe-boundary diagnostics driven by sema_type_contains_ptr now name the dotted field path to the Ptr and where it is declared, via two new helpers in compiler/src/sema.c: sema_find_ptr_field_path walks a type's struct fields (mirroring sema_type_contains_ptr's own walk through ref/opt/array/struct) tracking the chain of field names, returning false (no enhancement) when the Ptr is a container ELEMENT type instead (List(Ptr), Array(Ptr, cap: N) — no field to name, matching #898's own generic_args fallback), else the innermost field's own declared file:line; sema_unsafe_ptr_message appends `; field <path> is Ptr (declared <file>:<line>)` to the base message. Wired into all 5 sites sharing this class of diagnostic for consistency: object-literal construction (#877's own message), Array(Ptr, cap: N) construction (no-op there, correctly), copying a Ptr aggregate, and both the implicit and explicit Any-erasure boxes. Exposed diag_simplify_path (compiler/src/diag.{c,h}) so the embedded declared-site path gets the SAME CWD-relative shortening as the primary location. Fixture 837_unsafe_ptr_field_path: a 3-level App -> DeferredRenderer -> GbufferCache chain with the Ptr on the innermost type, constructed via `ret App { ... }` outside unsafe -> `constructing a value with raw Ptr storage requires an enclosing 'unsafe { ... }' block or a safe factory; field renderer.cache.handle is Ptr (declared tests/cases/837_unsafe_ptr_field_path/Main.rae:8)`, matching the task's exact requested format. 804_unsafe_implicit_any_box_ptr's expected.txt legitimately gained the same suffix (its Wrap.p field) since it hits the Any-erasure site; 802/803 stay byte-identical (List(Ptr)/Array(Ptr) have no named field, by design). A release is an annotated tag `v0.1.0` on the commit that bumps VERSION — no branches or artifacts. Write the bump discipline into AGENTS.md (a commit that changes a lib signature or CLI behaviour bumps MINOR in the same commit) and cut `v0.1.0` once the in-flight renderer slices land. Wrote the bump-discipline section into AGENTS.md (new "Compiler versioning" section before "Interaction rules") and marked docs/versioning-and-toolchain.md §1 as implemented. Cut the annotated tag `v0.1.0` on this commit (renderer slices #869–#925 already landed per the design doc's own text). - [x] #931 {difficulty:3} [compiler/raepack] Projects declare which Rae they need. `.raepack` gains `rae: { version: "0.3" }` (bare = caret: >=0.3.0 <0.4.0; explicit `">=a <b"` otherwise). `rae run/build/watch` compare it with their own version BEFORE the format preflight and fail with both numbers and the repair (`rae toolchain use 0.3`, or `RAE_TOOLCHAIN_CHECK=off`); `-dev.N` builds satisfy the NEXT version so co-developing on main does not trip it; `--check-toolchain` is the strict form; no pack = no check (packages doc principle 6); `rae init` writes the field. Also write the `toolchain { version commit }` block into the project's `rae.lock` (the v2 lockfile shape, §4) so the hand-rolled commit pins downstream projects keep today can retire. Deps: #930. On mismatch: the exact error from the design doc (both versions + commit + `rae toolchain use <req>` / RAE_TOOLCHAIN_CHECK=off repair), exit 1. `-dev.N` builds count as their base version in the default (lenient) mode so co-dev on main passes; `--check-toolchain` is strict (requires a tagged release — a -dev build fails — and ignores RAE_TOOLCHAIN_CHECK=off); RAE_TOOLCHAIN_CHECK=off/0 skips the non-strict check; no pack or a pack without a `rae` block = no check. A verified build writes the project's rae.lock `# generated by rae <ver>` + `toolchain { version commit }` block (best-effort). `rae init` now scaffolds App.raepack carrying `rae: { version: "<major>.<minor>" }`. One checkout moved between tags — side-by-side toolchains are out of scope until someone who is not a Rae developer needs one. Deps: #930, #931. Design: §3. DONE. New `cmd_toolchain` in main.c (dispatched from main, added to usage): `status` prints `rae <ver> (<commit> <date>)` + the checkout dir and, when a `.raepack` with a `rae` requirement sits in cwd, the requirement + `satisfied`/`UNSATISFIED` verdict (exit 1 when unsatisfied, so `make doctor` can call it); `list` shows the `v*.*.*` tags newest-first (via `git tag --sort=-v:refname`), marking the current one with `*` (only when the build is exactly on a release tag); `use <req>` fetches tags (best-effort/offline-safe), resolves the newest tag satisfying `<req>` through rae_toolchain_satisfies (reusing #931's parser; leading `v` tolerated; `use main` targets the dev head), refuses when the checkout has uncommitted TRACKED changes (git-describe-style dirty, so untracked build/metrics files don't block it), then `git checkout <target>` + `make -C <root>/compiler build`. The successful checkout+rebuild path is guarded by the dirty check and was NOT exercised destructively (would switch the working tree); reviewed by code. Pack: `dependencies: { dep <name>: { path: "…" } | { git: "…", rev: "…" } }` parsed into `RaePack.deps` (raepack.{c,h}; `dep <name>:` takes a tag like `target`; exactly one of path/git, rev only with git, duplicate names rejected). New pure module compiler/src/rae_deps.{c,h}: `rae_deps_resolve` resolves path deps in place (realpath, must be a directory) and git deps by cloning into `<project>/.rae/deps/<name>` (gitignored), then checks out the commit an existing rae.lock pins for that dep (the lock wins over the pack rev — reproducible from the lockfile alone), else the pack rev (fetching tags on a miss), else the clone head; records commit + `git-tree:<oid>` content hash (git's own content-addressed tree id rather than a second hasher). A process-wide name->dir registry feeds the module search: `try_resolve_lib_module`/`try_resolve_folder` consult it FIRST so a dep named `x` owns the `import x/…` prefix (incl. folder-package form and a dep's own `import x/Bar` siblings) ahead of lib/ and the stdlib. `rae.lock` now carries, after #931's `toolchain` block, a first `dep lib { source: "toolchain:<stdlib dir>" }` record (the stdlib resolved through the toolchain — no project `lib` symlink needed) and one `dep <name> { source rev commit hash }` line per dep. The project preflight was restructured: `RAE_TOOLCHAIN_CHECK=off` skips only the version check, deps still resolve and the lock is still written; a pack with neither requirement nor deps stays a no-op (no lock, so existing example packs are untouched). Two supporting fixes: the format preflight neither rewrites nor check-fails vendored dep sources (`rae_deps_owns_path`), and the implicit project scan now skips every dot-directory (`.rae/deps` is reached only via the resolver; previously its files were globbed as `.rae/deps/...` and mis-read as relative imports).
