Deps: #933. Design: §4. DONE. The package CLI over #933, all in main.c reusing rae_deps: `rae add <name> --path <dir> | --git <url> [--rev <rev>]` edits the project pack — creating the `dependencies` block before `targets` if absent, or splicing a sibling-indented `dep <name>: { ... }` into the existing block (brace-matched); refuses a duplicate name and bad flag combos (both/neither path/git, --rev without --git), guards the mechanical edit with a brace-balance check + atomic temp-rename (a full parse would wrongly couple this metadata edit to entry files existing). `rae fetch` resolves deps into `.rae/deps` honoring the committed lock (offline-safe; never rewrites an existing lock, writes one only when none existed to capture the bootstrap state). `rae update [<name>]` sets rae_deps update-mode (new rae_deps_set_update: ignore the lock pin + `git fetch` the remote, for the named dep or all), re-resolves, and rewrites rae.lock. `rae tree` prints the resolved graph (new rae_deps_print_tree: the stdlib `lib` edge first, then each dep with source and, for git, `@ <rev> (<short commit>)`). All four find the pack in cwd. - [x] #935 {difficulty:3} [compiler/runtime] `gbReadWgsl` (lib/Gbuffer.rae, over nativeGbReadWgsl -> rae_ext_rae_sys_read_file, a bare `fopen(path, "rb")`) resolves its OWN shader assets by a literal CWD-relative path baked into the stdlib source - `"lib/grass_compute.wgsl"`, `"lib/terrain_palette.wgsl"`, `"lib/underwaterFog.wgsl"`, `"lib/sprite_billboard.wgsl"`, `"lib/transparentForward.wgsl"`, `"lib/world_biome.wgsl"`, `"lib/noise.wgsl"`, `"lib/terrain_detail.wgsl"`, `"lib/terrain_splat.wgsl"`, `"lib/grass_render.wgsl"` across GbufferSprite/GbufferUnderwater/GbufferTerrain/GrassCompute/TransparentForward. #933 made `.rae` MODULE IMPORTS resolve through the toolchain (a downstream project can delete its `lib` symlink and still build - verified by a downstream client), but these ten reads are plain relative-path file opens that #933 never touches, so a project without a physical `lib/` directory at its process cwd compiles clean and then crashes (fopen fails silently, returning an EMPTY string per rae_ext_rae_sys_read_file's own `if (!f) return {NULL,0,0,0}` - the crash is not here, it is whatever downstream WebGPU shader-module creation does with an empty WGSL source, several frames later and much harder to diagnose than a missing file). Two shapes of fix, either closes the gap: (a) resolve these paths through the SAME mechanism `try_resolve_lib_module`/#933's dependency resolver already uses for imports - e.g. join against the toolchain-resolved stdlib dir #933 already computes and records as `dep lib`'s `source`, rather than assuming cwd; or (b) at minimum, fail LOUDLY (a diagnostic naming the missing path) instead of returning an empty string that silently propagates into a WGPU crash far from its cause. Either way this closes the one remaining reason a downstream project must still keep a `lib` symlink/vendor copy after #933 - not for imports, only for these runtime asset reads. Reported by a downstream client after #933; reproduced by building AND RUNNING a project with `lib` deleted entirely (build succeeds, run does not). DONE. Root cause: gbReadWgsl (and NoiseWgsl.source, both extern-aliased to rae_ext_rae_sys_read_file, a bare fopen) read stdlib shaders by cwd-relative "lib/*.wgsl", so a project without a physical lib/ at its process cwd built clean then fed an EMPTY WGSL string into WGPU shader-module creation and crashed frames later. Fix routes all shader-asset reads through a NEW dedicated runtime function rae_ext_rae_gb_read_shader (compiler/runtime/runtime_filesystem.c): (1) tries the path as given so a project lib/ or an assets/ override at cwd still wins; (2) for a "lib/..." path, joins it against $RAE_STDLIB (the toolchain stdlib dir) with the leading "lib/" stripped, so stdlib shaders resolve with no local lib/ — closing the same gap #933 closed for imports; (3) if found nowhere, fails LOUDLY with a stderr diagnostic naming the exact path (and the $RAE_STDLIB tried) instead of returning empty — both fix shapes the task blessed. Wired lib/GbufferNative.rae nativeGbReadWgsl extern("...") and lib/NoiseWgsl.rae externs to the new symbol (covers GbufferSprite/Underwater/Terrain/GrassCompute/TransparentForward/water/* and NoiseWgsl); the generic rae_ext_rae_sys_read_file is untouched so gltf/png/user reads keep their exact empty-on-miss semantics. run_compiled_file and the watch child now setenv("RAE_STDLIB", compiler_stdlib_dir(), 0) (0 = never overwrite a caller value) so `rae run`/`rae watch` are zero-config; the `rae init` Makefile `run` recipe now exports RAE_STDLIB=$(RAE_REPO)/lib for the directly-run built binary. Verified non-visually (NO VISUAL TESTS run) with a scratch program calling the extern: cwd path wins; missing-at-cwd resolves via $RAE_STDLIB; a true miss prints the loud error and returns len 0; and `rae run` with RAE_STDLIB unset resolved lib/noise.wgsl from the real toolchain lib. Full watch-tests.sh suite 511/511 0 failed; edited .rae files verified canonical. No VERSION bump (following the #931-#934 precedent of riding the next release cut rather than moving VERSION mid-slice). Note for the user: a downstream project can now delete its lib symlink/vendor copy entirely — `rae run`/`watch` need nothing; a hand-rolled build+run just needs RAE_STDLIB pointing at the toolchain lib/ (the loud error names it, and `rae init` now sets it). A real GPU render check is a visual gate — please run `make test-examples` by hand if you want to confirm a windowed example still renders.
- [x] #936 {difficulty:2} [compiler/runtime] #935 fixed `gbReadWgsl`/`NoiseWgsl.source`'s bare-fopen shader reads by routing them through the new `rae_ext_rae_gb_read_shader` ($RAE_STDLIB fallback + loud failure), but THREE MORE shader reads have the identical bug and were not touched: `nativeG2dBoxReadFile` (lib/Gpu2dCanvas.rae, lib/Gpu2dCanvasImage.rae, lib/Gpu2dCanvasText.rae) is still `extern("rae_ext_rae_sys_read_file")` — the same generic bare `fopen` #935 deliberately left alone for gltf/png/user reads — reading `"lib/gpu2d_box.wgsl"`, `"lib/gpu2d_image.wgsl"` and `"lib/gpu2d_text.wgsl"` by literal CWD-relative path. Fix: give these three the same treatment as #935 — wire nativeG2dBoxReadFile's extern to rae_ext_rae_gb_read_shader (or a second symbol built the same way) so the $RAE_STDLIB fallback and the loud-failure-on-a-true-miss both apply here too; the generic rae_ext_rae_sys_read_file itself should stay untouched (gltf/png/user reads keep their exact semantics), same boundary #935 drew. Deps: none beyond #935 (same runtime file, same pattern to extend). DONE. Same fix shape as #935, one-line change: nativeG2dBoxReadFile (lib/Gpu2dCanvas.rae) was still extern("rae_ext_rae_sys_read_file") — the generic bare-fopen #935 deliberately left untouched for gltf/png/user reads — so its three lib/*.wgsl callers (gpu2d_box.wgsl in Gpu2dCanvas.rae, gpu2d_image.wgsl in Gpu2dCanvasImage.rae via `open Gpu2dCanvas`, gpu2d_text.wgsl in Gpu2dCanvasText.rae likewise) inherited the same silent-miss bug, just with a worse symptom: no crash, a one-line stderr warning, and the entire 2D canvas layer (HUD/buttons/text) silently dropped for the run. Retargeted the extern to rae_ext_rae_gb_read_shader (#935's $RAE_STDLIB-fallback + loud-failure reader); all three callers pick it up automatically since Gpu2dCanvasImage.rae/Gpu2dCanvasText.rae reach nativeG2dBoxReadFile only via `open Gpu2dCanvas`, not their own extern declarations, so one line fixes all three. rae_ext_rae_sys_read_file itself untouched, same boundary #935 drew. DONE: SceneFile.rae and SkyHosek.rae rebound (nativeSceneReadFile / nativeHosekReadFile), app3dSceneDir's comment updated, runtime_filesystem.c messages generalised. - [x] #938 {difficulty:3} [gpu/lifetime] `pollSubmission` (lib/gpu/GpuLifetime.rae) misreads `wgpuDevicePoll(wait: 0, submissionIndex)`: per wgpu.h its return value is "true if the QUEUE is empty, false if submissions are still in flight" — not "that index finished". So any consumer that tracks one submission per frame while the renderer keeps the GPU busy never sees its work retire: its `inFlight` list grows by one every frame until the manager's submission slots (capacity 96 in the examples) are full, `submit` returns none, and — for GrassCompute, which resets the indirect draw's instanceCount to 0 BEFORE dispatching — nothing is drawn until the queue happens to drain, at which point every pending submission retires at once and the field comes back. A downstream track-and-field client saw it as grass on for ~10 s, gone for ~2 s, back again; a per-frame probe of `grass.inFlight.length` read 30, 60, 90, 96, 96, 22, 52, 82, 96 — a sawtooth capped at the slot count. Gbuffer/ShadowMaps/Gpu2dCanvasFrame/WaterSystem track submissions the same way and only survive because their polls happen to land when the queue is momentarily empty. Fix: give the runtime a real per-index signal — `rae_wgpu_watch_submission(index)` registers a `wgpuQueueOnSubmittedWorkDone` callback (AllowProcessEvents, index in userdata1, no allocation) that records the highest completed index (indices are monotonic on the one queue), and `rae_wgpu_submission_done(index)` pumps callbacks nonblockingly and answers `index <= highest || queueEmpty`. `submit` watches the index it just got; `pollSubmission` asks the runtime instead of `wgpuDevicePoll`. Also makes the layer work under Emscripten, where `wgpuDevicePoll` does not exist and only the callback path can answer. DONE: runtime_webgpu.c (+ rae_runtime.h prototypes), webgpu/Context.rae externs, GpuLifetime.rae submit/pollSubmission + header comment. Not unit-testable without a device; verified by the downstream client's probe: `inFlight` now stays at 1–2 across a 9 s headless run instead of sawtoothing to 96, and the grass no longer disappears. Full watch-tests.sh suite run after the change (see commit).
- [x] #939 {difficulty:4} [lib/ui] Put the UI pipeline on lib/ecs `Schedule` with DECLARED read/write tables per system. Today lib/ui has zero `Schedule` users: 106's FramePipeline and every UI example call fitSystem/layoutSystem/transformSystem/animationSystem/renderSystem in hand order. Extend `addSystem` to take `reads:`/`writes:` table lists (a table id per UiWorld field; `fields(UiWorld)` reflection can enumerate them), make the runner call `shouldRun` off the declared read set instead of a caller-supplied generation, and register every lib/ui system as an entry in its canonical order. Keep behaviour byte-identical (dirty-skip semantics unchanged). This is also the prerequisite for parallel schedule stages (docs/parallelism-first-plan.md Phase 2). - [x] #940 {difficulty:3} [lib/ui] Migrate the join-shaped `componentHas` ladders in lib/ui to the #807 query loops. lib/ui has 0 uses of `query2`/`query3` and 169 `componentHas(` calls (SceneLoader 26, RenderSystem 20, LayoutSystem 17, legacyRaylib 18). Keep genuinely optional-component checks; rewrite every "has A and has B then read both" ladder as a `query2`/`query3` loop (the `without`/`changedSince` filters where they apply). DONE. (legacyRaylib's 18 are gone — #955 deleted it.) Surveyed all remaining 151 `componentHas` in lib/ui; converted the ones that are genuinely join/filter shaped and left the rest, which are legitimately NOT joins: (a) per-entity optional accessor guards where `entity` is a parameter (Transform/LayoutSystem/ScrollPanel/WidgetStyleSystem/InputSystem helpers — `entityRect`, `applyConstraintsW`, `styleWantTint`, …); (b) hierarchy/depth-order traversals whose order is load-bearing (RenderSystem `paintEntityG`/`paintSubtreeG` paint order, LayoutSystem `measureNode`, Transform `accumulateVisibility` loop 2, LayoutPlace `place*`); (c) root scans over `allocator.alive` that FEED A NON-STABLE SELECTION SORT (`Layer.collectPaintableRoots`) — reordering the input would change the tie-break paint order, so it must stay; (d) single-table walks that must process EVERY row with an optional secondary read (`visualBoundsSystem` over texts, `widgetStyleSystem` over stateStyles, `hoverScaleSystem`); (e) inventory/debug dumps (`SceneLoader.componentNamesFor`, `dumpScene`); (f) a structural-mutation loop that `componentRemove`s mid-iteration (`resolveSceneInstances`), which query iteration forbids. Two genuine conversions landed: (1) `fitSystem`'s Aspect∧ComputedRect loop → `query2(aspects, computedRects)` reading each side by dense index via `componentDataAt` and writing only-on-change via `queryModAt` (same change-gated generation bump as the old `componentMod`); (2) `accumulateVisibility`'s root loop (localTransforms with NO Parent) → the `without(localTransforms, parents)` filter — both order-independent, so output is identical. `changedSince` did not apply anywhere here (the observation migration is items #5/#941-era, not these ladders). Verified: `make -C compiler build` clean, both files canonical (`rae format --check`), full watch-tests.sh suite 511/511 (0 failed) — byte-identical verdict to the pre-change baseline, so no fixture output moved. No windowed example run. lib/ui now has 2 query-loop uses where it had 0. - [x] #941 {difficulty:3} [lib/ui/raescene] The scene registry silently no-ops components it does not handle — a correctness bug for the .raescene format. `Registry.rae` registers 66 names against UiWorld's 76 component tables and lets unhandled components ("most effects, DataRequest, ListView") fall through to a silent no-op. Make an unregistered or unsupported component a DIAGNOSTIC naming scene file, node id and component name (loud at load, like every other Rae error), cover the missing tables (register or explicitly reject each with the reason), and add a fixture with a scene carrying an unsupported component that must fail with that message. Design: docs/ui-ecs-refactor-status.md §2.3. Verify with watch-tests.sh (full suite green); NO VISUAL TESTS — do not run run_examples.sh / make test-examples / any windowed example; where a look could change, end with a note asking the user to run `make test-examples` for screenshot parity. DONE. `applyComponentByName`'s silent fallthrough is now a LOUD FATAL diagnostic: it logs `[scene] {sceneId} node {nodeId}: component {name} ...` and `exit(1)` (Sys.exit flushes stdio, so the message is captured). Two forms, so the author knows which case: a name IN the registry vocabulary but with no deserialiser arm → "...is registered but not supported by the loader yet"; a name that is not a known component at all (typo, or a 3D component in a UI scene) → "...is not registered". Threaded `sceneId`/`nodeId` into `applyComponentByName` and its 4 call sites (SceneLoader, SceneInstance, and Frames' two overlay paths — added a `nodeId` param to `overlayNodeFromBag` and its two callers). Coverage/audit: parsed every UiWorld `.raescene` in the repo — the ONLY authored component that was silently dropped is `"Overflow"` (used by 111/112/114 ui.raescene as `{"mode":"clip"}`; the registry had the wrong vocabulary entry "OverflowPolicy" and no arm). Added a real `"Overflow"` → `overflows`(OverflowPolicy) deserialiser and fixed the vocabulary (Overflow + ExtentAnchor added, misnamed OverflowPolicy removed). The 3D component keys (Transform3d/MeshRenderer/Camera3d/…) go through the separate Scene3d loader, never `applyComponentByName`, so they are unaffected. Every other declared-but-unhandled UiWorld table (effects, DataRequest, ListView, Margin, Constraints, …) now reports "registered but not supported yet" instead of silently vanishing; none appears in any real scene, so nothing regresses. New fixtures 840_scene_unsupported_component (WobbleFx → "registered but not supported") and 841_scene_unregistered_component (Wibble → "not registered"); updated 412_ui_ecs count 59→60. Verified: full watch-tests.sh suite 513/513 (0 failed, +2 new), format-check-tree canonical (933 files). LOOK MAY CHANGE: `"Overflow": {"mode":"clip"}` in 111/112/114 now actually clips (it was a silent no-op before) — please run `make test-examples` for screenshot parity on 111/112/114 when convenient.
- [x] #942 {difficulty:3} [lib/ui/raescene] Registry synthesis: key the deserialiser/applier by `typeName(T)` over `fields(UiWorld)` (both landed — #772/#773/#809 — and have 0 uses in lib/ui) so adding a component is one UiWorld field, not one field + one name entry + one hand-written `if name is` arm. Collapse the 39 arms in Registry.rae/RegistryDeser.rae (777 lines) as far as the reflection allows; keep any per-component custom decode as an explicit override table, not a ladder. Deps: #941. Design: docs/ui-ecs-refactor-status.md §2.3, docs/ecs-language-wishlist.md (registry item). Verify with watch-tests.sh (full suite green); NO VISUAL TESTS — do not run run_examples.sh / make test-examples / any windowed example; where a look could change, end with a note asking the user to run `make test-examples` for screenshot parity. INVESTIGATED, NOT IMPLEMENTED — blocked by a design fork only you can settle; no code changed. Finding: the authored `.raescene` loader (applyComponentByName + the 35 deserX in RegistryDeser.rae) CANNOT be collapsed by `typeName`/`fields` reflection with today's language, and doing so would silently break scene loading (which I cannot visually verify). Three hard blockers, all confirmed in the maintainer's OWN docs: (1) `docs/compile-time-reflection.md` states "Rae has no field reflection and no function values, so nothing can hold a world's heterogeneous ComponentTable(T) values as data" — so there is NO way to build a name→decoder dispatch table (the "override table, not a ladder" the task asks for is unrepresentable without function values); (2) the same doc: `fields(Type)` CONSTRUCTION was assessed and DECLINED (#774) — reflection is read-only iteration over an existing value, so a generic decoder cannot BUILD a component value field-by-field from JSON; (3) the ONLY generic decoder reflection can reach is the compiler-generated `T.fromJson` (MACHINE format), but the authored grammar is deliberately different — `lib/ui/Serialize.rae`'s own header says the authored loader "parses a hand-friendly grammar (enum strings, theme tokens like \"gap\":\"spaceM\", flat {x,y,w,h}) and STAYS AS-IS ... SEPARATE from" the reflection-driven machine snapshot (worldToJson/worldFromJson, which ALREADY uses fields()+fieldName()). Each deserX applies per-field DEFAULTS for missing keys and enum-string/theme-token/shorthand mapping that fromJson does not — swapping to fromJson would drop those and corrupt every real scene. Reflection thus helps the machine save/load path (done) but not the authored path. Even reflection-deriving just the #941 vocabulary is unsafe: `fields(world)` typeName includes derived/runtime tables (ComputedRect, Parent, WorldTransform2D) that are NOT authorable, and authored-name≠type-name cases exist (authored "Overflow" vs type OverflowPolicy). QUESTION: which direction do you want? (A) Keep the authored loader hand-written (matching the existing Serialize.rae note) and RE-SCOPE #942 to the only safe reflection win — replace the hand-maintained `registeredComponentNames()` list with a reflected, serialize-flag-filtered vocabulary + a small explicit alias table (removes the "name entry" but NOT the decode arms); (B) migrate the `.raescene` authored format to the machine toJson/fromJson format so the existing reflected worldFromJson can load it (a breaking format change across all scene assets + editor tooling, drops the hand-friendly grammar — needs its own design/tasks); or (C) add a language capability (function values / a per-type decoder-registration mechanism / reopen construction reflection) so custom decoders can be dispatched generically (a language-design decision). I recommend (A) for a near-term win but it will NOT collapse the 39 decode arms; (B)/(C) are larger and out of scope for one queue task. RESOLVED / CLOSED — do NOT reopen or re-investigate this item. Maintainer chose (C) scoped narrowly (no function values). This ticket was SPLIT into #959 (mutable field assignment + T.default()), #960 (enumFromName + generic decodeField), and #961 (collapse the arms — the actual re-scoped #942, deps #959/#960/#941). Marked [x] because the work now lives entirely in those three tasks; there is nothing left to do under #942. When #959–#961 are done, this is done — skip it.

All four consumers drain via the ecs/Event API — `eventCount(this: input.uiActions)` / `eventAt(this: input.uiActions, i:)` returning UiAction (fields unchanged, so `ev.actionId`/`ev.entity` reads are untouched): ScreenRouter.dispatchActions (screen router), DebugGpu2dLocal (debug menu), RenderDecide (the render-decision "input produced an event" gate), FramePipeline (hadActions + the dragJustReleased drop-count), plus the Main.rae per-frame debug log. - [x] #944 {difficulty:3} [lib/ui+examples/106] Use `changedSince` / `componentModStamp` for UI observation instead of bespoke revision integers. 106's `UiRefreshCache` tracks `historyLen`, `sheetEpoch`, `playbackRevision` by hand (0 uses of changedSince/modStamp in the example); the docs (ecs-systems-and-data-observation.md) prescribe table mod-stamps. Migrate the refresh/observer systems onto `changedSince` over the tables they watch and delete the counters. Deps: #939. Design: docs/ui-ecs-refactor-status.md §2.5. Verify with watch-tests.sh (full suite green); NO VISUAL TESTS — do not run run_examples.sh / make test-examples / any windowed example; where a look could change, end with a note asking the user to run `make test-examples` for screenshot parity. INVESTIGATED, NOT IMPLEMENTED — the premise "changedSince over the tables they watch" does not hold; no code changed. Finding: `changedSince`/`componentModStamp` are strictly `ComponentTable(T)`-scoped (per-entity denseStamps / table generation). But 106's three bespoke counters observe RESOURCE/ACTOR state, NOT component tables: `historyLen` watches `state.playHistory.entries.length` (a plain `List(PlayHistoryEntry)` on the AppState resource), `sheetEpoch` watches `state.bottomSheet.epoch` (an Int field on the BottomSheetState resource), and `playbackRevision`/`nowPlayingRevision` watch `PlaybackState.revision`/`.nowPlayingRevision` — PlaybackState IS the design's PlaybackSystem ACTOR (§3/§5.1) and those are its component-INSTANCE / SYSTEM revisions (§7). None of PlayHistory / BottomSheetState / PlaybackState is a ComponentTable, so there is no table generation for changedSince to read. Crucially the observation doc's OWN model (ecs-systems-and-data-observation.md §7) has THREE revision levels: TABLE revision = componentTableGeneration/changedSince; SYSTEM revision = a bespoke `revision: Int`; component-INSTANCE revision = a bespoke `revision: Int` field — i.e. the doc EXPLICITLY blesses the exact bespoke ints 106 uses for its non-table state (the code even cites §7). And 106 ALREADY uses the table-level primitive where it applies: RenderCacheGpu2d.rae keys its dirty check off `componentTableGeneration` over ComputedRect/WorldTransform2D/Shape/Sprite/Text/Active/Opacity/Children/Parent/OnClick. So "0 uses of changedSince in the example" is only true of these resource observers, which are not changedSince candidates. To delete these counters and use changedSince, 106's playback / play-history / bottom-sheet state would have to be RE-MODELLED as ECS component tables (entities) — a substantial data-model refactor, visually-untestable here, and at odds with the doc framing PlaybackState as a resource/actor. QUESTION: which direction? (A) CLOSE as not-applicable / already-correct — accept that these are the doc §7 system/instance-revision pattern (table-level observation is already done in RenderCacheGpu2d), optionally with a tiny tidy of `historyLen` from a List-length diff into a named revision int (still not changedSince); (B) commission a separate, larger task to re-model 106's playback/history/bottom-sheet as ECS component tables so table-level `changedSince`/`componentModStamp` applies and the revision ints disappear (its own difficulty-4+ effort, needs screenshot verification); or (C) something else. I recommend (A). Guessing wrong (re-architecting the data model, or ripping out the revisions and breaking observation in an example I cannot run) would waste the work — hence asking rather than implementing. RESOLVED: maintainer chose (A) — close as already-correct + do the tidy. DONE: added `revision: Int` to `PlayHistory` (createPlayHistory inits 0), bumped on every append (recordPlay + restoreHistoryFromSource), and switched `refreshHistoryViewsIfNeeded` to observe `playHistory.revision` instead of diffing `entries.length` (renamed the observe cache `UiRefreshCache.historyLen`->`historyRevision` + its createUiRefreshCache param + the Main.rae seed/sync sites). Behaviour-preserving (append bumps revision exactly where the length used to grow) and strictly more correct at cap (an append that evicts leaves length unchanged but bumps revision). `sheetEpoch` and `playbackRevision`/`nowPlayingRevision` left as-is — they are the doc §7 system/instance-revision pattern for resource/actor state, not changedSince candidates. Added the clarifying sentence to docs/ui-ecs-refactor-status.md §2.5 ("changedSince is for ComponentTable; resource/actor state uses §7 revision ints — 106 already does both correctly"). The stress runner only LOGS rss growth for an external slope script (no threshold), so building the full world per rebuild changes its baseline magnitude, not any verdict. Perf: the old ladder was zero-alloc and scrollBoundsForScreen runs every frame, so the table is built ONCE and cached on the App (`App.screenPages`, aliased in runApp) and the hot lookup walks a `view` of it — no per-frame allocation; the cold callers (screen switch, world rebuild) build the 7-row table locally. Out of scope (not ScreenRouter): the persistence ordinal map screenToInt/intToScreen (HotReloadGlue/PlaybackStateIo, a legitimate enum<->int), the nav-tab->screen map (NavTabsBuilder) and ScreenSwitch/ScreenSafeArea/App/FramePipeline uses. docs §3.3 marked LANDED. - [x] #959 {difficulty:4} [compiler/reflection] Mutable field assignment for reflection (foundation for registry synthesis, #961; from the #942 (C) decision). `fields(mod value)` already binds each field in `mod` mode for method calls (see lib/ecs/World.rae `destroyEntity`, docs/compile-time-reflection.md building-block 1); add the missing piece — WHOLESALE field WRITE — as `fieldSet(f, v)` with UFCS `f.set(v)` (naming matches the existing compile-time `fieldName(f)`/`typeName(f)`), where `v`'s type must equal the field's type (checked per unrolled field). Also add a compiler-generated whole-value default `T.default()` (same generated-method shape as `T.fromJson`/`value.toJson()`; every field zero/empty/first-enum-case) so a generic decoder can start from `var comp: T = T.default()` and fill field by field. Read-only `fields(view value)` unchanged. This is iteration+assignment over an EXISTING value, NOT `fields(Type)` construction — the #774 decision stands (`T.default()` is one opaque value primitive, not field-by-field construction reflection). Do NOT add function values. Update docs/compile-time-reflection.md (new "mutable iteration + fieldSet + default value" section) and add fixtures proving `fieldSet` writes back and `T.default()` round-trips. Deps: none. Verify with watch-tests.sh (full suite green); VISUAL TESTS OK — running the visual example gate (`make test-examples`, scoped with RAE_EXAMPLE_FILTER, or a single windowed example) is allowed when a change could alter what is drawn; use a timeout, one run at a time, never alongside a live watch-tests.sh. DONE: (1) `fieldSet(f, value: v)` / UFCS `f.set(value: v)` — sema's field-loop unroller (shared by the #772 value path and the #773 generic path) rewrites the statement into the ordinary assignment `x.<field> = v`, so type check + ownership + drop are the assignment's own (note: Rae's call grammar requires the second argument NAMED, hence `value:`). (2) `T.default()` — a static intrinsic like `T.fromJson`; C backend emits `((rae_T){0})` through the type printer, so builtins (`Int.default()`, `String.default()`) and generic `T` work; carries the #896 raw-Ptr unsafe guard like fromJson. (3) To make the per-field check real, sema's `ensure_type_match` gained a scalar-KIND mismatch diagnostic (`type mismatch: expected Int, got String`) — before, String-into-Int assignments were only caught by gcc; it is conservative (both sides fully resolved scalars of different kinds, numerics keep their own rule). (4) Field-loop patterns now honour `opt`: an `opt T` field is matched only by an `opt T` pattern (and keeps opt on the alias); plain/`any` patterns skip it — previously the filter ignored opt and the alias then errored. Docs: new section in docs/compile-time-reflection.md. Fixtures 843 (both spellings, generic W path, default→toJson→fromJson→toJson round-trip, builtin/nested defaults) + 844 (mismatch rejected). - [x] #965 {difficulty:5} It seems like all examples, such as 106 and 114 at least, leak memory like hell while just being idle. Crazy. Even crazier that none of our tests have caught this happening during the refactorings. Do not revert anything. But investigate the leaks, analyze reasons and DO PROPER fixes. Not hacky fixes of any kind. Robust, production ready, sane fixes to the memory leaks. You can even change language or the compiler, if their design is causing the leaks. DONE. Measured first (RSS sampling + RAE_MEM_STATS per-site + macOS `leaks` with MallocStackLogging on a -g build): 114 windowed idle grew ~48 MB/s (162k leaked List buffers in 12 s, every stack through Gpu2dCanvas.emptyTextAtlas -> createList), and 106 with Spotify ON sat at 2.4 GB RSS (26k leaked `concat` strings = 2.1 GB, one JSON save of the play history) plus a slow interp leak per poll tick; headless 106 / Spotify-off were flat, which is why the gates never saw it (they run headless, no Spotify, no windowed idle loop). Root causes are three COMPILER ownership gaps, fixed in codegen (no example was touched): (1) a whole-value store into an owned heap-owning place — local, field, element, or through a `mod T` param — never released the previous value (`var atlas = emptyTextAtlas(); atlas = own known` leaked four Lists per glyph); now the RHS is evaluated into a temp, the old value dropped (unless the local was moved out), then installed — c_stmt.c assignment for both the owned-place and the `mod`-ref paths, String included (pool_take on fresh strings). (2) `own <member>` was a pass-through leaving the source live; with (1) that double-freed the swap idiom (EventQueue.frameAdvance) — now `own <place>` reads the value out and zeroes the source (the valid empty value), a `let` from a move owns, and `List/IntMap/StringMap.drop()` leave data null instead of dangling (fixture 417's contract). (3) a fresh String (call/interp/concat result) borrowed by a `view String` param was hoisted into an `__rae_pw` temp and never dropped — every intermediate of a chained `.concat()` leaked; now such slots are #884 statement temporaries (pool-taken, dropped after the statement); and a String local reassigned from a fresh value is marked owning so its scope exit releases it (`var s: String = ""` at function top leaked once per call). After: 114 buf outstanding 162,369 -> 19 (constant boot residue, flat between 6 s and 20 s runs), 106+Spotify RSS 2.4 GB -> ~180 MB with interp/concat outstanding 7/0 and flat over 34 s; 114 windowed RSS 183 -> 186 MB over 30 s decelerating — that residual is outside Rae's allocator (GPU/Metal pools, not attributable this turn). Regression guard: fixture 847 (all three patterns, RAE_MEM_STATS outstanding delta < 100 over 3000 rounds; added to run_tests.sh's mem-stats list). Docs: docs/drop-semantics-and-defer.md gets the decided assignment rules. VERSION 0.3.1 -> 0.3.2. Verified: watch-tests.sh 494/494 (one round caught 417's explicit-drop contract, fixed in lib), visual gates 106/112/114 PASS. Follow-up filed: #971.
- [x] #968 {difficulty:2} [examples/106] Move the last UI-thread artwork download onto the #950 queue: `spotifySystem/SpotifyView.rae` still calls the blocking `Spotify.fetchArtwork` on the UI thread when the current track changes (`loadHistoryEntryArt` + the primary/iTunes-fallback pair in the now-playing cover path, ~lines 143/234/245) — a beach-ball of one curl round-trip per track change. Route both through `artworkFetchStart` on `appState.artworkFetch` and finish the work (texture load + `spotify-current` sprite swap, history-entry textureKey) when `artworkFetchStatus` turns terminal on a later frame; the iTunes fallback becomes "on failed, start the fallback fetch". Keep the visible behaviour (cover appears within a frame of the download landing; `wake()` already repaints). Deps: #950. Verify with watch-tests.sh (full suite green); VISUAL TESTS OK — a windowed 106 run with Spotify playing is the real check (timeout, one run at a time, never alongside a live watch-tests.sh). DONE: `loadHistoryEntryArt` no longer fetches — it only computes the deterministic `spotify-art:<hash>` key (safe to record immediately; #950's `loadCachedHistoryArtChunk` already scans+downloads+loads any `spotify-art:` entry it finds unloaded, so the history half needed no new async plumbing, just removing the sync block). The now-playing cover got a real two-phase pipeline in SpotifyView.rae: `syncNowPlayingCoverStart` (on track change) picks the target path and calls new `artworkFetchStartWithFallback` (ArtworkFetch.rae: one spawned worker tries the primary URL, then — off the UI thread — the iTunes Search fallback, before posting one result, so neither blocking call ever runs on the UI thread); `syncNowPlayingCoverFinish` runs every frame (independent of the ~1Hz Spotify poll tick, so a landed download is picked up within a frame) and applies the texture once `Sys.exists`/`artworkFetchStatus` turns terminal. `SpotifyState` gained `coverAppliedPath`/`coverPendingPath` to track this across frames. Verified: emit-c clean, format-check clean, watch-tests.sh 494/494, and two live windowed runs against the real Spotify desktop app on fresh (never-cached) tracks — track change recorded into play_history.json immediately with the correct key, the artwork file appeared in app_cache/album_art mid-run, frame count advanced smoothly throughout (no stall/beach-ball), no crashes.
- [x] #969 {difficulty:4} [lib/Channel+runtime] Struct payloads for `Channel(T)`: today only `Channel(Int)` is instantiable (the runtime ring stores int64 and `channelSend`/`receive` pass the value through as Int), which forced #950 to encode an artwork result as `serial * 2 + okBit` instead of the `Channel(ArtworkResult)` the design named. Add boxing for POD/String-owning struct payloads: the ring stores an owned copy of T (deep-copied on send, moved out on receive, dropped with the channel on free), keeping MPSC, the no-blocking-receive rule and the Int fast path; the C backend must specialise the ring element size/copy/drop per T the way List does. Then switch `ArtworkFetch.rae` to `Channel(ArtworkResult { serial, ok })` and add a fixture (spawn'd workers sending a struct with a String field, drained in order, no leak under the runtime's leak check). Deps: #950. Verify with watch-tests.sh (full suite green); VISUAL TESTS OK — scoped `RAE_EXAMPLE_FILTER="106_mobile_ui" make test-examples` (timeout, one run at a time, never alongside a live watch-tests.sh). DONE. Runtime: `RaeChannel` is a byte ring of `cap * elemSize` (`rae_ext_rae_chan_new(elemSize)`), and the C side only hands out slot INDICES under its mutex — `reserve`/`commit` (producer) and `take`/`release` (consumer, zeroes the slot) — while the typed store/load stays in Rae: `channelSend(value: own T)` moves the value into the slot with `rae_ext_rae_buf_set` over the ring exposed as `Buffer(T)` (`rae_ext_rae_chan_ring`), `receive` moves it out with `rae_ext_rae_buf_get` + `ret own val`, `freeChannel` drains and drops what is still buffered before freeing — the same per-T specialisation List uses, so Int is the same code with an 8-byte slot (no separate fast path needed), MPSC and no-blocking-receive unchanged, full ring still refuses the send. Two compiler ownership gaps this surfaced, fixed in codegen: an OWNING local typed as the bare generic `T` inside a monomorphized body (`let leftover: T = receive(this)`) was never dropped (read as the abstract T = no heap) — now substituted to the concrete type for the scope-exit drop (c_stmt.c), and symmetrically a bare-`T` local passed to an `own T` param / stored via buf_set is move-marked as its concrete type (c_call.c; without it `decodeComponent`'s `comp` double-freed once it was dropped). `ArtworkFetch.rae` sends `ArtworkResult { serial, ok }` on `Channel(ArtworkResult)`; SpotifyPoller keeps `Channel(Int)`. Fixture 848_channel_struct_payload: two spawn'd producers send a struct with a String field, per-producer FIFO verified, a channel freed with 50 buffered messages, RAE_MEM_STATS outstanding delta < 20 over 20 rounds (added to run_tests.sh's mem-stats list); ASan-clean. Docs: lib/Channel.rae header, docs/concurrency-model.md §5, parallelism-first-plan status, drop-semantics rule 4, 106 DESIGN.md. VERSION 0.3.2 -> 0.4.0 (MINOR: `channelSend` value param `view T` -> `own T`). Verified: watch-tests.sh 495/495, scoped 106 visual gate PASS, 106 emit-c clean.
- [x] #970 {difficulty:3} [compiler/sema] A method call on an UNDECLARED identifier passes sema and only fails in gcc. Repro: `import Time` then `let seconds: Float64 = now.epochSeconds()` with no `now` in scope — `rae build --emit-c` succeeds and emits `rae_epochSeconds()`; the C compiler reports "call to undeclared function". Found in #950 (a removed `now` parameter left a `now.epochSeconds()` behind and the Rae build still passed). Sema must resolve the receiver of a UFCS/method call like any other identifier and report `unknown identifier 'now'` at the Rae source position; add a compile-error fixture. Check the sibling forms too (`now.field`, `now[0]`, `now as Float`). Deps: none. Verify with watch-tests.sh (full suite green). DONE. Root cause: sema analyzed a UFCS/method-call and `.member` receiver in NON-value position (to let a module/type qualifier through), so a bare-ident receiver that named no value — a since-removed parameter (`now.epochSeconds()`, the #950 case), or a bare function name — left resolved_type NULL, the method-resolution block was skipped, and the C backend emitted a bare `rae_epochSeconds()` that only gcc rejected. Fix (compiler/src/sema.c): (1) in the IDENT value-position handler a resolved FUNCTION symbol is now an error (functions are not first-class values in Rae) — covers the sibling forms `now as Float`, `now[0]`, and interpolation `"{now}"`, which are already analyzed in value position; (2) after analyzing a method-call/`.member` receiver, a new `sema_report_bad_receiver` reports a bare-ident receiver that resolves to no value and to no FUNCTION either, at the receiver's own line:col — with the "names a function" message for a function symbol and the existing "unknown value" message for a truly-undeclared name. It deliberately does NOT flag a module/type/enum namespace, a generic parameter (decl-less symbol), a primitive-type namespace, or a `__`-intrinsic; a new `sema_name_is_module_component` recognizes lib/subfolder/imported/project-folder module qualifiers (case-insensitive, so the PascalCase folder form `Particles.tick()` is accepted) which sema leaves for the backend to resolve. Fixture 849_method_call_undeclared_receiver pins the method-call form (`unknown value 'now'`). VERSION 0.4.0 -> 0.4.1 (new diagnostic). Verified: watch-tests.sh 496/496 green (initial run surfaced 5 module-qualifier regressions — 527/643/677/730/836 — all fixed by the module-component recognizer); all five repro forms now error at the Rae source position.
- [x] #971 {difficulty:3} [examples/114+lib/ui] 114 churns ~140k List buffers per second while idle (RAE_MEM_STATS: 2.8M buf allocs in 20 s, all balanced after #965 — not a leak, but 2.4 GB of malloc/free traffic for an idle frame loop) and its windowed RSS still creeps ~0.1 MB/s decelerating (183 -> 186 MB over 30 s) with Rae-level allocations flat, i.e. outside Rae's allocator. Two parts: (a) find the per-frame scratch Lists (macOS `leaks`/`malloc_history` or RAE_MEM_STATS deltas per system; candidates: gpu2d text batching `emptyTextAtlas` copies per glyph — the `var atlas = emptyTextAtlas(); if let known = copyAt {...}` copy-mutate-set pattern deep-copies the whole atlas (glyph List) per glyph — plus render-graph/uniform scratch) and make them persistent per-frame buffers (`modAt` in place instead of copyAt+set); (b) attribute the residual RSS creep with `vmmap`/`heap` over a 2-minute windowed run (Metal drawable / wgpu pool warm-up vs a real C-level leak) and fix or document. Verify with RAE_MEM_STATS cumulative alloc counts (docs/parallel-tests.md style) + a 60 s RSS sample; watch-tests.sh green. DONE (part a: the identified O(n^2) driver; part b: attributed). MEASURED first with RAE_MEM_STATS on a headless 18 s run: baseline buf alloc=2,847,728 (2.4 GB traffic), outstanding=19 (confirmed not a leak). ROOT of the churn: `canvasDrawGlyphEx` (lib/Gpu2dCanvasText.rae) runs ONCE PER GLYPH and used the copy-mutate-set anti-pattern — `var entry = emptyTextAtlas(); if let known = copyAt(ai) { entry = own known }; entry.glyphs.add(...); set(ai, own entry)` — so every glyph DEEP-COPIED the whole already-accumulated glyph List (O(n^2) per frame, a fresh Buffer per glyph). FIX: append in place through a `mod` window (`if let entry: mod Gpu2dTextAtlas => canvas.textAtlases.modAt(index: ai)`), no per-glyph copy/alloc. Applied the same copyAt+set -> modAt conversion to the other per-frame/per-flush whole-atlas copies that deep-copied the (large) glyph List for a trivial mutation: `clearTextBatches`, `clearTextBatch`, `ensureTextAtlas`, `textFlushBuffer` (all only mutate `resources`, never `canvas`, between get and set, so an in-place window is safe), and `boxFrameEnd`'s flush-cursor reset loop (Gpu2dCanvasFrame.rae). AFTER: buf alloc 2,847,728 -> 1,791,821 (-37%), traffic 2.4 GB -> 1.08 GB (-55%), outstanding still 19. Part (b): 60 s headless RSS sample climbs 183 -> 190 MB and PLATEAUS from ~t=30 s (flat 190 through t=55 s) while Rae buf outstanding stays 19 — a decelerating warm-up that stabilises (wgpu/Metal pool + buffer growth), NOT a C-level leak; matches #965's windowed finding and the drs-memory-leak note. The residual per-frame churn (~1.79M/18s) is now the renderer's per-pass bind-group descriptor Lists (`List(ResourceRef)` rebuilt every draw across ~20 GbufferPasses/ShadowMaps/Gpu2dCanvas* sites) — a broad, separate concern (and arguably a bind-group-caching question), split out as #973 rather than half-done here. Verified: watch-tests.sh 496/496 green; scoped visual gates 114 AND 106 (heaviest text user) PASS; format-check-tree clean.
- [x] #972 {difficulty:5} example 106, mobile ui, when i press a menubar button, the page changes ,but the menubar itself moves up, by the height of the menubar every time. So this way it is possibly to strangely move the menubar to the top of the screen, as it always gains an offset somehow. - Also the home page is quite black, with only the text ”Recently played” at the top. Other pages work better with actual UI elemenets shown. DONE — two separate bugs, both reproduced headless and fixed at the root. REPRO TOOLING: new `RAE_UI_SCREEN_SEQ="library,home,search,home"` (inputSystem/InputGpu2d.rae, documented in Headless.rae) sends the same `nav.<name>` UiAction a menubar tap produces every 20 frames, so a scripted run drives the real screen-switch path and a `RAE_GPU2D_SCREENSHOT` shows the state after N presses — the first run showed both bugs in one frame. BUG 1 (menubar climbs one bar height per press): `unmountPage` -> `destroyEntitySubtree` destroyed the old nav-tabs bar but never unlinked it from the dock's `Children` cache; the dock's child list grew by one per switch (traced kids=2->3->4, mini-player y 2089->1898->1708 = exactly navTabsH), and with entity-id recycling the stale slot even aliased live entities. Fix (lib/ui/Ecs.rae): `destroyEntitySubtree` now `unlinkFromCurrent`s the root from its parent before destroying. BUG 2 (Home page black after any return to it): `findNodeInPage` stores only an entity INDEX and every caller rebuilt the handle with `id(v: raw)` = `{ index, generation: 0 }` — correct only until a slot is recycled, i.e. only for the boot mount. On re-mount (ScreenSwitch re-mounts Home on entry) the scene nodes resolved to stale handles, so the Recently-played strip and CoverGrid tiles were attached to dead entities and never laid out; the header showed because it is authored in the scene. Fix: new `nodeEntity(world, index)` (lib/ui/Ecs.rae) reads the allocator's current generation; all 19 `id(v:)` lookup sites in lib/ui (Frames, FramesHierarchy, SceneLoader, ScrollPanel, ListViewSystem), lib/app3d/UiShell and 106 (HomeView, NowPlayingView, Playback, UiPlaybackSystem, PlaylistData, ScreenSafeArea, SpotifyView) now use it. `id(v:)` itself stays (fixtures use it as a fresh-handle constructor; 17 fixtures failed when it was removed) but its doc now forbids using it to rebuild a stored-index handle. VERSION 0.4.1 -> 0.4.2 (additive lib helper). Verified: scripted 4-switch screenshot — dock pinned at the boot position (kids stays 2) and Home fully populated; watch-tests.sh 496/496; scoped 106 visual gate PASS; format-check-tree clean.
- [x] #973 {difficulty:3} [lib/ui+renderer] Per-frame bind-group scratch Lists. After #971 fixed the gpu2d text O(n^2) glyph churn, 114's remaining idle malloc/free traffic (~1.79M Buffer alloc+free / 18 s headless, all balanced — not a leak) is dominated by the renderer rebuilding a fresh `List(ResourceRef)` per draw call / per pass every frame: ~20 sites (lib/GbufferPasses.rae, GbufferSprite/Skinned/Sdf/Terrain/Underwater/Inspector.rae, ShadowMaps*.rae, GrassCompute.rae, Gpu2dCanvas.rae:799, Gpu2dCanvasImage.rae:505, Gpu2dCanvasText.rae:358 `entries`, plus textFlushAtlas's per-flush `batch` copyAt). Reduce the traffic: either (a) reuse a persistent per-frame scratch `List(ResourceRef)` (clear+refill instead of create) threaded on the resources/canvas, or (b) cache the bind groups themselves by (pipeline, entry-ids) key so the descriptor list is built once, not per frame — decide which. Not a correctness bug (outstanding is flat); this is allocator-traffic/perf. Verify with RAE_MEM_STATS cumulative buf alloc counts on a headless 114 run (target: well under the current 1.79M/18s) + watch-tests.sh green; visual gate 114 + one 3D example. DONE — but the task's stated target (bind-group ResourceRef lists) turned out to be a MINOR contributor. MEASURED with a temporary env-gated return-address histogram in rae_ext_rae_buf_alloc (added, used, removed) on a headless 114 run: baseline 1.876M buf allocs / 18 s. Bind-group `List(ResourceRef)`/`List(WGPUBindGroupEntry)` were only ~18k combined. The real per-frame churn was four sites, all now fixed: (1) `GpuArgs.pushF32` allocated a 1-element `List(Float)` scratch per call to reinterpret a float to bytes (~500k allocs) — replaced with a new `rae_ext_rae_f32_bits` reinterpret intrinsic + `appendWord`, no heap; (2) `GltfSkin.nodeWorldMatrix` allocated a `chain: List(Int)` per joint per frame (~486k) — rewritten to walk parent->root pre-multiplying `local(cur) * acc` (Mat4 is a value Array, so mat4Mul allocates nothing), same product, no list; (3) `layoutSystem.activeChildren` allocated a fresh `List(EntityId)` per node per frame in measureNode + placeChildren (~185k) — added `activeChildrenInto(out:)` filling a REUSED scratch threaded from the flat measure/place drivers (layout is a non-reentrant linear walk, so one buffer per phase is safe); (4) `textFlushWith` did `copyAt` of the WHOLE atlas (4 Lists) for EVERY atlas EVERY frame just to read `glyphClips.length` (~124/frame) — changed to a `view` window. RESULT: 1.876M -> 230,843 buf allocs (-88%), 1.13 GB -> 234 MB traffic, outstanding still 19 (no leak, as before). Remaining small sites (imageFlushWith image batch, textFlushAtlas per-flush `batch` copyAt, the actual bind-group entries ~8k) are per-flush not per-frame and blocked on the canvas mod/view aliasing rule — left as-is (diminishing returns; a persistent bind-group cache is a separate design). VERSION 0.4.2 -> 0.5.0 (lib signatures: measureNode/placeChildren gained a scratch param, activeChildrenInto + f32-bits intrinsic added). Verified: watch-tests.sh 496/496 (one parallel-run flake on 585_hot_reload_channel_paths, unrelated to these files, passed in isolation and on a clean re-run); visual gates 114 (skinned) + 110 (deferred gbuffer) PASS; format-check-tree clean.

- [x] #982 {difficulty:2} [compiler] A .rae file over the 1,000-line cap HANGS the compiler instead of failing: `rae format --check lib/ui/Components.rae` at 1014 lines spun until the alarm (SIGALRM), and `rae build` died with `arena_alloc: out of space (request=32, used=67108864, cap=67108864)` in `parser_alloc` (found in #978). The cap is documented as a refusal ("a canonical form over 1,000 lines is REFUSED, never written" / "an error to split"), so the format preflight or the parser must report `file exceeds 1000 lines: split it` at the file's position and exit non-zero — no retry loop, no arena exhaustion. Add a fixture with a 1,001-line file expecting that diagnostic. Verify with watch-tests.sh. DONE. ROOT CAUSE, confirmed by direct repro: the lexer's own 1000-line hard cap (lexer.c, predates this task) already stops tokenization cleanly and reports `file exceeds the hard cap of 1000 lines` — that part never hung. The bug was downstream in `rae_format_source` (used by BOTH `rae format --check` and the `rae build` preflight, lib/ui/Ecs.rae's `module_graph_load_module`): it called `parse_module` UNCONDITIONALLY on the token stream even when the lexer had already flagged it broken (`tokens.had_error`), instead of short-circuiting the way `main.c`'s `parse`/`lex` commands already did. Parser error-recovery over that leftover/truncated token stream could cascade into a large number of diagnostics and allocations in the function's private 64MB arena — matching the reported `arena_alloc: out of space (... cap=67108864) in parser_alloc` crash; a big-enough cascade (confirmed with a synthetic 1001-line file of unfixed-type `let`s: 999 cascaded errors) is the same class of bug that likely hung under different content. FIX (compiler/src/rae_format.c): (1) check `input_lines > 1000` FIRST, before creating any arena or touching the lexer/parser at all — an O(n) line count that cannot hang, refusing with `<path> has <N> lines: file exceeds 1000 lines, split it into smaller modules`, reusing the existing `over_cap` field/reporting path (same one the post-format output-cap check already used); (2) added a `tokens.had_error` short-circuit before `parse_module` as defense in depth for any OTHER lexer-flagged breakage, not just the line cap. Verified via direct repro: reconstructing the actual over-cap Components.rae+BindingComponents.rae content (1093 lines) through `format --check` AND through `rae build --emit-c --project examples/106_mobile_ui` both now fail instantly and cleanly instead of hanging/crashing. Fixture: NEW `compiler/tests/cases/850_format_check_over_cap` (1001-line valid-syntax file, `format --check`, expects the new diagnostic + exit 1) — the pre-existing `390_long_file_error` already covered the default `parse` command's message but nothing exercised the format/preflight path specifically. Also fixed `format-check-tree.sh`: its over-cap check fired unconditionally (even under tests/cases/), which would have failed the tree check on 390 and the new 850 fixture now that they report `overCap` instead of a generic parse error — folded over-cap into the same tests/cases/-tolerant branch as parse errors (a REAL over-cap file under lib/ or examples/ still fails the gate; verified by transiently growing lib/ui/Components.rae past 1000 lines and confirming format-check-tree.sh catches it). watch-tests.sh 497/497 (496 + the new fixture), format-check-tree PASS (954 files canonical).
- [x] #983 {difficulty:2} [lib/Gpu2d+106] Headless runs must not take keyboard focus: since #976 the SDL3 window calls SDL_StartTextInput and `SDL_RaiseWindow` runs even under `RAE_UI_HEADLESS=1`, so keystrokes typed into the terminal during a headless run land in the app (in #978 a stray Space toggled playback — persisted to assets/state.json — and a stray 'to' filled the search field, polluting screenshot comparisons). Under RAE_UI_HEADLESS (and RAE_SDL_HEADLESS_MS) create the window hidden / without focus (SDL_WINDOW_HIDDEN or no SDL_RaiseWindow, and skip SDL_StartTextInput), keep the scripted `RAE_UI_SCREEN_SEQ` / `RAE_UI_TYPE` inputs working since they bypass SDL, and verify a headless screenshot run ignores typing. Verify with the 106 visual gate + a headless run. DONE. Fixed in `rae_ext_Gpu2d_initWindow` (compiler/runtime/runtime_gpu2d_platform.c, shared by every gpu2d app, not just 106): a new `rae_g2d_headless_requested()` checks all three headless-budget envs (RAE_UI_HEADLESS, RAE_SDL_HEADLESS_MS, RAE_HEADLESS_FRAMES -- matching the BOTH-headless-vars convention already used by 111/112, since checking only RAE_UI_HEADLESS would still leave an MS/frame-budget-only run stealing focus); when any is set, the window is created with SDL_WINDOW_HIDDEN and SDL_RaiseWindow/SDL_StartTextInput are skipped entirely. Screenshot capture is unaffected by design: rae_g2d_present reads the offscreen render target for RAE_GPU2D_SCREENSHOT before the (already best-effort, visibility-gated) present-to-drawable step, the same path that already skips presenting for an occluded/backgrounded window. RAE_UI_SCREEN_SEQ/RAE_UI_TYPE dispatch synthetic UiActions/typed text directly in Rae and never touch SDL events, so they are unaffected either way. VERIFIED: (1) a runtime trace confirmed SDL_WINDOW_HIDDEN is set under RAE_UI_HEADLESS=1 alone AND under RAE_SDL_HEADLESS_MS alone (no RAE_UI_HEADLESS) -- both trip the fix; (2) osascript/System Events polled the frontmost application four times across a live headless run and it never changed to the app (stayed the terminal's own frontmost app) -- the window never took OS focus, closing off the whole class of stray-keystroke bugs, not just the two reported symptoms; (3) headless screenshot pixel-compared against a git-stashed pre-fix build: only the HUD fps/mem/timestamp rows (0-132, pre-existing non-deterministic noise) differ, everything below is byte-identical; (4) scripted RAE_UI_SCREEN_SEQ/RAE_UI_TYPE (nav.search + typing brat) still logs and dispatches correctly; (5) scoped RAE_EXAMPLE_FILTER=106_mobile_ui make test-examples PASS; (6) full watch-tests.sh 497/497 (this is a shared-runtime change, so ran the whole suite, not just 106's slice). One side-effect noted, not a regression: a hidden headless window now logs the pre-existing throttled 'nothing presented ... surface status=window-not-visible' warning (previously silent because the window stayed visible/raised) -- expected per that code's own comment, and harmless (screenshot capture happens before that gate).
- [x] #984 {difficulty:2} [compiler/tools] The Tetris visual gate fails at HEAD: `RAE_EXAMPLE_FILTER=94_tetris2d make -C compiler test-examples` builds the example with `rae build --emit-c` + gcc into a mktemp dir and then execs `$TMP_OUT/app` directly from `compiler/` (tools/run_examples.sh, the 94_tetris2d branch — the same shape as the 91/95/96/97 TEST_FRAME gates), so the binary runs with cwd=compiler/, no `$RAE_STDLIB`, and fails every asset read: `could not read stdlib asset 'examples/94_tetris2d/assets/ui.raescene'` / `'examples/104_ui_hello/assets/theme.raescene'` / `'lib/gpu2d_box.wgsl' … $RAE_STDLIB is unset. Run through rae run, or set RAE_STDLIB` — the 2D boxes/images are skipped and the screenshot is blank, so the render gate FAILs (confirmed failing on a clean checkout before the build-timing commit, i.e. not caused by it; 106 passes because its gate goes through `rae run`, which sets RAE_STDLIB and cwd for the child). Fix the gate, not the app: run the built binary the way `rae run` runs it — cwd = repo root and `RAE_STDLIB=<repo>/lib` exported (compiler_stdlib_dir() in main.c is the source of truth) — for EVERY hand-built gate in run_examples.sh, then run the whole visual gate (`make -C compiler test-examples`, bounded) and list which examples were silently failing the same way. Verify: 94_tetris2d PASS, full gate green. DONE. Root cause exactly as diagnosed: every hand-built gate in run_examples.sh execs the standalone "$TMP_OUT/app" binary (not bin/rae) without setting its cwd or $RAE_STDLIB, so any stdlib asset read (a .raescene, lib/gpu2d_*.wgsl) fails. Fixed ALL 8 affected gates, not just Tetris -- baselined first (RAE_EXAMPLE_FILTER scoped to the suspects) and confirmed 91_pong_implicit, 94_tetris2d, 95_easing_2d, 96_easing_3d, 97_tetris3d, 109_gpu3d_pbr, 110_deferred_gbuffer all failed with the stdlib error before the fix (120_resource_lifetimes happened to pass -- it reads no assets); 111/112/114/115/117/118/119 already wrapped their app-exec in `(cd .. && ...)` and were untouched. Fix: wrapped every remaining app-exec in `(cd .. && ...)` (matching the existing pattern) and added `export RAE_STDLIB="$(cd .. && pwd)/lib"` once at the top of the script, mirroring exactly what `rae run` gives its own child (main.c). Verified: rerunning the same scoped filter after the fix, all 8 gates are stdlib-error-free; 94_tetris2d PASS confirmed twice, including a final isolated run. The full `make -C compiler test-examples` is NOT green right now, but for reasons OUTSIDE this task's bug: every heavier GPU3D/deferred example (96, 97, 109, 110, and the untouched 111/112/114/115/117/118/119) intermittently misses its fixed 1000-1500ms `RAE_SDL_HEADLESS_MS` first-frame budget under the machine's current load (uptime showed load averages 3.3-5.2 from an unrelated long-running process on this shared box) -- confirmed NOT caused by this change since it hits gates #984 never touched, identically, and persists even with the budget quadrupled as a diagnostic. Filed as #986 (the timing-budget flake) and #987 (an unrelated pre-existing zz_gpu_args_check type-error compile failure also surfaced by the full run). Only compiler/tools/run_examples.sh changed.

Found while verifying #984 (confirmed NOT caused by that task: identical failures occur on gates #984 never touched, under `uptime` load averages of 3.3-5.2 from an unrelated long-running process). Fix: either make the budget adaptive (poll for first-frame-rendered instead of a fixed sleep, with a generous upper bound) or raise the fixed budgets enough to absorb normal CI/shared-machine contention, whichever fits the existing RAE_SDL_HEADLESS_MS mechanism (compiler/runtime, main.c) best.
